# Codebase Patterns

Durable, never-truncated home for reusable project conventions (the old
top-of-progress.txt block, CAM-31). This file is versioned on main and read by
agents to absorb project conventions without re-reading the whole codebase. Add
a bullet whenever a story reveals a reusable insight (a convention, a library
quirk, a gotcha). Unlike the per-story factual record (the event log) and the
per-story handoff (handoff.json), this knowledge persists.

- **Bun runtime**: always `Bun.spawn` / `Bun.$` / `Bun.file` over `node:child_process` / `node:fs`.

- **Permission mode**: never register a `--permission-mode` CLI flag on any subcommand (enforced by `test/no-permission-mode-flag.test.ts`). It is forwarded only to the spawned `claude` process.

- **claude -p is forbidden** (subscription rule, CAM-42): workers are interactive TUI `claude` sessions detected by polling `capture-pane` for the sentinel; never reintroduce `-p` in a worker argv (tests assert its absence). `claude -p` survives only in the separate `cam claude` retry wrapper.

- **noUncheckedIndexedAccess**: array indexing and regex capture groups are `T | undefined`. Guard with `?? fallback` or a justified non-null assertion.

- **Ink screens**: success/failure is signalled by the glyph (check accent / cross destructive), never by divider color. Render and look at the real output; do not trust header comments (see `lessons.archive.md` 2026-06-05).

- **Tests**: `bun test` from repo root; test files live under `test/`, mirroring source. Inject fake reader/writer shapes instead of touching real stdin/stdout.

- **Commits**: conventional commits required (`feat:`, `fix:`, `chore:`, `refactor:`, `docs:`).

- **No em-dash in persisted .md** (project rule, 2026-05-20): use colon, comma, parens, period, or cut the connector.

- **File-per-issue hygiene** (CAM-90, 2026-06-28; supersedes the CAM-47 array-backlog note): `cam issue` / `/cam-issue create` allocate the next id as `max(parsed ids in scripts/cam/issues/) + 1` (no counter field exists). Allocation and the file write are done atomically via CAS `update-ref` on main; concurrent writers retry automatically. Do not hand-edit `scripts/cam/issues/` files in the working tree while a `cam run` session is active: the sidecar is a concurrent writer via `cam issue --file-local`. The old monolithic array backlog and its counter field were removed in CAM-90 US-005.

- **Pure render/merge modules for deterministic-doc writers** (US-001, CAM-118, 2026-07-06): when a story needs to produce hand-authored-looking markdown (glossary, ADR) without LLM judgment (CAM-106 hazard), put the parse/render/merge logic in a zero-dependency module with no git/fs/network calls (`src/domain-docs/render.ts` is the template): string-in/string-out only. This lets a later story (the writer, e.g. US-002) inject a real filesystem/git-tree layer around it without re-testing the rendering logic; the pure module's tests never need fixtures on disk. Idempotent merge functions (merging the same payload twice yields byte-identical output) are proven by parsing your own rendered output back and re-merging, not by hand-crafting a second "already merged" fixture that can drift from the renderer.

- **Test timeout with no-op sleepFn**: when injecting `sleepFn: () => {}` in tests for `waitForOrchestrator`, always pair it with a tiny `waitTimeoutMs` (5-10ms). The `while (Date.now() < deadline)` loop spins the CPU until the deadline expires; with a 5_000ms budget and a no-op sleep the test actually burns 5 real seconds and Bun's 5s per-test timeout kills it. Correct: `sleepFn: () => {}, waitTimeoutMs: 5`. (US-006, 2026-06-15.)

- **Single-hub dispatch invariant (CAM-55)**: `cam run` is the only dispatch hub. CLI subcommands (`cam plan`, `cam issue`, `cam next`, `cam ship`) are thin-proxies: detect the orchestrator session and inject the request via atomic `send-keys` (text + Enter in one call); if no session exists, bootstrap `cam run --no-attach` first and wait for the `.claude/.cam-orch-ready` marker before injecting. Workers run in a single reused titled 3rd pane (mutex: dispatch only when session has exactly 2 panes). Completion is push-report (worker writes a result file at exit), not pull-based scrollback polling. The idle-guarantee (ensuring the orchestrator is idle before the worker pushes its report) is its own story (US-008), not folded into the proxy.

- **@cam_label pane-labeling pattern (US-002, 2026-06-15)**: Label any pane before dispatch with `tmux set-option -p -t <paneId> @cam_label <value>`. The session-level `pane-border-format #{@cam_label}` (set in run.ts `setupPanes`) picks this up automatically for any pane added to the session later, including the worker pane. `set-option -p` is validated by tmux docs: pane-scoped `@`-prefixed user options are supported. In the supervisor (loop.ts), the label is set immediately before `respawn-pane -k`; in the review branch, before `reviewDispatch`. The label is best-effort (silent no-op if the pane is not yet open).

- **Mutex busy check in dispatch sites (US-009, 2026-06-15)**: Every thin-proxy dispatch site (plan, next, issue, review, ship) must call `paneCountMutex(sessionName, tmuxSpawnFn)` after the alive/bootstrap path and before `sendKeysWhenIdle`. If the result is `'busy'`, the command calls `printError('worker busy', 'blocked until the worker-pane closes')` and returns exit code 1 without sending any keys. Test helpers that fake `tmuxSpawnFn` must handle ALL `list-panes` format strings: `#{@cam_label}` (orchestratorAlive), `#{pane_index};#{pane_id}` (getOrchPaneId), `#{pane_current_command};#{@cam_label}` (isSessionStale), and `#{pane_id}` (paneCountMutex). If `#{pane_id}` is missing from the fake, `paneCountMutex` gets empty output, counts 0 panes, and returns `'busy'`, breaking the happy-path test. Use `paneMutexBusy` option in `makeFakeTmuxSpawn` to return 3 pane IDs (busy) vs 2 (available).

- **Idle-guarantee before send-keys (US-008, 2026-06-15)**: Thin-proxy commands that inject slash commands (plan, issue, review, ship) must call `sendKeysWhenIdle` (src/tmux/dispatch.ts) instead of bare `tmuxSpawnFn send-keys`. Exception: `cam next` does NOT call `sendKeysWhenIdle` or any `send-keys` at all (it is a pure `active:true` flip, not a slash-command injector; see "cam next is a pure sidecar trigger" bullet). `sendKeysWhenIdle` polls `capture-pane -p -t <paneId>` until `isOrchPaneIdle` returns true (no spinner glyph ⠋-⠏, no active tool-call glyph ◆ in the last 5 lines, and at least one line ending with `>` or `❯`). Fallback on timeout: log to stderr + still send (never block indefinitely). Default `idleTimeoutMs` is 5 000 ms; tests must inject `capturePaneFn: () => '> '` so the idle check passes immediately without a real tmux call. All test fake spawn fns (makeFakeTmuxSpawn) must handle `capture-pane` subcommand by returning idle content, or the idle-loop will spin for 5 s and hit Bun's per-test timeout.

- **Sidecar-supervisor invariant (US-FIX-001, 2026-06-15)**: the deterministic loop is a SIDECAR, not in-process and not absorbed by the LLM. Roles: `cam run` = sidecar host (spawns `runSupervisor` as a detached background process); the sidecar = the deterministic loop driver (gated on the `active` flag in `.claude/cam-loop.local.md`; holds the supervisor lock; carries the CAM-36 no-progress streak, CAM-44 dead-worker backoff, MAX_ITERATIONS, event log); the LLM orchestrator = human interface (narrates the sidecar's terminal report and routes plan/review/ship/issue), NOT the loop driver; `cam next` = trigger (a thin-proxy that flips `active:true`, never calls `runSupervisor` in-process); `scripts/cam/worker-report.json` = push completion signal (the worker writes it on exit; the sidecar reads it, no scrollback poll). The "orchestrator absorbs the loop" model is REJECTED: an LLM-driven loop loses every deterministic guard above.

- **Report-file completion detection (US-004, 2026-06-15)**: The supervisor poll loop uses an optional injected `ReadWorkerReport` dependency to detect worker completion instead of polling `capturePane + parseAnySentinel`. When `readWorkerReport` is injected and returns a non-null `WorkerReport`, the loop exits with `pollOutcome='sentinel'`. The state-primary outcome is still confirmed via `readWorkerOutcome` (prd.json + handoff.json). The fallback to `capturePane + parseAnySentinel` is preserved for backward compat when `readWorkerReport` is absent. `clearWorkerReport` should be called before `respawn-pane -k` to erase any stale report from the previous run (prevents false-positive on first poll tick). In next.ts, `readWorkerReport` reads `scripts/cam/worker-report.json` via `readFileSync`; `clearWorkerReport` uses `existsSync` + `unlinkSync` wrapped in try-catch (node:fs docs: `unlinkSync` throws ENOENT on missing file, `existsSync` returns false gracefully).

- **Sidecar dep-wiring in host.ts (US-FIX-002, 2026-06-15)**: The supervisor production wiring (spawn, capturePane, readPrd, writePrd, readHandoff, clock, reviewDispatch, writeSessionMarker, runGates, finalizeStory, ensurePushed, readWorkerTokens, readWorkerReport, clearWorkerReport, onProgress) lives in `src/supervisor/host.ts:buildSupervisorOptions(cwd)`. Never duplicate it inline; always call `buildSupervisorOptions`. The sidecar outer loop (gated on the `active` flag) lives in `src/supervisor/loop.ts:runSidecarLoop(opts)`. The production caller is `src/commands/sidecar.ts:runSidecar()`, registered as `cam sidecar` in index.ts (internal, not in help). `cam run` spawns it via `Bun.spawn(['cam','sidecar'], { stdio:['ignore', logFd, logFd] })` with the log fd pointing to `.claude/cam-supervisor.log`; the process is killed in the SIGINT/SIGTERM cleanup handler. `cam next` flips `active:true` by overwriting the state file (force:true) before its send-keys call. The lock is always released in a `finally` block so it is freed even when `runSupervisorFn` throws.

- **Agent-prompt self-hosting pattern (US-001, 2026-06-16)**: `templates/agents/` and `templates/commands/` are the SOURCE OF TRUTH for distributed agent prompts; `.claude/agents/` and `.claude/commands/` are the runtime mirrors materialized at `cam init` time. Any story that edits agent/command prompts MUST edit both the `templates/` copy and the `.claude/` copy, then run `bun run embed-vendor` to sync `src/vendor/_generated.ts`. Call this out in the commit message as "intentional self-hosting" so a later auditor does not flag it as harness drift. The `templatesContents` byte-parity test in `test/embedded.test.ts` will catch drift between `templates/` and the embedded copy.

- **ensureWorkerPane self-heal (CAM-57, 2026-06-16)**: A 2nd autonomous loop in a long-lived cam session can wedge because the worker pane was closed after the 1st loop (remain-on-exit off). The sidecar reads the marker once at boot and only does `respawn-pane -k` (no recreate): respawning a dead/missing pane is a no-op, so the worker never starts and `isPaneAlive` returns `false` forever. Fix: inject an `ensureWorkerPane` capability into `RunSupervisorOptions` (and `MakeReviewDispatchOptions`). Before every `respawn-pane -k` dispatch, call it: re-read the marker fresh (never cache the pane id from boot), call `isPaneAlive(currentId)`, and if dead call `openPaneInSession` to create a new pane, write the new marker, and return the new id. Backward compat: when the option is absent (older callers/tests), the static `workerPaneId` from opts is used unchanged. This is proved by a real-tmux integration test (`test/integration/ensure-worker-pane-selfheal.test.ts`) that kills a pane and asserts the self-heal creates a live one and rewrites the marker.

- **worker-report.json is the authoritative outcome source (US-001, 2026-06-25)**: `readWorkerOutcome` (src/supervisor/result.ts) inverted its priority: (1) `worker-report.json` (PRIMARY, when `workerReportPath` is provided and file is valid/non-stale) - `report.story` drives which story completed, `report.outcome` drives DONE/BLOCKED/PRD_COMPLETE. (2) Integrity confirmation: `prd.json passes:true` (read-only, no writes). (3) Fallback (no valid report): sentinel BLOCKED/PRD_COMPLETE detection, then `handoff.json` for backward compat (old loop tests that do not provide `workerReportPath`). `handoff.json` and the sentinel are no longer outcome gates: a stale/absent handoff does not change the named story when a report is present; a DONE sentinel disagreeing with the report does not produce a fail-on-mismatch. The DONE-sentinel-vs-handoff mismatch->fail block was dropped. Detail strings still contain 'worker-report-fallback' (legacy label, preserved for `loop.ts` outcome-fallback detection). `handoff-string-coerced` no longer fires when a report is present (report takes priority).

- **tmux mangles TAB in -F output; key panes on @cam_label, not command (CAM-55 operator smoke, 2026-06-15)**: two real-tmux behaviors broke the whole introspection layer and were invisible to unit fakes (which encoded idealized output, so they matched the buggy code). (1) tmux converts a literal TAB (0x09) in a `-F` format string to `_` (0x5f) in its output: confirmed with `tmux display-message -p $'A\tB'` emitting `A_B`. NEVER use a TAB as a field separator in a tmux `-F` format; use `;` (preserved by tmux; command names, `@cam_label`s and pane ids never contain one). This had silently nulled `getOrchPaneId` (`#{pane_index}\t#{pane_id}`) and broken `isSessionStale` (`#{pane_current_command}\t#{@cam_label}`), so every thin-proxy failed with "Could not find orchestrator pane" and the C3 staleness check never matched. (2) The orchestrator pane runs claude UNDER a bash respawn-wrapper (CAM-23), so its `pane_current_command` is `bash`, never `claude`: `orchestratorAlive` must key on `@cam_label === 'orchestrator'`, never on the command (same lesson as isSessionStale). LESSON: a unit fake that returns the output the code EXPECTS cannot catch tool-real-behavior bugs. The tmux-introspection helpers are now guarded by a real-tmux integration test (`test/integration/tmux-introspect.test.ts`, skips when tmux is absent) that runs the helpers against an actual tmux session with real `@cam_label` panes. When adding/altering any `tmux -F` parse, add a case there.

- **Sidecar PID persistence pattern (US-001, CAM-67, 2026-06-17)**: `cam run` persists the sidecar process pid to `.claude/.cam-sidecar.pid` immediately after `Bun.spawn` returns (via `writeSidecarPid` in `src/supervisor/sidecar-pid.ts`). `cam stop` reads it via `readSidecarPid`, probes liveness with signal-0 (`process.kill(pid, 0)` in `sidecarPidAlive`), SIGTERMs only when alive, then removes the file (idempotent). The constant name is `SIDECAR_PID_FILE`. Injectable fakes (`sidecarPidReader`, `sidecarPidAliveFn`, `sidecarPidRemover`) keep the unit test free of real OS pids. Signal-0 throws `ESRCH` on a dead pid; `EPERM` means the process is alive but unpermissioned (treat as alive, conservative). `Bun.Subprocess.pid` is `readonly pid: number` (confirmed against Bun 1.3.x docs).

- **Per-session marker set + cam stop cleanup (US-002, CAM-67, 2026-06-17)**: `cam stop` removes all six per-session marker files idempotently (existsSync + unlinkSync in try/catch; absent markers are a no-op). The six markers are: `.claude/.cam-supervisor.lock` (SUPERVISOR_LOCK_FILE from `lock.ts`), `.claude/.cam-orch-session` (ORCH_SESSION_MARKER from `session.ts`), `.claude/.cam-worker-pane` (WORKER_PANE_MARKER from `session.ts`), `.claude/.cam-orch-ready` (ORCH_READY_MARKER from `bootstrap-wait.ts`), `scripts/cam/worker-report.json` (WORKER_REPORT_FILENAME from `worker-report.ts`), and `.claude/cam-loop.local.md` (STATE_FILE_PATH, local constant). Diagnostic-value files (cam-worker-events.jsonl, cam-supervisor.log, .cam-worker-*.session) are intentionally NOT removed. Rule: every new marker file introduced in the lifecycle MUST be (a) backed by a named export constant in its canonical module, and (b) added to the marker-removal loop in `performStop`. Never use bare string literals for marker filenames in stop.ts; the negative-grep oracle enforces this. `ORCH_SESSION_MARKER` was promoted from a bare string literal in run.ts to a named export on `src/tmux/session.ts` next to `WORKER_PANE_MARKER`; both the writer (run.ts) and the remover (stop.ts) import it from there.

- **Sidecar self-exit via startup-grace latch (US-003, CAM-67, 2026-06-17)**: `runSidecarLoop` accepts an optional `hasSessionFn: () => boolean` dep. On each idle tick, the loop calls it and tracks a `sessionSeen` one-shot latch: the first PRESENT result sets `sessionSeen = true`; a subsequent ABSENT result returns cleanly only if `sessionSeen` is already set. This prevents premature exit during the spawn-vs-session-creation race at startup (the session may not yet exist when the sidecar boots). The lock is NOT held during idle ticks, so the exit path needs no lock.release(). Production wiring in `sidecar.ts` builds a closure over `hasSession(projectSessionName(cwd), spawnFn)` using a `spawnSync`-backed `SpawnFn`. Tests inject a `hasSessionFn` array-sequence fake (no real tmux). The `sessionSeen` latch and "startup grace" text must appear in loop.ts to satisfy the file-assert oracles.

- **Scoped fallback kill for orphaned sidecars (US-006, CAM-67, 2026-06-17)**: `performStop` includes a two-phase sidecar-kill. Phase 1: read `.cam-sidecar.pid`, probe liveness (signal-0), SIGTERM if alive. Phase 2 (fallback, only when phase 1 found nothing alive): call `listProcessesFn()` which returns `ProcessRecord[]` with `{ pid, argv, cwd }`, filter via `matchesSidecarForProject(record, cwd)` (strict `record.cwd === projectCwd` equality, argv[0]==='cam', argv[1]==='sidecar'), SIGTERM matches. Injectable dep: `listProcessesFn?: ListProcessesFn` in `StopOptions`; production default uses `ps -eo pid,args` + `lsof -a -d cwd -p <pid> -F n`. Never use blanket `pkill cam sidecar` (kills foreign-project sidecars). Scoping primitive is strict equality, not prefix/substring. The `fallbackSidecarKilled: boolean` field in `StopReport` tracks whether the fallback found anything. Test file: `test/stop-sidecar-fallback.test.ts`.

- **Zero-dep vendor scripts: hand-rolled YAML parser (US-001, CAM-69, 2026-06-23)**: vendored scripts run in a tmpdir via `bun <script>` with no node_modules present. Bun auto-install kicks in for any bare-specifier import (e.g. `import yaml from 'js-yaml'`); if the package resolves to the wrong major (js-yaml@5 is ESM-only, no default export) the script crashes with `Missing 'default' export`. Fix: replace external deps with a hand-rolled parser. Scope for agent frontmatter: top-level `key: scalar` pairs (unquoted/quoted string, int, float, bool, null) plus block-sequence lists (`  - item` lines under a key with empty value). The parser returns a 0-indexed line within the frontmatter text on error; add +2 to convert to a 1-indexed file line (matching the old js-yaml `mark.line + 2` semantics). `$` in grep patterns is regex end-of-line; escape as `\$` when checking for literal `$` in template literals. After any vendor edit: `bun run embed-vendor` then `bun run embed-vendor:check`.

- **Injectable onResults callback for structured CLI output (US-004, CAM-59, 2026-06-23)**: when a CLI script needs both human-readable output (stdout lines) and machine-readable output (JSON), use an `onResults?: (results: T[]) => void` injectable on the options object. The caller supplies a callback (e.g. write to a file, write to stdout, capture in test). Tests inject a capture function to assert shape without touching the filesystem. The CLI entrypoint supplies a file-writer when the `--json` flag is present. This keeps the runner function pure and testable. For the CI summary pattern: write to a well-known filename (e.g. `gate-results.json`) in cwd; the follow-up `if: always()` step reads it with `jq` and appends a markdown table to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`. Add the output file to `.gitignore` so it is not accidentally committed.

- **js-yaml named import (US-003, CAM-69, 2026-06-23)**: always use `import { load } from 'js-yaml'` (named import), never `import yaml from 'js-yaml'` (default import). js-yaml v4.1.1 has no default export (`typeof y.default === 'undefined'`); a default import only works because Bun/CJS interop injects one at runtime. js-yaml v5 is ESM-only with no default export at all, so the default import crashes with "Missing default export". Named `load` exists in both v4 and v5, making it the forward-compatible form.

- **Gate-spine convention (US-005, CAM-59, 2026-06-23)**: all quality gates are declared as entries in the `GATES` manifest in `scripts/check-all.ts`. New gates are added there (never invoked ad-hoc in CI). The CI workflow calls only `bun run check:all` (the spine); `ci-parity` (`scripts/check-ci-parity.ts`) enforces that `ci.yml` and the GATES manifest stay in sync. If you need a new gate: (1) add it to `GATES` in `check-all.ts`; (2) do NOT add a separate step in `ci.yml` for it (the spine picks it up automatically); (3) run `bun run check:ci-parity` locally to confirm parity. See `docs/recovery-runbook.md` CAM-59 section for debugging parity failures.

- **Real-git integration test pattern (US-004, CAM-72, 2026-06-23)**: unit fakes for spawnFn (see test/ship-finalize.test.ts) cannot catch git-behavior bugs (the fake records the call and returns success regardless of whether real git would refuse). Add a real-git integration test in test/integration/ for any function that calls git rm / git commit on user-modified files. Pattern: `mkdtempSync` a fresh repo in tmpdir, `git init`, `git config user.email/user.name` (required for commit), write+stage+commit the baseline files, then overwrite to simulate the dirty state, run the function with a `realSpawnFn` wrapper over `spawnSync`, and assert with real `git ls-files` / `git status --porcelain` / `git log -1 --format=%s`. Register dirs in an `afterEach` cleanup array (`rmSync` with `{ recursive: true, force: true }`). Skip when git is absent via `spawnSync('git', ['--version'], { stdio: 'pipe' }).status === 0` + `test.skipIf(!gitAvailable)`.

- **finalizeCycleClose hardening pattern (US-005, CAM-72, 2026-06-23)**: three idempotency/safety guards must be present: (a) wrap `readPrd()` in try-catch; ENOENT means cycle already closed, return `{ noOp: true }` with a `printHint` instead of crashing; (b) when `issue_system == 'none'` and the issue file is absent from `scripts/cam/issues/`, call `printError` and throw (hard failure: rm+commit must NOT silently proceed with a corrupt issue log); (c) after all `git rm` + `git add` calls, run `git diff --cached --quiet`; exit 0 means nothing staged, skip commit (prevents "nothing to commit" git error). In recording-spawn fakes, default `diffCachedStatus` to 1 (staged = commit proceeds); override to 0 in tests that assert noOp. AC4 (second invocation = no-op) is a corollary of guard (a): the first run removes prd.json via `git rm`, so the second run finds it absent.

- **Pane worker vs Task subagent (when to use which, architecture note 2026-06-16)**: the cam agents split by WHO DRIVES them, not by being "agents". TWO run as interactive `claude` sessions in a tmux pane: the **implementer** and the **reviewer** (the reviewer reuses the same titled worker pane, re-labeled, via `review.ts`). Pane is correct for both because they are UNATTENDED LOOP WORKERS dispatched by the deterministic sidecar (`respawn-pane -k` into the single titled worker pane): (1) the sidecar (TS code), not an LLM, owns scheduling, retry, and limits (MAX_ITERATIONS, no-progress streak CAM-36, dead-worker backoff CAM-44, supervisor lock); (2) crash isolation plus independent kill/respawn; (3) the operator can watch and attach the pane live; (4) it is a full dev session that commits, pushes, and runs gates; (5) the subscription rule (CAM-42) forces interactive TUI, not headless `-p`. TWO run as Task subagents (`Task(subagent_type=...)`) inside the interactive `/cam-plan` flow: the **planner** (Step 7) and the **auditor** (Step 8). Task is correct for them because they are PLAN-TIME HELPERS orchestrated by the LLM during an operator-approved conversation (scope approval at Step 6, the bounded audit loop at Step 8), are ephemeral, and their output is consumed immediately by the orchestrator. They need none of the pane benefits, and a pane would only inherit the tmux-lifecycle bug class (CAM-40/43/57) for no gain. The **orchestrator** is pane 0 itself (the root persona of `cam run`, never Task-invoked). DIVIDING LINE: a deterministic-loop unattended worker is a pane; an LLM-orchestrated interactive helper is a Task subagent. Do not convert planner/auditor to panes, and do not convert implementer/reviewer to Task subagents (the latter would force an LLM-driven loop and lose every deterministic guard, the model CAM-55 rejected).

- **Oracle-friendly manifest design (US-001, CAM-59, 2026-06-23)**: when a gate-manifest array is checked by grep-based oracles (e.g. `grep -q 'tsc --noEmit' scripts/check-all.ts`), define each entry as a full command string (e.g. `'bunx tsc --noEmit'`) and split at runtime via a small helper (`g(name, cmdStr)` in check-all.ts splits on space). This makes the oracle grep match the literal command while keeping the runtime representation as `{ cmd, args[] }`. The split-at-runtime approach is only for commands that are clean space-separated tokens; do NOT use it for commands with quoted args or embedded spaces.

- **bun test --coverage output format (US-004, CAM-60, 2026-06-23)**: the text reporter emits columns `% Funcs | % Lines` (in that order; no statements/branches columns). The aggregate row header is `All files`. Parse with regex `^All files\s*\|\s*([\d.]+)\s*\|\s*([\d.]+)`. Coverage output goes to stderr (not stdout), so capture both streams in the production adapter. When the coverage gate runs inside `bun run check:all`, bun test executes twice (once for the `test` gate, once for `coverage`). This is expected and by design: the coverage gate is the only consumer of `--coverage` output.

- **Debt-marker gate self-reference gotcha (US-003, CAM-60, 2026-06-23)**: the gate script itself must not contain a bare `TODO/FIXME/HACK/XXX` token followed by space or colon, or it trips its own gate. When documenting the list of target markers in a comment like `// Scans for TODO/FIXME/HACK/XXX markers`, the `XXX` at the end is followed by a space and matches the regex. Fix: wrap in parens so `XXX` is followed by `)` which is not `\s|:`, e.g. `// Scans for (TODO/FIXME/HACK/XXX) markers`. The regex is `(?<!-)\b(TODO|FIXME|HACK|XXX)\b(?=[:\s])`. Negative lookbehind `(?<!-)` correctly excludes `US-XXX` and `CAM-XXX` identifier forms (hyphen precedes `XXX`). EXCLUDED_DIRS are pinned in the script: `vendor/`, `node_modules/`, `claude-code-harness/`, `dist/`.

- **biome 2.x lint-only config pattern (US-001, CAM-60, 2026-06-23)**: `linter.rules.recommended: false` is deprecated in biome 2.x (use `preset: "none"` instead) but still accepted and only emits an `i` info diagnostic (NOT a warning/error), so `--error-on-warnings` does NOT fail on it. Keep `recommended: false` when oracles check `.linter.rules.recommended == false`. The per-file grandfather pattern uses `overrides[].includes: ["concrete/path.ts"]` with `linter.rules.complexity.<ruleName>: "off"`. Catch-all globs (`**`, `**/*.ts`, etc.) must NOT appear in includes. A stale `biome-ignore` suppression for a rule that is NOT enabled becomes a `suppressions/unused` WARNING (affected by `--error-on-warnings`): remove the comment or enable the rule. Run `bunx biome lint --max-diagnostics 200 2>&1` to see the full violator list when max-diagnostics is hit.

- **Biome cognitive complexity: use factory/helper extraction not grandfather (US-002, CAM-60, 2026-06-23)**: when a function's cognitive complexity exceeds 15, prefer extracting default adapters as `makeDefault*` top-level factory functions AND extracting inner loop/if bodies into named helper functions (e.g. `checkSizeCeilings`, `checkDiffTrackerRef`). This brings the main orchestrator function well under the limit. Prefer refactoring over adding a new per-file `noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity: "off"` override, since the override masks future growth. The `??` null-coalescing operator counts as +1 complexity each in biome; four `??` defaults in one function can push complexity from 8 to 12 before adding any loops.

- **knip grandfathering strategy (US-005, CAM-60, 2026-06-23)**: to introduce a green knip gate on an existing codebase with many findings, use a combination of: (1) broad entry points including `test/**/*.ts` and `scripts/**/*.ts` so test-consumed exports are reachable; (2) `ignore` for specific unused files; (3) `ignoreBinaries` for external tools (claude, tmux, ps, lsof); (4) `exclude: ["exports","types","duplicates"]` to grandfather the bulk of export/type issues without deleting code. This satisfies the "green today, prevents NEW dead files" goal while leaving tightening to future stories. knip "configuration hints" (suggestions to remove redundant ignore entries) are informational-only and do not affect the exit code; leave the ignore entries in place as explicit documentation of what was grandfathered. `content.split('\n').length` (the file-size script's counter) is always `wc -l + 1` for files with a trailing newline: set budgets accordingly.

- **Backward-compatible TOML reader for scalar-to-section evolution (US-003, CAM-53, 2026-06-24)**: when a TOML key transitions from a scalar form (`backend = "claude"`) to a section form (`[backend]\nname = "claude"`), update the reader to handle both: check `typeof value === 'string'` first (scalar), then `typeof value === 'object'` for the section form (`value['name']`). This preserves backward compat with existing config files while consuming newly written section-form configs. Applied to `readBackend` in `src/config/models.ts`.

- **jscpd v5 config format (US-006, CAM-60, 2026-06-23)**: jscpd v5 (Rust rewrite) reads the same `.jscpd.json` as v4. Keys: `threshold` (number), `reporters` (array of strings, e.g. `["threshold"]`), `ignore` (array of globs). The `threshold` reporter exits 0 when duplication is at or below the threshold and exits 1 (with an error message) only when duplication exceeds it: safe as a ratchet gate. Paths to scan are positional CLI args (not a config key), so the gate command must pass them explicitly: `bunx jscpd@5 --config .jscpd.json src scripts`. The `reporters: ["threshold"]` + threshold combo produces silent output on pass and a one-line error on fail, which is CI-friendly. Confirmed on jscpd@5 with src/+scripts/ (3.87% actual duplication, threshold 4%).

- **makeNotifyOrchestrator factory pattern (US-002, CAM-70, 2026-06-23)**: exported from `src/supervisor/host.ts`; accepts `sessionName` and a `TmuxSpawnFn` (session.ts type: returns `SpawnSyncReturns`, not the loop.ts `{ stdout, exitCode }` shape). Internally calls `getOrchPaneId` then `buildWorkerReportSendKeysArgv`. In `buildSupervisorOptions`, wire it with a `tmuxSpawnFn` adapter over `spawnSync`; the adapter parameter must be named `spawnOpts` (not `opts`) to avoid collision with the outer `opts: RunSupervisorOptions` variable. `notifyOrchestrator` is then a bare property in the `opts` object: `notifyOrchestrator,`. The factory is the testable seam; the production wiring always goes through `buildSupervisorOptions`.

- **notifyOrchestrator seam + FIXES_PENDING:K verdict format (US-001, CAM-70, 2026-06-23)**: `RunSupervisorOptions.notifyOrchestrator?: (line: string) => void` is the injectable seam for pushing review verdicts to the orchestrator pane. The loop calls it after every non-error review dispatch when `prd.review.lastVerdict` is non-null. `formatReviewVerdictLine(round, lastVerdict)` in `worker-report.ts` is the pure formatter (mirrors `formatWorkerReportSummary` as precedent). The stored verdict for FIXES_PENDING is now `'FIXES_PENDING:K'` (with the finding count) not bare `'FIXES_PENDING'`; this lets the formatter include K without needing to re-count stories. US-002 (wiring story) injects the actual tmux `send-keys` call via this callback; the loop itself contains no tmux details.

- **Ratchet gate pattern (US-007, CAM-60, 2026-06-23)**: ratchets enforce snapshot-on-adopt, change-direction-locked. Snapshot: the metric ceiling/floor is captured in a JSON budget file (e.g. `file-size-budget.json`, `coverage-budget.json`) at the point the gate is adopted. Change-direction-locked: improvement (lower size, higher coverage, lower dup) is always free; degradation (raise size ceiling, lower coverage floor, raise dup threshold) requires a tracker reference (`CAM-NNN`, `#N`, or `https://`) in the staged diff of the budget file. The check reads `git diff --cached <budget-file>`, so the tracker ref must be staged, not just present in the working tree. jscpd has no built-in tracker-ref check: the tracker-ref convention for `.jscpd.json` changes is enforced by code review and the commit message, not by the gate script. When a story legitimately degrades a ratchet metric: file a tracker issue first, include the ref in the budget diff, then implement. Recovery: see `docs/recovery-runbook.md` scenario (j). Coverage floors snapshot the measured value but the gate compares with a small tolerance epsilon (0.5pp) to absorb cross-environment jitter, since the CI runner measures slightly different coverage than local (CAM-60, found on PR #66).

- **Spawn-resolution emitter pattern (US-007, CAM-53, 2026-06-24)**: `src/logging/spawn-resolution.ts` exports `emitSpawnResolution({ phase, model, backend, writeEvent?, emitNotice? })`. Call it at every cam-controlled spawn (orchestrator in run.ts, implementer in loop.ts, reviewer in review.ts) immediately before the `respawn-pane -k` tmux call. Injectable seams: `writeEvent` (capture in tests, omit in production for stderr-only mode), `emitNotice` (capture the codex guard notice in tests without hitting real stderr). Codex guard: when `backend === 'codex'`, `CODEX_GUARD_NOTICE = 'codex backend not yet wired (CAM-54)'` is emitted via `emitNotice` (default: `process.stderr.write`). Guard is informational, never aborts the spawn. Test files live under `test/logging/` mirroring `src/logging/`.

- **[models]/[backend] config surface and read-at-spawn invariant (US-008, CAM-53, 2026-06-24)**: per-phase models are stored under `[models]` in `scripts/cam/project.toml` (keys: orchestrator, planner, auditor, implementer, reviewer, ship); the backend is stored under `[backend]` as `name = "<value>"`. Both are read at spawn time via `readPhaseModel(phase, configPath)` and `readBackend(configPath)` from `src/config/models.ts`; both fall back to `DEFAULTS` on any read/parse error so the loop is never blocked by a misconfigured toml. Inspect the resolved values non-interactively with `cam config --show` (implemented via `printConfigShow` in `src/commands/config.ts`, injectable writer seam for tests, no Ink/TTY required). Reset to defaults: remove the `[models]` and `[backend]` sections from `project.toml`. Recover from a typo'd model: run `cam config` (interactive wizard) or hand-edit the key and verify with `cam config --show`.

- **Frontmatter model: rewriter pattern (US-006, CAM-53, 2026-06-24)**: in-session phases (planner, auditor, ship) run as Task subagents or slash steps where `cam` cannot pass `--model` on the command line; the chosen model must reach them via the `model:` frontmatter line of their `.claude/` runtime files. Use `rewriteFrontmatterModel(content, newModel)` (scoped regex `/^(model:)\s*.+$/m` over the single line) to rewrite string-in/string-out without parsing YAML (CAM-69 rule: js-yaml v5 ESM-only breaks in vendored scripts). Export `FRONTMATTER_TARGET_PHASE_PATHS` (keyed by phase name, values relative to cwd) so tests can assert that none of the targets is under `templates/`. `mergeConfigChoices` accepts an optional `cwd?: string`; when provided it reads/rewrites each existing target file; when absent (old callers, unit tests that only test TOML) the frontmatter step is skipped silently.

- **Structured reviewer exit report pattern (US-001, CAM-75, 2026-06-25)**: The reviewer agent is READ-ONLY for source code (Edit/NotebookEdit disallowed) but writes one ephemeral file: `scripts/cam/review-report.json` (REVIEW_REPORT_FILENAME in `src/supervisor/review-report.ts`). To enable this, `Write` is listed in the agent's `tools:` frontmatter (not in `disallowedTools`), and the constraint section calls it out explicitly as the single permitted write exception. The file is gitignored in both `.gitignore` and `templates/.gitignore` (symmetric to worker-report.json). The ReviewReport schema: `{ verdict: string, findings: Array<{severity, file?, line?, text}> }`. Mirror this "ephemeral exit report + REVIEW_REPORT_FILENAME constant" pattern for any new agent that needs to hand structured output back to the sidecar without committing state to the repo.

- **Review-report.json reader dep-injection pattern (US-002, CAM-75, 2026-06-25)**: `makeReviewDispatch` accepts `readReviewReport?: () => ReviewReport | null`; when present it is checked BEFORE the pane-death guard in the poll loop (the file may arrive just before the pane registration catches the exit). Returns null on read/parse error (never throws), matching the `makeReadWorkerReport` contract. File presence breaks the loop immediately (primary completion signal); `parseReviewVerdict` over capture-pane text is retained as a human-readable fallback. `makeReadReviewReport(cwd)` in `host.ts` is the production factory. Fix stories created by `buildFixStories` receive each finding as `notes` (one finding per story, formatted as `"SEVERITY [file:line]: text"`). When the fallback tag-based path is used (no file), stories get no `notes`. Mirror this same "inject reader dep + check file before pane-death + null-on-error" pattern for any future agent structured exit report.

- **Findings durability channels (US-003, CAM-75, 2026-06-25)**: Two channels carry review findings; no third artifact is allowed. (1) `scripts/cam/review-report.json` (REVIEW_REPORT_FILENAME) = ephemeral event, written by the reviewer at exit, gitignored, overwritten each round. (2) `prd.review.findings` in prd.json = durable state, written by `makeReviewDispatch` on the FIXES_PENDING path when `fileFindings !== undefined`. The conditional spread `...(fileFindings !== undefined ? { findings: fileFindings } : {})` ensures the tag-based fallback path leaves `prd.review.findings` absent (not `undefined`). `PrdSnapshot.review` in `decide.ts` must declare the `findings` field so TypeScript accepts it.

- **warnFn injectable seam for dispatch fallback warnings (US-004, CAM-75, 2026-06-25)**: when a dispatch function has a "try file, fall back to tag" pattern, add `warnFn?: (msg: string) => void` to its options (default `console.warn`). Emit the warning at verdict-resolution time (once, after the poll loop exits with fallback), not on every failed file-read poll. The guard is `if (primaryReader !== undefined)` (was the file expected?) before calling `warnFn`. Tests inject a capture array for `warnFn` and assert `warnings.length === 1` to confirm the warning fires exactly once. Do NOT call the default `console.warn` in tests that don't inject a custom warnFn if you want silent output; the default fires and produces expected log noise.

- **File-assert oracle robustness for import paths (US-R2-001, CAM-53, 2026-06-24)**: `grep -q "from '../config/models'"` fails when a follow-up story adds a `.ts` extension (making it `from '../config/models.ts'`) because the closing quote `'` no longer directly follows `models`. To write an oracle that tolerates both forms, omit the closing quote from the pattern: `grep -q "from '../config/models"` matches both `models'` and `models.ts'`. Apply this whenever an import path oracle must survive future extension additions or removals.

- **JSON reader shape guards: validate discriminator fields, not just typeof (US-R2-001, 2026-06-25)**: checking only `typeof parsed === 'object'` allows wrong-shape objects (e.g., `{ notAVerdict: true }`) to pass the guard and be cast to a typed interface. The missing required field becomes `undefined` at runtime, causing downstream code to silently derive wrong values (e.g., `/^FIXES_PENDING:(\d+)$/.exec(undefined)` returns null, yielding count 0 instead of a fallback). Fix: add `!Array.isArray(parsed) && typeof (parsed as Record<string, unknown>)['key'] === 'string'` for every required string discriminator before the cast. This applies to any `readXxx` factory that parses external JSON files (`makeReadWorkerReport`, `makeReadReviewReport`, etc.).

- **Ratchet raise-and-retry now happens in-story, not at the ship gate (US-003, CAM-79/CAM-260, 2026-06-25, updated 2026-07-10)**: the deterministic ship runner (`src/supervisor/ship-runner.ts`) does not auto-raise a ratchet ceiling -- `bun run check:all` is run as a plain gate, and a ratchet failure there simply fails the ship step. The raise-and-retry procedure (edit the budget file, add the current issue tracker ref in the top-level `"_ref"` field, stage the file, re-run the gate) is now the implementer's responsibility in-story, before the ship phase ever runs: if a story's own diff legitimately grows a budgeted file past its ceiling, the implementer raises that file's ceiling as part of the same story commit. `docs/recovery-runbook.md` (j.1) remains the recovery-time fallback for when a ratchet gate blocks a story or is discovered stale after the fact.

- **Up-to-date guard + best-effort push pattern (US-005, CAM-86, 2026-06-25)**: before any mutation in a git-writing function, run: (1) `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` and return `{ ok: false, reason: 'detached-head' }` if result is `'HEAD'`; (2) `git rev-parse main` and return `{ ok: false, reason: 'missing-main' }` on non-zero exit; (3) best-effort `git fetch origin main` (ignore exit code); (4) `git rev-parse origin/main` -- if exit is non-zero, SKIP the divergence check entirely (no remote configured, e.g. real-git tmpdir integration tests); if exit is zero compare local sha vs origin sha and return `{ ok: false, reason: 'diverged' }` on mismatch. After the commit, call `git push origin main` through the injected spawnFn; on non-zero exit call `printError` and continue (push is best-effort, commit already landed). Return type is a discriminated union: `{ ok: true; ...fields } | { ok: false; reason: string }`. Callers must narrow with `if (!result.ok)` before accessing success fields. The guard design keeps all six git calls on the injected spawnFn so unit tests assert argv with no network.

- **State-file phase-writer thin-proxy template (US-005, CAM-149, 2026-07-05)**: `runPlan`'s `buildPlanningBody` (plan.ts) + `writeStateFile` pattern is now proven twice (plan.ts for `phase:planning`, ship.ts for `phase:shipping`): liveness/bootstrap preamble -> `paneCountMutex` busy-refusal -> build a merged state-file body (read+parse the existing file if present via `parseStateFile`, carry forward `max_iterations`/`completion_promise`/`started_at`/`pid`/`iteration`/`current_story`/`stories_done`/`stories_total`/`plan_issue`, override only `phase` + `lastActivity`) -> `(options.writeFn ?? writeStateFile)(cwd, body, { force: true })`. Any future thin-proxy that needs to trigger a new sidecar phase (instead of injecting a slash command via send-keys) should copy this exact shape: drop the `getOrchPaneId`/`sendKeysWhenIdle`/`capturePaneFn` send-keys machinery entirely (no longer needed once the write IS the signal), keep only `tmuxSpawnFn` for the liveness+mutex checks. The rewritten test file mirrors plan.test.ts 1:1: a `makeWriteCapture()` helper capturing `{ body }` calls, then `parseStateFile(writeCalls[0].body).phase` assertions instead of grepping `spawnFn.calls` for a `send-keys` argv.

- **0.x semver convention placement (US-002, CAM-89, 2026-06-26)**: the 0.x demotion rule (major bump -> minor increment while major == 0) lives ONLY in `computeNextVersion` (src/release/version.ts), NOT in `classifyBump` (src/release/bump.ts). `classifyBump` always returns the raw signal (`major` for breaking changes regardless of current version); `computeNextVersion` applies the version-aware demotion. This separation keeps each function single-purpose and testable in isolation.

- **Thinning a phase-signal command doc: hygiene test + doc cross-refs are part of the story (US-006, CAM-149, 2026-07-05)**: when a `.claude/commands/*.md` file is reduced from step-by-step prose to a thin phase-signal stub (mirroring `cam-plan.md`), two collateral surfaces must be updated in the same story or `bun test`/doc accuracy regresses: (1) any `test/vendor/*-hygiene.test.ts` regression guard that asserted the OLD prose was present (e.g. `toContain("gh pr create")`) must be rewritten to assert the NEW invariants (signal written, no raw gh/git prose, ADR cross-reference present) -- these tests exist precisely to catch drift, so leaving them stale makes them fail for the right reason but the wrong content; (2) any `docs/recovery-runbook.md` (or similar) cross-reference that names a numbered "Step N" of the now-thinned doc goes stale the moment the numbering disappears -- grep the target doc's old step markers (`Step 3`, `Step 7`, etc.) across the docs tree before finishing and repoint them at the TS module that now owns that behavior (e.g. `src/supervisor/ship-runner.ts`, `src/release/ship-pr.ts`). Both fixes are in-scope surgical follow-through of the same edit, not scope creep: the story's own diff is what made the old assertions/pointers stale.

- **Widened SpawnFn for git plumbing (US-001, CAM-86, 2026-06-25)**: When a primitive needs to pass a temp GIT_INDEX_FILE via env AND feed stdin to `git hash-object -w --stdin`, extend the SpawnFn options to `{ encoding: 'utf8'; env?: Record<string, string>; input?: string }` (strict superset of ship-finalize's `{ encoding: 'utf8' }`). This is the pattern in `src/commands/issue-file.ts`. Build a safe `Record<string, string>` from process.env by filtering undefined values with `Object.entries(process.env).filter(([,v]) => v !== undefined)`. Use `mkdtempSync` + `rmSync` in a finally block for the temp index; in unit tests the fake SpawnFn means the temp dir is created empty and cleaned up correctly without any real git calls.

- **Discriminated-union parseXxxArgs pattern (US-003, CAM-86, 2026-06-25)**: when a CLI command gains an in-process flag (e.g. `--file-local`, `--finalize`) in addition to the thin-proxy path, change the parser return type to a discriminated union `{ mode: 'flag-name'; help: false } | { mode: 'text'; text: string; help: false } | { mode?: never; help: true }` and export the type. Route through a `dispatchXxx(parsed, deps?)` function that branches on `parsed.mode`. Injectable `deps` (e.g. `fileLocalFn`, `runIssueFn`) are both `() => Promise<number>` thunks so tests can assert branch isolation without touching real I/O. After the `if (parsed.help)` guard in `main()`, TypeScript narrows to the non-help union members and the call to `dispatchXxx(parsed)` typechecks cleanly. Existing tests that access discriminated fields (e.g. `result.text`) must add a `if (result?.mode === 'text')` narrowing guard or TypeScript will complain that the field does not exist on all union members.

- **cam-ship gate is `bun run check:all` (US-001, CAM-79, 2026-06-25)**: `/cam-ship` Step 3 runs `bun run check:all` as the single quality gate -- the exact spine CI runs (ci-parity from CAM-59 pins local check:all == CI). Never use the partial three-command form (typecheck + test + embed-vendor:check) in the ship step; check:all is a strict superset. Both the runtime copy (`.claude/commands/cam-ship.md`) and the `cam init` template (`templates/commands/cam-ship.md`) pin the literal `bun run check:all` command. After touching either copy, run `bun run embed-vendor` and verify with `bun run embed-vendor:check`.

- **Allowlist .dockerignore + BuildKit lazy-context gotcha (US-001, CAM-177, 2026-07-07)**: the repo-root `.dockerignore` for the cam-worker image build is an ALLOWLIST, not a denylist: `*` excludes everything, then `!.devcontainer`, `.devcontainer/*`, `!.devcontainer/claude-config.json` re-include only the one path the Dockerfile COPYs (last-match-wins evaluation requires BuildKit, not the legacy builder). Any future Dockerfile COPY needs a matching re-include line here or the build breaks. Verification gotcha: on a modern BuildKit driver (confirmed with `docker buildx` via the `orbstack` driver), the `[internal] load build context` step already lazily syncs only the files the Dockerfile actually references, so the "transferring context" line can read the same tiny size (`87B`) with or without the `.dockerignore` present locally. This does not make the `.dockerignore` redundant: it is still load-bearing for the legacy (non-BuildKit) builder, for BuildKit drivers/CI runners without lazy FSSync, and as the documented contract for future COPY additions. Cite the `[internal] load build context: transferring context: <N>B` line from the build log as the oracle evidence regardless of which builder produced it.

- **capture-pane is rendered markdown: structured findings need a file channel (CAM-75, 2026-06-25)**: `tmux capture-pane -p` returns what the TUI renderer printed to the terminal. Ink renders `##` headings and `- [file:line]` bullets as plain text with indentation; any markdown-flavored structure in the reviewer output is consumed by the renderer and does not appear verbatim in capture-pane output. This means the sidecar cannot reliably parse structured findings (per-file, per-line severity entries) out of scrollback text. Solution: the reviewer writes `review-report.json` (a dedicated structured exit file, gitignored), and the sidecar reads the file directly instead of parsing pane output. The `<review>` tag in the pane remains the human-readable sentinel and the last-resort fallback; `review-report.json` is the primary machine-readable channel. See docs/recovery-runbook.md section (m).

- **Orchestrator reads state files from main (US-006, CAM-86, 2026-06-25)**: when an orchestrator slash-command reads a shared state file (e.g. `scripts/cam/issues/CAM-NNNN.json`) that is committed on `main` but may lag on a feature branch, use `git show main:<path> 2>/dev/null || cat <path>`. The `git show` form reads the committed object from the main branch ref without requiring a checkout, so a just-filed issue (committed to main by `cam issue --file-local`) is immediately visible from any branch. The `|| cat` fallback handles repos without a main branch or git read errors gracefully. Apply this pattern to any orchestrator command that reads cam state that is always committed to main.

- **commit-tree-to-main backlog mutation** (US-007, CAM-86, 2026-06-25): `cam issue --file-local` writes a new `scripts/cam/issues/CAM-NNNN.json` file directly on `main` without a checkout using three git plumbing steps: (1) read-from-main allocation -- enumerate existing files via `git ls-tree -r --name-only main scripts/cam/issues/` and derive `max(parsed ids) + 1`; (2) off-main commit-tree -- hash the new JSON via `git hash-object -w --stdin` into a temp index (`GIT_INDEX_FILE=<tmpdir>/index git update-index --add --cacheinfo`) and write a tree object via `git write-tree`; (3) on-main direct commit -- `git commit-tree <tree> -p $(git rev-parse main) -m "<msg>"` then `git update-ref refs/heads/main <sha>` with CAS advances main atomically. Current HEAD branch is never touched. Recovery for a wrong-branch commit: cherry-pick the stray commit onto main, then `git rebase main` on the feature branch to drop it (`git push --force-with-lease`). See docs/recovery-runbook.md section (n).

- **Single-source outcome contract and 5-concern model (CAM-77, 2026-06-25)**: The implementer outcome is determined by one authoritative source per concern. Event=worker-report.json (AUTHORITATIVE: sidecar reads `report.story` + `report.outcome`; absent/malformed/stale report falls to pane-died/timeout failure nets, no false-pass). State=prd.json (`passes:true` integrity confirmation only, read AFTER the report; not a primary outcome source). Context=handoff.json (forward-context for the NEXT implementer agent only; NOT a sidecar control signal; an absent or stale handoff does not change the named story when a valid report is present). Observability=.claude/cam-worker-events.jsonl (append-only flight recorder, supervisor-owned). Wisdom=scripts/cam/patterns.md (this file). The sentinel string `CAM_IMPLEMENTER_STATUS=DONE` in the pane scrollback is human-readable corroboration only, never parsed as a gate. Supersedes the 'worker-report-fallback' framing from CAM-58: the fallback path (no valid report present) is the FAILURE path that routes to the pane-died/timeout nets, not an alternate success path. Detail strings in `loop.ts` and `result.ts` still carry 'worker-report-fallback' as legacy labels for backward compat with older tests; the label marks the PRESENT-REPORT primary path (the valid-report block in result.ts, covering DONE/BLOCKED/PRD_COMPLETE branches); loop.ts:877 derives winningSrc='worker-report' precisely when the detail string contains this label (absent-report failure path has no such label and derives winningSrc='fallback').

- **Single-pusher invariant: sidecar is the sole emitter of [cam] narrations (US-003, CAM-78, 2026-06-25)**: The sidecar's `notifyOrchestrator` is the SOLE emitter of `[cam]` terminal-event narrations to the orchestrator pane. Both the implementer outcome line (`[cam] US-XXX DONE ...`) and the review verdict line (`[cam] review round-N CLEAN`) reach the orchestrator pane through the same seam in `loop.ts`: `formatWorkerReportSummary(report)` called after the no-progress guard on genuine advances, and `formatReviewVerdictLine(round, verdict)` called after non-error review dispatches. The implementer worker no longer self-pushes its `[cam]` line (Step B was removed in US-003/CAM-78 from both `.claude/agents/subagent-implementer.md` and the `templates/` mirror). `cam next` is a pure `active:true` flip with no pane interaction (US-001/CAM-78). No code path outside `loop.ts` calls `notifyOrchestrator`. Consequence: one terminal event produces exactly one `[cam]` narration line; the old vestigial glued-double-push (task prompt and `[cam]` line adjacent on the orchestrator pane) is impossible by construction.

- **spawnSync exit-status guard (US-R1-001, 2026-06-26)**: `spawnSync` (node:child_process / Bun SpawnFn) does NOT throw on non-zero exit. It returns `{ status: number | null }` where `null` means signal-kill. ALWAYS check `(result.status ?? 1) !== 0` after any git call that must succeed (git add, git commit, git tag, etc.). On failure: call `printError` then `throw new Error(...)`. This prevents silent swallowing of pre-commit hook rejections, which leave files staged-but-uncommitted while the function returns success. Established pattern in tag.ts and finalizeCycleClose; now also in runShipBump.

- **Template hook executable-bit restoration (US-004, CAM-91, 2026-06-26)**: `writeFileSync` does not carry the source file mode. Any shell script shipped under `templates/.claude/hooks/` must be chmod'd executable after writing: call `chmodSync(dst, 0o755)` immediately after `writeFileSync` for any `relPath.startsWith('.claude/hooks/')` path in `materializeTemplates`. The source mode is never stored in the `templatesContents` map (it is a plain `Record<string, string>`), so the caller must always restore the mode explicitly. Assert in tests with `statSync(hookPath).mode & 0o100` (owner-exec bit = S_IXUSR).

- **Sibling axis-mutation CLI subcommand pattern (US-003, CAM-210, 2026-07-07)**: `cam issue close <id>` (stage axis) and `cam issue abandon <id>` (status axis) are siblings mirrored 1:1: same positional-parse shape ahead of the free-text fallthrough (`args[0] === '<verb>'`, missing id is a parse error, extra args are a parse error), same `IssueDispatchDeps.<verb>IssueOnMainFn?: (id: string) => <Verb>IssueOnMainOutcome` seam, same dispatchIssue branch shape (printHint on ok:true + `CAM_ISSUE_RESULT=<id>`, printError + `CAM_ISSUE_RESULT=ERROR reason=<reason>` on !ok, always exit 0/1 accordingly), and same production-default placement: `default<Verb>IssueFn(cwd, id)` lives in `src/release/post-merge.ts` next to its sibling (wraps `<verb>IssueOnMain` from `issue-specify.ts` with a real spawnSync+clock closure), even when `runPostMerge` itself never calls the new default. This keeps every real-spawnSync closure in exactly one place (jscpd-avoidance) and makes a third sibling (e.g. a future `reopen <id>`) a copy-paste of this exact shape across four sites: `ParsedIssueArgs`, `parseIssueArgs`, `IssueDispatchDeps` + `dispatchIssue`, and `ISSUE_HELP`.

- **Bun.spawn stdin/stdout pattern for shell-script integration tests (US-003, CAM-91, 2026-06-26)**: to invoke a real shell script with piped input in a bun test, pass `stdin: new TextEncoder().encode(payloadString)` (a Uint8Array, which satisfies the TypedArray | DataView contract), set `stdout: 'pipe'` and `stderr: 'pipe'`, then read with `new Response(proc.stdout).text()` and `await proc.exited` (Promise<number>). Use `test.skipIf(Bun.which('tool') === null)` to skip gracefully when a CLI dep (e.g. jq) is absent on the runner. Do NOT re-implement the script logic in TS (anti-shadow-mock: CAM-55 lesson).

- **PreToolUse deny contract + defensive subagent_type read (US-002, CAM-91, 2026-06-26)**: The Claude Code PreToolUse deny contract is: exit 0 + stdout JSON `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PreToolUse","permissionDecision":"deny","permissionDecisionReason":"..."}}`. Allow = exit 0 with no output. The spawned subagent type for Task/Agent calls lives in `tool_input`; the top-level `agent_type` field is the CALLER's identity (present only when running under `--agent` or inside a subagent), not the spawned type. Read defensively: `.tool_input.subagent_type // .tool_input.agent_type // .agent_type // ""`. The `.claude/hooks/` directory was gitignored by default convention; to commit a project-level hook, remove the directory entry from `.gitignore` (or add a negation). The settings.json hook registration shape: `hooks.PreToolUse[].{matcher, hooks:[{type:"command", command:"<path>"}]}`. jq `//` (alternative operator) falls through on null/false, so the three-path read correctly returns empty string when all fields are absent.

- **cam next is a pure sidecar trigger, no send-keys (US-001, CAM-78, 2026-06-25)**: `cam next` writes `active:true` to `.claude/cam-loop.local.md` and returns. It does NOT send any task prompt to the orchestrator pane. The sidecar (`src/supervisor/host.ts`) holds the canonical worker prompt ('Implement the next user story from scripts/cam/prd.json per your AGENT.md.') and injects it when it dispatches the implementer worker via `respawn-pane -k`. Consequence: `sendKeysWhenIdle`, `DEFAULT_TASK_PROMPT`, `capturePaneFn`, `idleTimeoutMs`, and `taskPrompt` are absent from `src/commands/next.ts`. Tests that formerly asserted send-keys carried the prompt must assert instead that no send-keys call was made.

- **Re-plan prompt builder mirrors the target-aware planner prompt, adds a findings block (US-001, CAM-204, 2026-07-06)**: `buildReplanPlannerTaskPrompt(issueId, findings: PlanVerdictFinding[])` in `src/supervisor/plan-runner.ts` mirrors `buildPlannerTaskPrompt` (CAM-157: name the exact issue id, forbid backlog re-selection, instruct writing to `scripts/cam/prd.json`) and adds a numbered findings block embedding each finding's `description` and, only when `suggestion !== undefined`, an appended `Suggestion: <text>` clause. Never string-interpolate an optional field directly (`` `${finding.suggestion}` `` renders the literal word `undefined` when the field is absent); always branch on `!== undefined` first and build the suffix conditionally. This is the only channel that reaches a freshly spawned planner subagent: it does not read `patterns.md` or `plan-verdict-report.json` on its own (session isolation), so every fact the round-2 planner needs must be embedded in the prompt string itself. Round-cap constant `MAX_REPLAN_ROUNDS = 2` lives beside it (ADR-0012: non-convergence after the cap is a hard stop, escalate, never proceed-with-debt; there is no plan analog of the review loop's MAX_ROUNDS_DEBT). Both are exported but NOT yet wired into `runPlanPhase`/`runPostAuditAction` as of US-001; the actual BLOCK->re-plan loop consuming them is a later story in the same PRD.

- **file-size-budget.json ceilings are `content.split('\n').length`, not `wc -l` (CAM-204, 2026-07-06)**: the file-size gate's `makeDefaultGetSizes` counts lines via `content.split('\n').length`, which is `wc -l + 1` for any file ending in a trailing newline (the common case). When raising a ceiling to match a file you just edited, do not trust `wc -l <file>` as the new ceiling value: run `bun run check:all` (or `bun scripts/check-file-sizes.ts`) once first and read the exact `<path>:<N> exceeds budget ceiling` number it reports, then set the ceiling to that `<N>`. Off-by-one here just re-trips the same gate on the next run.

- **Read-only-orchestrator write-channel triad + flag-order-independent parsing (US-003, CAM-118, 2026-07-06)**: three CLI entrypoints now share one shape for letting a read-only orchestrator (Edit/Write/NotebookEdit disallowed) persist state without tmux: `cam journal append`, `cam issue --file-local`, and `cam spec --write-docs <id>` all read a JSON blob from stdin (`Bun.stdin.text()`), parse+validate in-process, call a `*OnMain` git-plumbing writer, and exit 0/1 with a machine-readable sentinel on success. When adding a fourth such command, mirror `parseSpecArgs`'s discriminated union AND its argv scanning style: strip the mode-selecting flag (e.g. `--write-docs`) from the args array FIRST via `.filter()`, then scan the remainder for the single positional id. This makes flag/positional order irrelevant (`cam spec --write-docs CAM-1` and `cam spec CAM-1 --write-docs` both parse identically), which matters because the orchestrator's own slash-command prose is not guaranteed to emit flags in a fixed order. Also: `validateDomainDocsPayload(x: unknown)` (src/domain-docs/render.ts) accepts `unknown`, not a pre-typed payload, specifically so a stdin-JSON entrypoint can pass the raw `JSON.parse()` result straight through without an unsafe cast at the call site living anywhere but the single guarded boundary inside the `*OnMain` writer.

- **Wiring a pure loop-driver into a production factory: extract the opts-assembly, not the seams (US-004, CAM-204, 2026-07-06)**: when a pure dep-injected driver (e.g. `runPlanPhaseWithReplan` in plan-runner.ts, US-003) gains new optional seams (`teardownPlanPanesFn`, `writeEscalationMarkerFn`) that the production caller must wire to real side effects, build two tiny single-purpose factories in the caller module (`makeTeardownPlanPanesFn(claudeDir): () => void`, `makeWriteEscalationMarkerFn(claudeDir): (params) => void`) that close over `claudeDir`/paths and do exactly one real thing each (mirror `host.ts:398 teardownWorkerPaneFn` verbatim for the kill-pane case: fresh `readWorkerPaneMarker` read, `tmux kill-pane` never `respawn-pane -k`, try/catch silent no-op, never throws). Then, if wiring all the deps into one call pushes the enclosing closure over biome's 80-line limit, extract the ENTIRE options-bag assembly (not just the two new seams) into a `runProduction<Thing>(deps: GroupedDepsInterface)` helper that takes one grouped-deps object and returns the driver's result type directly; the caller closure shrinks to a single grouped-object literal + one call. This is a stronger extraction than the established "factory/helper extraction" pattern (which pulls out sub-pieces) because it moves the WHOLE fan-out call, and it keeps the source-text wiring oracle simple: assert `function makeProductionXFn` contains `runProductionXWithY({` and assert `function runProductionXWithY` contains the actual `driverFn({` call with the new seam keys. Convergence-only marker removal (`removeEscalationMarkerFn`) is a THIRD seam that belongs on the post-audit action function (`runPostAuditAction`), not the loop driver: it fires exactly once, on the specific proceed-branch (audit-approved -> branch-created) path, never on escalated/pause-operator/no-action, so it doesn't belong inside the generic re-plan loop at all.

- **New on-main caller: extract shared guard+push instead of a 5th verbatim copy (US-002, CAM-118, 2026-07-06)**: `checkMainUpToDate`/`pushMainBestEffort` existed as byte-identical private copies in 4 files (issue-specify.ts, issue-file.ts, journal.ts, triage.ts); adding a 5th verbatim copy for a brand-new on-main writer risks tripping the jscpd duplication gate (`bun run check:all`, threshold already raised twice, CAM-124). Instead of copying again, export the pair from `src/git/on-main.ts` (parameterize `checkMainUpToDate(cwd, spawnFn, actionLabel)` so each caller's detached-head error message keeps its own wording) and have ONLY the new caller import them; leave the 4 existing private copies untouched (deduping those is CAM-124's separate scope, not the new story's). This keeps the new file small without expanding the refactor blast radius. Also applicable: reading a lazily-created doc (CONTEXT.md, absent until the first term is written) via `git show main:<path>` must treat a non-zero exit as `null` (not an error), unlike a doc that MUST pre-exist (journal.md, where non-zero exit is a hard failure per US-006/CAM-122). ADR-style idempotent-by-title creation: skip creating a new numbered file when `slugifyAdrTitle(incoming.title)` already matches the slug portion of an existing `NNNN-<slug>.md` filename (strip the numeric prefix and `.md` suffix to compare); this is a distinct idempotency axis from `mergeContextMd`'s idempotent merge-by-term-text (US-001) and both are needed together for a domain-docs writer that touches both CONTEXT.md and docs/adr/ in the same atomic commit.

- **Poll-loop staleness + shape guard before sentinel break (US-006, CAM-77, 2026-06-25)**: `readWorkerReport()` returning non-null must NOT unconditionally break the poll loop with `pollOutcome='sentinel'`. A stale report (story field != advisoryStoryId) would bypass the pane-died/timeout nets (and CAM-44 backoff) and route to 'unknown' -> terminal 'blocked'. Fix: apply a two-part guard before the break: (1) shape check -- `typeof report.story === 'string' && typeof report.outcome === 'string'`; (2) staleness check -- `advisoryStoryId === undefined || report.story === advisoryStoryId`. Only break the poll when both pass. Absent/malformed/stale reports fall through; the pane-died/timeout nets with CAM-44 backoff remain the terminal signal. Pair this with a tightened `makeReadWorkerReport` shape guard in host.ts (US-R2-001 pattern: `!Array.isArray(parsed) && typeof parsed['outcome'] === 'string' && typeof parsed['story'] === 'string'`) so wrong-shape JSON never reaches the poll loop as a non-null value.

- **Repo-root markdown hygiene test pattern (US-002, PR-98, 2026-07-06)**: for any invariant on a committed repo-root markdown file (e.g. "CHANGELOG.md has exactly one line-anchored `## [Unreleased]` heading"), add a dependency-free `test/**/*-hygiene.test.ts` that reads the file directly via `readFileSync(join(import.meta.dir, '..', '..', 'FILE.md'), 'utf8')` (no fixture duplication) and asserts with the same line-anchored regex the production code uses (`/^## \[Unreleased\][ \t]*$/gm`, matching the US-001 rollChangelog fix). This makes the regression guard live next to the file it protects and catches any future hand-edit or roll bug that reintroduces a duplicate/stale heading, not just the transform function's unit behavior.

- **notifyOrchestrator placement rule in loop.ts (US-003, CAM-78, 2026-06-25)**: notify must fire AFTER the no-progress guard, not before it. The CAM-36 no-progress guard sits AFTER outcome-source resolution (line ~975-1012). Two categories fire: (1) GENUINE advances -- the `else { noProgressStreak = 0; }` branch (story was NOT already passing) and PRD_COMPLETE (`outcome.storyId === undefined`). (2) Terminal-blocked returns -- every `return { status: 'blocked', ... }` in the implement branch. The no-progress RETRY path (completedAlreadyPassing = true, streak < MAX) does NOT fire notify (it sleeps + continues without narrating). At every insertion site, re-read the report via `const r = readWorkerReport?.(); if (r) opts.notifyOrchestrator?.(formatWorkerReportSummary(r))` -- the fileReader-closure report binding is out of scope there. The discriminating unit test for this rule: a no-progress test where the poll exits via a report matching the advisory story (ensuring the report-path is active), but readWorkerOutcome returns pass/already-done-story (handoff stale), triggering completedAlreadyPassing = true. Assert notifyOrchestrator called ZERO times. This test FAILS if notify is placed before the guard and PASSES only with the post-guard placement.

- **cam ship --bump step placement and return-type propagation (US-003, CAM-89, 2026-06-26)**: the bump step runs AFTER check:all and BEFORE finalize/push (Step 4 in cam-ship.md). It commits `chore(release): bump version to X.Y.Z` as a visible branch commit. When all commits classify as none, it is a complete no-op (no file writes, no commit). The step is wired via `dispatchShip` with an injectable `bumpFn?` dep (same pattern as `finalizeFn?`). When extending `parseShipArgs` to add a new flag, update all existing `toEqual` assertions in test/ship-args.test.ts and test/ship.test.ts to include the new field -- TypeScript structural equality in `expect().toEqual()` requires an exact shape match. Use `replace_all` in the Edit tool to sweep all call sites at once.

- **Unified result type + writeEvent + emitResultLine injectables (US-007, CAM-89, 2026-06-26)**: when a function has two outcome branches (e.g. no-op vs. real bump) that share the same observable fields, prefer a single result interface over a discriminated union. For ShipBumpResult: `{ from, to, bumpType: BumpLevel, commitsClassified }` with `bumpType: 'none'` and `from === to` for no-op. Both branches emit the same structured event and result line, removing branch-specific dead-ends in callers. Injectable seams: `writeEvent?: (event: Result) => void` (for structured event capture in tests) and `emitResultLine?: (msg: string, suffix?: string) => void` (overrides the shared emitOk call, avoids stdout monkey-patching in tests). The emitOk shape is pinned by a test and annotated with a SHAPE STABILITY comment in source. The caller in index.ts can be simplified to `bumpFn(); return 0;` because the result line is now emitted inside the function.

- **cam-init adaptation markers: downstream-only HTML comments in templates (US-002, CAM-88, 2026-06-26)**: to signal the setup-agent at a point where a cam-cli-specific value (e.g. `bun run check:all`) needs downstream adaptation, add an HTML comment `<!-- cam-init: adaptation point -- ... -->` in the `templates/commands/*.md` or `templates/agents/*.md` source. Exception to the self-hosting rule: do NOT copy the marker into the `.claude/` runtime mirror when the comment is irrelevant noise in cam-cli itself (e.g. `check:all` IS the canonical gate in cam-cli). After adding a marker run `bun run embed-vendor` and verify with `bun run embed-vendor:check`. The grep oracle for AC uses the marker token `cam-init`, NOT the adapted value (`check:all`), because the value already appears elsewhere in the embedded file.

- **Bounded gh pr update-branch auto-recovery gated on autoMergeRequest, not pollCount (US-001, CAM-182, 2026-07-05)**: `gh pr view --json ...,autoMergeRequest,url` returns `autoMergeRequest` as null/absent when GitHub auto-merge is not armed and a non-null object once armed (`gh pr merge --auto`); gate any BEHIND-branch auto-recovery on this field so `gh pr update-branch` is only run when the PR will merge itself once CI goes green (never leaves the PR mid-flight needing a manual merge follow-up). Right after `gh pr update-branch` runs, `mergeStateStatus` flips to `BLOCKED`/`UNSTABLE` while CI re-runs, NOT back to `BEHIND` -- so the retry cap (`MAX_UPDATE_BRANCH_ATTEMPTS`) must be spent only on actual BEHIND observations (incremented inside the BEHIND-handling branch), never on raw poll count; a poll-count-based cap would spuriously exhaust while CI is simply re-running. Mutating calls (`gh pr update-branch`) strip `GITHUB_TOKEN` from the child env (`Object.entries(process.env).filter(([k,v]) => v !== undefined && k !== 'GITHUB_TOKEN')`) so `gh` falls back to its keyring OAuth token, same as `gh pr create`/`merge`/`comment` (the .env fine-grained PAT lacks "Pull requests: write"); the read-only `gh pr view` poll keeps the ambient token unchanged.

- **File-size ratchet: edit the JSON budget with the Edit tool, not a full json.dump rewrite (US-001, CAM-182, 2026-07-05)**: `scripts/file-size-budget.json` uses 2-space indentation; re-serializing the whole file with Python's `json.dump(..., indent='\t')` (or any indent/key-order that differs from the original) produces a full-file diff (hundreds of lines) even when only one ceiling changed, because `git diff` is line-based. Use the `Edit` tool for a surgical two-line change: bump the ceiling value and prepend one new sentence to the front of the `_ref` log string (format: `"<branch> (<story>, <tracker>): raise <file> <old>-><new> (<reason>). Prior: "` followed by the existing `_ref` value verbatim). This keeps the diff minimal and the tracker-ref oracle (`checkDiffTrackerRef` in `scripts/check-file-sizes.ts`) passes because the ref string appears in the staged diff.

- **gh pr merge --auto best-effort pattern (US-001, CAM-84, 2026-06-26)**: `gh pr merge --auto --squash` without a PR number detects the current branch's PR automatically. When 'Allow auto-merge' or 'Allow squash merging' is disabled in repo Settings, the command exits non-zero with a GraphQL enablePullRequestAutoMerge error (the gh manual does not document this failure mode). The correct ship-step idiom is `gh pr merge --auto --squash || echo "Prerequisite hint: ..."` -- the `|| echo` keeps the ship from aborting (the PR is already created, so failure is recoverable). Pair every best-effort gh command with a hint that names the GitHub Settings location (Settings > General > Pull Requests) so the operator knows exactly where to look.

- **Optional 4th opts-bag for backward-compat extension of persistence helpers (US-004, CAM-101, 2026-06-26)**: to add injectable side-effects (branch-protection, external calls) to an existing pure persistence helper without breaking callers that pass 2-3 args, add an optional 4th parameter as an interface bag (e.g. `MergeModeOpts` in `mergeConfigChoices`). Include all injectable seams in the bag: spawnFn, emitHint, emitWarning, emitResult, and any data field (ownerRepo) that tests need to bypass resolution calls. Production defaults (real spawnSync, printHint, printWarning) are applied inside the function when the bag field is absent. Existing callers (unit tests, config-persist.test.ts) need no update because the 4th arg is optional. The bag type is exported so consumers can construct it in tests without casting.

- **Pure-helper extract for headless-testable setup steps (US-003, CAM-101, 2026-06-26)**: when a cam init/config step mixes Ink/readline TTY detection with business logic that must be unit-tested, extract the business logic into a pure, injectable-dependency function in its own module (e.g. `src/commands/setup-merge-mode.ts`). The test drives the pure function directly with fake spawnFns; the TTY call-site simply delegates to it. To extend `SetupScreen` with a new wizard step: (1) add the value to the `Step` union type and to the step-derivation closure (in order matching the visual layout); (2) add state + OPTIONS constant + `showSection` / `showSummary` flags; (3) add the `<Section>` block to JSX; (4) update `SetupAnswers` and the `onDone` call; (5) add a label helper. Apply the same sequence to `collectViaReadline`, `collectSetupAnswers`, `SetupOptions`, `ParsedSetupArgs`, and `parseSetupArgs`. The step-derivation order determines sequencing: all steps above in the closure must be answered before the current one is active.

- **Branch-protection helper: widened SpawnFn + dual-check GET verification (US-002, CAM-101, 2026-06-26)**: `configureBranchProtection` in `src/release/branch-protection.ts` uses a widened SpawnFn `{ encoding: 'utf8'; input?: string }` (strict superset of ship-finalize's type) to pipe the PUT body via `--input -` without a temp file. PUT requires ALL FOUR top-level fields (omitting any yields 422): `required_status_checks` (strict:true, checks:[{context:"ci"}]), `enforce_admins:false`, `required_pull_request_reviews:null`, `restrictions:null`. GET verification accepts `ci` in either `checks[*].context` (current) or `contexts[*]` (deprecated; GitHub mirrors them). Accept header: `application/vnd.github+json`. Non-zero PUT/GET exit or 403/no-auth text triggers fallback-warned (never throws); CAM-84 prereq check (allow_auto_merge + allow_squash_merge) is best-effort (repo GET failure is silently skipped). Fallback hint and AUTOMERGE_NOTICE are injected via emitHint/emitWarning optionals so tests capture output without touching stderr/stdout.

- **[ship].merge_mode config surface (US-001, CAM-101, 2026-06-26)**: `readMergeMode(configPath?)` in `src/config/models.ts` is the single source of truth for the cam ship merge mode. Returns `"immediate" | "ci-gated"`. Default is `"immediate"` (preserves CAM-84 behavior for existing projects). Returns `"ci-gated"` only when `[ship] merge_mode = "ci-gated"` is set exactly; all other values (missing file/section/key, malformed TOML, non-string, any unrecognized string) fall back to `"immediate"`. Follows the same defensive try/catch/default pattern as `readPhaseModel` and `readBackend`. `MergeMode` union type is exported from the same module.

- **Post-merge version-from-disk pattern (US-006, CAM-101, 2026-06-26)**: `runPostMerge` in `src/release/post-merge.ts` reads the version string from `src/version.ts` on disk AFTER `git pull origin main`, never from the `CAM_VERSION` constant embedded in the running binary. This prevents a stale installed binary from mis-tagging (the installed binary may be several commits behind main). Injectable `readVersionFile?: (path: string) => string` in `PostMergeOptions` enables hermetic unit tests without filesystem access. Parse the version with `/export const CAM_VERSION = '([^']+)'/`. Branch prune (local `branch -d` + remote `push origin --delete`) is best-effort: non-zero exit sets `branchPrunedLocal/Remote: false` but does NOT abort the outcome (result remains `ok: true`).

- **Commit-existence gate as a pure injection point (US-001, CAM-187, 2026-07-05)**: `readWorkerOutcome` (src/supervisor/result.ts) gates a passes:true story behind an optional `commitExistsForStory?: (storyId) => boolean` callback, applied identically at BOTH resolution sites (the worker-report primary path and the handoff/sentinel fallback path) via one shared `confirmCommitGate` helper, never duplicated inline. When the callback is absent, the gate is a no-op (preserves all pre-existing behavior byte-for-byte -- verified by leaving every pre-existing test in result.test.ts unmodified and green). The `requires:'operator'` exemption is read from the SAME `PrdJson.userStories[]` record already being checked for `passes`, so extending that internal shape (adding `requires`) is the only prd.json-reading change needed; no new file read.
