A new WorkerOutcomeKind (`'no-commit'`) was added rather than reusing `'incomplete'`: `'incomplete'` has an established loop.ts side-effect (drives `finalizeStory`/auto-commit), which is wrong for "prd says passes:true but no commit found" (there is nothing to finalize; the claim itself is suspect). Adding a new WorkerOutcomeKind member is safe here because nothing in the codebase switches exhaustively over it (grep `WorkerOutcomeKind` first to confirm before adding one elsewhere). The exported pure matcher `commitSubjectMatchesStory(subject, storyId)` validates the `feat: <Story ID> - <Title>` commit convention (scripts/cam/CLAUDE.md step 8) with a single anchored regex `^feat:\s*<escaped-id>\s*-\s*\S`; anchoring the separator hyphen immediately after the id (not `\S+`/substring matching) is what rejects a longer id sharing this id as a prefix (e.g. subject for "US-0010" must not match a query for "US-001") and rejects the id merely appearing later in the title.

- **ci-gated orchestrator MUST write .cam-merge-watch.json (US-R1-001, 2026-06-26)**: The sidecar's `runMergeWatchFn` early-returns via `existsSync` when `.claude/.cam-merge-watch.json` is absent. If the orchestrator skips writing it, the entire ci-gated pipeline is dead (merge-watch never polls, post-merge never runs). The write happens in cam-ship.md Step 7 ci-gated branch, immediately after `gh pr create`: capture `PR_NUMBER=$(gh pr view --json number --jq '.number')`, capture `BRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)`, then `printf '{"prNumber":%s,"mergedBranch":"%s"}\n' "$PR_NUMBER" "$BRANCH" > .claude/.cam-merge-watch.json`. This is the ONLY producer; unit tests that call `runMergeWatch` directly bypass the file and cannot catch this gap, so the guard lives in cam-ship-hygiene.test.ts.

- **Merge-watch state machine pattern (US-007, CAM-101, 2026-06-26)**: `src/release/merge-watch.ts` owns the gh poll loop for CI-gated ship mode. Injectable deps: `pollFn: GhPollFn` (wraps `gh pr view <N> --json state,mergeStateStatus`), `postMergeFn: PostMergeFn` (wraps `runPostMerge`), `notifyOrchestrator`. Terminal outcomes: state=="MERGED" -> run post-merge + narrate; state=="CLOSED" -> narrate "closed-not-merged" + stop; state=="OPEN" && mergeStateStatus=="BLOCKED" -> narrate "CI red, open, not merged" + stop; timeout (maxPolls exhausted) -> narrate + stop. Non-terminal: pollFn returns null (gh error) = silent retry; OPEN + non-BLOCKED (CLEAN, BEHIND, CONFLICTING, DRAFT, UNKNOWN) = keep polling. GitHub GraphQL MergeStateStatus values: BLOCKED, CLEAN, CONFLICTING, DRAFT, BEHIND, UNKNOWN (no UNSTABLE or MERGED). The sidecar wires it via `runMergeWatchFn?: () => Promise<void>` in `RunSidecarLoopOptions` - called on every idle tick (active:false); production implementation reads `.claude/.cam-merge-watch.json`, removes it before starting (re-entry prevention), then awaits `runMergeWatch`. Only injected when merge_mode=="ci-gated"; immediate mode leaves the field absent (zero behavior change, backward compatible).

- **Sub-state-machine logEvent seam pattern (US-008, CAM-101, 2026-06-26)**: when a sub-state machine (e.g. merge-watch) needs to emit structured WorkerEvents without knowing ts/storyId/uuid, inject `logEvent?: (kind: WorkerEventKind, detail: WorkerEventDetail) => void` on its options bag. The caller (sidecar or test) wraps ts/storyId/uuid into the full WorkerEvent shape and passes the closure. New event kinds are added to `WorkerEventKind` and paired with typed `*EventDetail` interfaces exported from `src/supervisor/events.ts`; the interfaces join the `WorkerEventDetail` union. Tests use `makeInMemoryEventLogger()` from events.ts and a thin wrapping closure to capture emitted kinds and assert the full sequence. `logEvent` is always optional: calling code that omits it continues working with no events emitted (zero side-effects). The `closed` reason on `MergeWatchCiRedEventDetail` distinguishes a CLOSED PR from a BLOCKED one on the same `merge-watch-ci-red` kind.

- **post-merge checkout-main guard (US-R1-002, 2026-06-27)**: any post-merge helper that does `git pull origin main`, `git tag`, or `git branch -d <branch>` MUST first `git -C cwd checkout main`. Without it: `git pull origin main` merges origin/main INTO the current feature branch (not updating main), `git rev-parse HEAD` returns the feature-branch SHA (wrong tagging target), and `git branch -d <feature>` fails because you cannot delete the currently-checked-out branch. Guard pattern mirrors `runTag` (src/commands/tag.ts:66-79). Unit fakes cannot catch this class of bug -- add a real-git integration test (see test/integration/post-merge-on-main.test.ts).

- **Production dep-builder must wire writeEvent or the event is silently discarded (US-R1-002, CAM-89, 2026-06-26)**: when a function accepts `writeEvent?: (event: T) => void`, unit tests confirm the call fires -- but if the production dep-builder (e.g. `_buildBumpOpts`) omits the field, `opts.writeEvent?.()` is a no-op and the event is never persisted. Pattern: the production dep-builder must call `makeFileEventLogger(join(cwd, '.claude/cam-worker-events.jsonl'))` and wrap the payload as `{ ts, storyId, uuid, kind, detail }` before passing to the logger. For payload types without an index signature (TypeScript rejects direct assignment to `Record<string, unknown>`), use `detail: payload as unknown as Record<string, unknown>`. Add an oracle test that reads `index.ts` source text and asserts the dep-builder contains `writeEvent`, `makeFileEventLogger`, `cam-worker-events.jsonl`, and the event `kind` string -- this catches production-wiring gaps that unit injection tests cannot see.

- **CAM_SESSION scope-gate pattern for hooks (US-001, CAM-102, 2026-06-26)**: when a PreToolUse hook must be active only in cam-managed sessions (not in interactive dev sessions), gate at the very top with `if [ -z "${CAM_SESSION:-}" ]; then exit 0; fi` BEFORE reading stdin. The hook process inherits the session environment (confirmed by Claude Code docs: "standard environment inherited from the parent process"), so CAM_SESSION set on `tmux new-session -e` is visible. Test isolation: pass an explicit `env:` to `Bun.spawn` that either includes or excludes `CAM_SESSION`; never rely on ambient env inheritance (ambient CAM_SESSION is set locally in a cam session but unset on CI, causing green-local/red-CI divergence). PATH must be included in the explicit env for bash to resolve jq.

- **readline EOF guard pattern (US-001, CAM-104, 2026-06-26)**: `rl.question(cb)` uses the callback API: the callback fires ONLY on a 'line' event. On EOF (piped /dev/null, CI, closed stdin), the input stream emits 'end', readline emits 'close', but the question callback NEVER fires, so the Promise never resolves. Fix: register `rl.on('close', () => { if (!answered) { answered = true; resolve(defaultValue); } })` BEFORE `rl.question(...)`. The `answered` boolean ensures the normal line-driven resolve wins and the later close-driven resolve is a harmless no-op. Make the function accept an injectable `input: NodeJS.ReadableStream = process.stdin` and use `output: input === process.stdin ? process.stdout : undefined` so tests can inject a stream without writing to stdout. Extension (US-R1-001, 2026-06-26): the `rl.on('close')` guard only fires for the FIRST readline interface on a given stream. When `ask()` is called multiple times on the same stream (e.g. `collectViaReadline({})` with all options undefined), subsequent interfaces created on an already-ended stream NEVER receive 'close' and hang indefinitely. Fix: add a `readableEnded` short-circuit at the top of `ask()`: `if ('readableEnded' in input && (input as unknown as { readableEnded: boolean }).readableEnded) return defaultValue;`. The duck-typed form is required because `NodeJS.ReadableStream` (minimal interface) omits `readableEnded`; `Readable` from `node:stream` exposes it at runtime.

- **Single-stream EOF reuse gotcha in collectViaReadline tests (US-003, CAM-104, 2026-06-26)**: a Node.js Readable stream emits 'end' only once. If `collectViaReadline` is called with multiple undefined options and a single EOF stream, the first `createInterface({ input })` consumes the EOF signal; subsequent readline interfaces created on the same (already-ended) stream never receive 'close', so those questions hang until the test timeout fires. Fix: pre-fill all options except the one being tested so only one readline interface is opened. This is the correct pattern for any integration test that drives `collectViaReadline` with an injected stream.

- **Sub-state-machine optional dep injection gap: add source-text oracle (US-R1-004, 2026-06-27)**: when a sub-state-machine (e.g. runMergeWatch) accepts an optional dep (`logEvent?`), calling code that omits it silently no-ops (zero events, no error). Tests that inject the dep DIRECTLY into the sub-state-machine cannot detect a missing wire in the outer production caller. Fix: add a source-text oracle test that reads the production caller source file and asserts the dep field name is present in the relevant call block. Pattern: `source.match(/await runSubMachine\(\{[\s\S]*?\}\)/)` then `.toContain('depField:')`. This oracle catches what unit injection tests cannot.

- **GitHub BLOCKED = pending OR failed; use statusCheckRollup to distinguish (US-R1-003, 2026-06-27)**: GitHub's GraphQL `mergeStateStatus == "BLOCKED"` fires whenever any required merge condition is unmet -- including required checks that are still running (pending/in-progress). It is NOT exclusive to failed checks. Treating BLOCKED as a terminal ci-red (without inspecting check results) causes a false "CI red" stop on every ci-gated PR while CI is in progress. Fix: request `statusCheckRollup` in `gh pr view --json state,mergeStateStatus,statusCheckRollup` and only treat BLOCKED as ci-red when `hasFailedCheck(statusCheckRollup)` is true (CheckRun.conclusion in FAILURE/CANCELLED/TIMED_OUT/ACTION_REQUIRED/STARTUP_FAILURE, or StatusContext.state in FAILURE/ERROR). An absent or all-null-conclusion rollup means checks are pending: keep polling. This pattern applies to any integration that polls GitHub PR state for CI completion.

- **B-1/B-2 staged dispatch gating pattern (US-005, CAM-150, 2026-07-01)**: when adding a new gating mechanism to the dispatch loop (e.g. container preflight), split into two stories: B-1 (observe-only) wires the injectable seam and logs the result without gating; B-2 (fail-closed) flips the gating using escalateFn. This prevents blocking the live spawn path during integration and keeps each story's diff focused. The seam is an optional dep on `RunSupervisorOptions` (like `ensureWorkerPane`/`escalateFn`); production wiring in `host.ts`; fake dep in tests. The `container-preflight` WorkerEventKind follows the standard pattern: add kind to `WorkerEventKind` union, add typed detail interface, add to `WorkerEventDetail` union.

- **Shared production dep-builder factory prevents two-caller drift (US-004, CAM-149, 2026-07-05)**: when the SAME production wiring (e.g. real fs/git adapters for `finalizeCycleClose`/`runShipBump`) is needed by two independent production callers (the `cam ship --bump`/`--finalize` CLI in index.ts, and the sidecar's deterministic ship-phase runner in sidecar.ts), extract the builder functions into their own module (`src/commands/ship-deps.ts`) rather than duplicating the object-literal wiring in both callers (jscpd duplication gate) or importing one caller's private helper into the other (would create an index.ts <-> sidecar.ts circular import, since index.ts already imports `runSidecar` from sidecar.ts). A source-text oracle test that greps for `function _buildBumpOpts` in `index.ts` must be retargeted to the new module/function name when the function moves (see `test/release/ship-bump.test.ts`'s `buildShipBumpOpts` oracle) -- moving a function invalidates any oracle hardcoded to its old file path.

- **Shipping-phase branch mirrors the planning-phase branch, but the closure owns its own phase reset (US-004, CAM-149, 2026-07-05)**: `loop.ts`'s `phase === 'shipping'` branch is a sibling of the `phase === 'planning'` branch (same precedence: it MUST precede the `active !== true` idle check, since `makeReadActive` derives `active:false` for any phase other than `'implementing'`) and the same outer try/catch-and-log pattern (catches a throwing injected fn, logs `sidecar-exit` with a phase-specific reason, sleeps, continues -- never crashes the sidecar). The one difference: the injected closure (`makeProductionShipPhaseFn`) is responsible for its OWN phase-\>idle reset via a `try/catch/finally` with `setPhase('idle')` in the `finally` block (so idle is restored on both success and failure), whereas the planning closure resets phase conditionally per `PostAuditActionResult` kind. When adding a new phase-branch, decide up front whether the closure or the outer loop owns the phase reset, and grep the existing branch's oracle tests (`test/plan-phase-crash-safety.test.ts`) for the exact assertion shape to mirror.

- **Ratchet-gate detection only fires when the target file is staged (US-004, CAM-149, 2026-07-05)**: `bun run check:all`'s `check:file-size` step reports the CURRENT ceiling violation regardless of staging, but the companion tracker-ref oracle (`checkDiffTrackerRef`) only validates against `git diff --cached scripts/file-size-budget.json` -- so always `git add` the budget-file edit (with the new sentence + tracker ref prepended to `_ref`) BEFORE the final `bun run check:all` run in a story, not after; running the gate against an unstaged budget bump reports the same failure as not having bumped it at all.

- **Layer B artifact-of-record pattern (US-004, CAM-116, 2026-07-01)**: the reviewer agent writes TWO ephemeral exit files, not one. (1) `scripts/cam/review-report.json` (REVIEW_REPORT_FILENAME): structured findings + verdict, primary machine signal. (2) `scripts/cam/review-artifact.txt` (REVIEW_ARTIFACT_FILENAME): Layer B behavioral gate capture-pane output, artifact-of-record for the independent re-run. Both are gitignored in `.gitignore` AND `templates/.gitignore`. Both constants live in `src/supervisor/review-report.ts`. The `ReviewReport` type carries an optional `artifactOfRecord?: string` field (backward compatible: parsers that pre-date this field still parse). When widening a "single permitted write" exception in an agent prompt to cover a second file, update BOTH the Constraints clause AND the Exit-report clause -- missing either one leaves the prompt self-contradictory.

- **Merge-watch null-state GC discriminator pattern (US-001, CAM-161, 2026-07-02)**: when a null state from readMergeWatchState may be caused by a valid pre-PR issueId-only seed (not just garbage), extract the GC decision into an exported helper (e.g. `gcMergeWatchIfGarbage` in `sidecar.ts`). The helper distinguishes a valid seed (JSON object + `issueId` string + no numeric `prNumber`) from real garbage (malformed JSON, array, or object with neither field). Exporting the helper enables anti-shadow-mock real-fs regression tests that call the actual production GC path against a tmpdir file. The discriminator lives on the reader/GC side, not on the writer side, so it survives future rewrites of who writes the seed.

- **Recycle-watcher PID resolution: ps ppid-walk is canonical (US-001, CAM-165, 2026-07-03; supersedes CAM-162/CAM-173 pgrep entries)**: to terminate the orchestrator claude process on a recycle signal, resolve the child pid via `ps -ax -o pid=,ppid=` filtered by ppid==wrapperPid (kernel proc table). The signal is SIGTERM (not SIGKILL). History: CAM-162 used `pgrep -f <uuid>` (broken: argv truncation on macOS). CAM-173 switched to `pgrep -P <wrapper_pid>` (still broken: claude 2.1.200 rewrites its own process.title, leaving KERN_PROCARGS2 in a state where pgrep skips the process in ALL modes on macOS -- verified empirically ~10x). CAM-165 switches to `ps -ax -o pid=,ppid=`: ps reports the process via the kernel ppid field, immune to title rewriting. Pattern: `readWrapperPid(pidFilePath)` reads the ORCH_PID_MARKER, `resolveChildViaPs(wrapperPid)` runs ps and filters by ppid. For >1 children: select the highest pid (newest) and emit an `ambiguous-children` event via `emitEventFn` injectable. Seams: `PsSpawnFn = () => { status; stdout }` (no pid arg, since ps lists ALL processes and TS does the filtering). All three binaries (pgrep, ps, lsof) must be in `ignoreBinaries` in knip.json. Consume-once invariant: remove ORCH_RECYCLE_MARKER after firing SIGTERM even when pid resolution fails. Never reintroduce pgrep as a fallback -- ps strictly dominates. Integration test must use a title-rewriting bun stand-in (not plain `sleep`) to avoid the false-confidence trap.

- **bun test non-TTY output format: (fail) name [Nms], not ✗ (US-R1-002, CAM-186, 2026-07-05)**: in a non-interactive capture path (e.g., docker exec without a TTY), `bun test` emits failing test names as `(fail) <name>` without CLAUDECODE=1 and `(fail) <name> [Nms]` with CLAUDECODE=1 (AI agent mode). The `✗` (U+2717 ballot X) glyph is TTY-only and never appears in piped/non-TTY output. `(todo)` tests use the same `(todo) <name> [Nms]` format. The summary lines (`N pass / N fail / N skip`) are unchanged across modes. Correct regex for name extraction: `^\((fail|todo)\)\s+(.+?)(?:\s+\[\d+.*ms\])?\s*$` (group 2 = test name, strip optional `[Nms]` suffix). Do NOT use a regex matching `✗` for non-TTY parsing; it will produce empty `failingTests` arrays in production while appearing to work against manually crafted fixtures.

- **Git credential helper in Dockerfile: avoid .ssh/.git-credentials literals in comments (US-003, CAM-150, 2026-07-01)**: when adding a git credential helper to a Dockerfile, comments that mention `.ssh` or `.git-credentials` (even to say they are NOT used) will trip any `grep -Eq '\.ssh|\.git-credentials'` oracle that scans the Dockerfile. Fix: rephrase comments using "SSH key mount" and "host cred file" rather than the literal strings. The correct helper pattern for GitHub HTTPS: echo the helper script file via `echo '#!/bin/sh'` + echo statements (not printf with escapes, to avoid Dockerfile quoting complexity), then `git config --file /home/bun/.gitconfig credential.https://github.com.helper <path>` and `chown bun:bun /home/bun/.gitconfig`. Use `credential.https://github.com.helper` (scoped to host) not `credential.helper` (global). The helper outputs `username=x-access-token` and `printf "password=%s\n" "${GITHUB_TOKEN}"` (shell variable expanded at runtime, not at build time). File-assert tests that mirror grep oracles: read the files with `readFileSync` and use `toContain`/`not.toMatch` assertions matching the oracle patterns exactly.

- **Fast-track guardrail + WSJF inheritance contract (US-002, CAM-144, 2026-06-30)**: `--fast-track` (specSource:'operator') and `--derived-from` (specSource:'derived') are MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE flags -- using both is a parse error. Both cause `createLocalIssueOnMain` to run 4 hard pre-commit guardrails (specSource-required, derivedFrom-when-derived, description-non-empty, WSJF-resolvable) via `applyFastTrackGuardrails` before any mutation. WSJF resolution order: (1) explicit `wsjf` in stdin JSON wins (operator override); (2) for `--derived-from` with omitted wsjf, inherit from the first `derivedFrom` parent that has a wsjf field (suggested default); (3) neither resolvable -- refuse with non-zero exit and a clear error. This is the "SUGGESTED but overridable" contract from Gotcha #2 (CAM-143 footgun): a derived fix can legitimately be MORE time-critical than its parent, so the inherited value is always a default the operator can beat. Normal filing (no flags) bypasses ALL guardrails and files at stage:'idea'. Fast-track always files at stage:'specified'. Extract the multi-branch guardrail + inheritance block into a named helper (`applyFastTrackGuardrails`) to keep the main function under biome's complexity limit of 15.

- **pgrep last-line = child process; first-line = parent bash wrapper (US-004, CAM-162, 2026-07-02)**: when `pgrep -f <uuid>` is used to find the orchestrator claude PID, it returns MULTIPLE PIDs because the bash respawn-wrapper (`bash -c 'sid=<uuid>; ...'`) also embeds the UUID in its -c argument. pgrep lists PIDs in ascending order; the bash wrapper has a LOWER PID (started first) than the claude child process (started later). Taking the FIRST line (lowest PID) would SIGTERM the bash wrapper: bash exits without running the handoff-and-respawn logic, breaking the recycle mechanism. Always take the LAST line (highest PID) to target the child claude process. When adding new pgrep-based PID resolution, add a comment explaining the parent/child ordering and why the last line is correct. This is the fix in `makeResolvePidFn` (src/commands/orch-recycle-watch.ts): `lines[lines.length - 1]` not `lines[0]`.

- **Behavioral gate: exit-code marker pattern for tmux command completion (US-002, CAM-116, 2026-07-01)**: to detect when a command sent via `tmux send-keys` has finished inside a captured pane, append `; echo "MARKER_$?"` to the send-keys payload. Poll `tmux capture-pane -p` in a tight loop (e.g. 300ms ticks) until the marker pattern matches (`/MARKER_(\d+)/`); the captured group is the exit code. This avoids needing a PTY allocation or a shell escape mechanism. Extract the poll loop into a named helper (`pollForExitCode`) to keep the caller under biome's complexity limit of 15. The marker string must not contain tmux-special characters; plain ASCII uppercase identifiers (e.g. `CAMGATE`) are safe. Default socket for any new gate harness should be a private name (e.g. `'cam-behavioral-gate'`), never bare `'cam'`, to avoid touching the live orchestrator session.

- **makeSetPhaseFn generalized phase writer + makeFlipActiveFn delegation (US-003, CAM-151, 2026-07-01)**: when a `flipActiveFn: () => void` seam needs to become phase-aware, create `export function makeSetPhaseFn(claudeDir, cwd): (phase: LoopPhase) => void` that reads the existing state file, builds `renderStateFile({ ..., phase })`, and writes it back with `writeStateFile(cwd, body, { force: true })`; then have `makeFlipActiveFn` delegate to `makeSetPhaseFn(...)(implementing)` so the existing `RunSidecarLoopOptions.flipActiveFn: () => void` interface is unchanged (auto-chain callers need no update). Downstream consumers that need phase control (e.g. `runPostAuditAction`) receive the generalized `(phase: LoopPhase) => void` shape. Non-fatal catch block in both functions: sidecar continues if FS write fails. Export `makeSetPhaseFn` so tests can assert AC2 production behavior (phase:implementing -> active derives to true) against a temp dir.

- **React duplicate-key warnings: dynamic array renders only, never static siblings (US-R1-001, CAM-129, 2026-06-30)**: React's "Encountered two children with the same key" warning fires ONLY on dynamic array renders (`.map()`, keyed arrays) where two siblings share the same key string. Static JSX siblings listed directly in JSX (not from `.map()`) are reconciled positionally and can NEVER trigger this warning, regardless of whether explicit `key` props are present or absent. Corollary: the `key="cam-run"` / `key="cam-plan"` / `key="cam-help"` props on `NextCommand` siblings in InitScreen/SetupScreen are no-ops for warning suppression (harmless but unnecessary). The screens are clean because the only dynamic render (`checks.map((c) => <CheckRow key={c.id} />)`) uses unique check ids by design. Regression guard: spy on `console.error` in `beforeAll`, restore in `afterAll`, assert no captured error contains "same key" or "Encountered two children" -- this correctly guards against future dynamic renders introducing duplicate key values.

- **Non-interactive init: mandatory answer and readline EOF rule (US-004, CAM-104, 2026-06-26)**: two invariants must hold for every new mandatory cam init answer. (1) Mandatory answer: pair a CLI flag (e.g. `--merge-mode`) with a build-release smoke update that passes the flag; without the flag, the smoke hits an unanswered prompt and hangs (2026-06-06 "build smoke nunca trava" lesson in lessons.archive.md, CAM-15 lineage). (2) Readline EOF: every `ask`/`askChoice` built on readline MUST resolve to a default on the readline 'close' event (EOF, piped /dev/null, CI, closed stdin), or the Promise never settles and non-interactive callers hang. Pattern: register `rl.on('close', () => { if (!answered) resolve(defaultValue); })` before `rl.question(...)`.

- **oven/bun Docker image does NOT include Node.js or npm (US-R1-001, CAM-111, 2026-07-01)**: `oven/bun:1.2-slim` (and all `oven/bun` variants) ships ONLY the bun runtime. It has no `node`, no `npm`, and no `npm` shim binary. The comment "bun and node are provided by the base image" that appeared in the CAM-111 Dockerfile was factually wrong. Fix: install `nodejs` and `npm` via `apt-get install -y nodejs npm` alongside git. The `imbios/bun-node` image exists specifically to bundle bun+node together (alternative base image if Node.js is needed). Verified by reviewer finding + Docker Hub docs. Do not assume bun provides an npm shim: `bun install -g` works but installs into `~/.bun/bin`, not `/usr/local/bin`, requiring PATH setup.

- **import.meta.main guard for testable script CLI entrypoints (US-005, CAM-106, 2026-06-26)**: when a script in `scripts/` exports a pure function AND has a CLI entrypoint (reads/writes files via `Bun.file`), gate the CLI block with `if (import.meta.main) { ... }`. Without this guard, the top-level CLI code (including `await Bun.file(...).json()`) runs when the file is imported by a test, crashing or causing side-effects. Bun sets `import.meta.main = true` only when the file is the entry point of `bun <file>`, not when imported as a module. This is the canonical pattern for scripts that are both importable pure functions AND runnable CLI tools.

- **issues.schema.json describes a single IssueEntry, not the array wrapper (US-001, CAM-106, 2026-06-26)**: The AC oracle accesses `s.properties.stage.enum` at the top level of the parsed schema object. This means the schema root must be the IssueEntry shape (with `stage`, `status`, etc. as direct `properties`), NOT the old file-level wrapper (an object with a counter field and an `issues` array). If the schema wrapped the array, `s.properties.stage` would be `undefined` and the oracle would throw. The WsjfScore is a nested object under `wsjf`. Both the templates copy (`templates/scripts/cam/issues.schema.json`) and the dogfood copy (`scripts/cam/issues.schema.json`) must be byte-identical (enforced by `diff`).

- **Epico A issue schema + gate-as-code invariant (US-007, CAM-106, 2026-06-26)**: `IssueEntry` (src/issues/types.ts) gains `stage` (idea | specified | planned | shipped), `status` (open | abandoned), `blockedBy[]`, `wsjf{value,timeCriticality,riskReduction,jobSize}`, `rank` (derived), and `spec`. The gate-as-code invariant: `selectPlannableIssue` in `src/issues/select.ts` filters `stage === 'specified'` and `status === 'open'` (blocked is computed from the graph via src/issues/graph.ts, never stored as status); sort order is rank ascending (lower rank = higher priority), then id ascending (rank/id sort fallback until Epico C brings full topo-sort). Three-copy cam-plan sync requirement: any edit to `.claude/commands/cam-plan.md` MUST be mirrored identically to `templates/commands/cam-plan.md` and regenerated via `bun run embed-vendor`; drift fails `bun run embed-vendor:check`. Schema canonical copies: `scripts/cam/issues.schema.json` (dogfood) and `templates/scripts/cam/issues.schema.json` (distributed); both must be byte-identical.

- **Vendoring skills (US-001, CAM-107, 2026-06-27)**: to vendor a new skill chain, (1) download verbatim from source (use `curl <raw-url>` or `gh api ... | base64 -d` for exact bytes, never WebFetch or manual copy), (2) place under `.claude/skills/<skill-name>/` and copy byte-identically to `templates/skills/<skill-name>/` via `cp`, (3) run `bun run embed-vendor` (walkFiles walks `templates/` recursively -- no per-file map edit needed), (4) verify with `bun run embed-vendor:check` and `diff -r .claude/skills templates/skills`. Skill `.md` files are consumed by LLMs so the no-em-dash rule does NOT apply: copy all punctuation verbatim. See `scripts/cam/patterns.md` "Agent-prompt self-hosting pattern" for agents/commands (same invariant).

- **Backlog mutation guard order: validate inputs before reading the backlog (US-003, CAM-107, 2026-06-27)**: for any `*OnMain` writer that writes to `scripts/cam/issues/<PREFIX>-NNNN.json`, run validation guards in this order: (0) up-to-date guard (detached-head, missing-main, diverged); (1) validate pure inputs (spec, wsjf, etc.) before the `git ls-tree`/`git cat-file --batch` read; (2) read backlog; (3) check target state (exists, stage, status); (4) mutate in-memory; (5) checkReferentialIntegrity on the post-mutation backlog; (6) serialize + commit-tree. Validating inputs before reading the backlog is a "fail fast" pattern that avoids a git network call on bad inputs. The discriminated union for errors should be typed as `{ ok: false; reason: 'x' } | { ok: false; reason: 'y'; errors: string[] }` -- union members without `errors` must NOT have it; callers narrow with `'errors' in result` before accessing it.

- **Writer-owned event emission with default file sink (US-007, CAM-107, 2026-06-27)**: when a writer (e.g. specifyIssueOnMain) that is operator-invoked (not sidecar-driven) needs to emit a structured observability event, add `eventSink?: WorkerEventLogger` to its options and default to `makeFileEventLogger(join(cwd, '.claude/cam-worker-events.jsonl'))` inside the function (not in the caller). This differs from the supervisor dep-builder pattern (CAM-89): the writer itself wires the default, so the production path is always correct even when no DI container wires it. Anti-pattern: a test that ONLY exercises the injected sink proves nothing about production (CAM-53/89). Required: a production-wiring test that omits `eventSink` entirely and asserts the file was written at the expected path -- this test fails if the default wire is absent. Unit tests that use fake cwd (e.g. `/fake/cwd`) must supply a no-op `eventSink: () => {}` (or set it as the default in the test helper) so the file logger does not crash on a read-only path. Real-git integration tests similarly inject a no-op to keep tmpdir working trees clean.

- **Best-effort external client: injectable sendFn pattern (US-007, CAM-109, 2026-06-27)**: when wrapping a third-party SDK call that must never crash the pipeline, (1) define an injectable `sendFn?: () => Promise<{data,error}>` type that mirrors the SDK surface, (2) wrap the entire call (including the `await`) in `try-catch`, (3) also check `result.error !== null` after the await (API errors surface in `error`, not as throws), (4) on any failure: log to stderr and return `{ sent: false, error: msg }` never re-throw. The injectable seam lets tests mock without a network hit (no `requires:operator`). The escalateFn in loop.ts follows the same best-effort wrapper: `void (async () => { try { await escalateFn(); } catch (e) { stderr.write(...); } })()` so rejections are swallowed and the pipeline always reaches the terminal return. New mandatory setup/config answers (planApproval here) MUST add a CLI flag (`--plan-approval`) AND add `planApproval` to any existing test that calls `runSetup` without the flag, or the readline hangs on test stdin (CAM-104 "Non-interactive init" rule applies transitively to all callers).

- **Safety-surface-is-TS invariant: binary branch decisions must be deterministic helpers, not markdown prose (US-004, CAM-109, lineage CAM-49/72/86/104, 2026-06-27)**: any plan-time decision that gates a human ceremony (operator pause vs auto proceed) must live in a pure TypeScript function (zero I/O, discriminated-union return), never in LLM-interpreted markdown. The markdown references the helper by path but never reimplements the logic. Pattern: export `decideXxx(input: ConfigType): { kind: 'a' } | { kind: 'b' }` from `src/plan/*.ts`, mirror the style of `decideNextAction` in `src/supervisor/decide.ts`. The test file asserts that the helper is invoked only on the post-APPROVE path (BLOCK loop precedes the call) to prove ordering. File-assert oracle: `grep -n 'do NOT branch' cam-plan.md` line number must be less than `grep -n 'decidePostAuditAction\|plan_approval' cam-plan.md` line number in both cam-plan.md copies.

- **Non-convergence hard terminal pattern (US-006, CAM-109, 2026-06-27)**: when review hits maxRounds without CLEAN, the loop must promote `prd.review.lastVerdict` to `'MAX_ROUNDS_DEBT'` and RETURN immediately (not `continue`). The check lives in the review branch of `runSupervisor` AFTER `writePrd(updatedPrd)` (the re-read after reviewDispatch) and BEFORE the `notifyOrchestrator` verdict-line call. Two writePrd calls happen: (1) the original re-read, (2) the promotion. Returning immediately (not `continue`) is load-bearing: if you `continue`, `decideNextAction` sees the passes:false fix stories the reviewer added and dispatches a (maxRounds+1)-th fix. Terminal status is `'complete'` (the sidecar has done all it can; the DEBT is captured in the verdict, not the status). `DEFAULT_MAX_ROUNDS` is exported from `decide.ts` so loop.ts can reference it without duplicating the constant. The oracle test (AC3) proves the seam behaviorally: status=='complete', lastVerdict=='MAX_ROUNDS_DEBT', prdCall==2 (no 3rd read).

- **Auto-chain vs non-convergence terminal dual-interaction fix (US-008, CAM-109, 2026-06-27)**: the US-005 auto-chain (`flipActiveFn` in sidecar outer loop) defeats the US-006 MAX_ROUNDS_DEBT terminal if orphan fix stories created at `newRound==maxRounds` leave `passes:false` entries in prd.json. `makeHasPendingStories` sees them and returns true, triggering a re-run. Three-layer defence: (1) PRIMARY: `review.ts` change `newRound > maxRounds` to `newRound >= maxRounds` so the terminal round skips `buildFixStories` entirely and sets MAX_ROUNDS_DEBT directly (no orphans). (2) SIDECAR guard: in `makeHasPendingStories` (sidecar.ts), add `if (currentVerdict === 'MAX_ROUNDS_DEBT') return false;` BEFORE Case (a) so any orphan story is ignored. (3) DECIDE guard: in `decideNextAction` (decide.ts), resolve review state before Case 1 and add `!verdictIsMaxDebt` to the implement gate so orphan stories cannot route back to implement. CLEAN is NOT guarded by layers 2/3: CLEAN + pending stories is a valid scenario (operator added work after a clean review) and must still route to implement. Only MAX_ROUNDS_DEBT means "the pipeline is exhausted". Gotcha: `newRound > maxRounds` fires on the round AFTER maxRounds; the offending fix-story creation happens AT `newRound==maxRounds` (the `<=` path), so the terminal-round skip must guard `newRound>=maxRounds`, not just `>maxRounds`.

- **cam journal append: deterministic housekeeping channel (US-003, CAM-122, 2026-06-27)**: `cam journal append` is the gated write channel for structured cycle entries into `scripts/cam/journal.md`. The orchestrator (read-only LLM pane) cannot write files directly; it pipes a JSON object to `cam journal append`, which validates, normalises, and commits to main via the commit-tree-to-main primitive (same as `cam issue --file-local`). Design constraints: (1) orchestrator-authored content means all content validation runs server-side in TS (required fields, em-dash normalisation, duplicate rejection); (2) command-guaranteed format means the journal entry format is enforced by `renderJournalBlock` in `src/commands/journal.ts`, never left to the LLM; (3) gated write channel means the LLM never holds a write token -- it is always a producer of structured JSON, and `cam journal append` is the sole committer. This pattern is the canonical model for any future housekeeping channel where the orchestrator must write structured state without holding direct file-system write access.

- **Kahn residualIds exposure pattern (US-002, CAM-108, 2026-06-28)**: `rankIssues` in `src/issues/rank.ts` exposes `residualIds: string[]` in its return shape -- the set of ids with in-degree > 0 after Kahn exhaustion (cycle members). The pure ranking function does NOT throw on a cycle; it silently excludes the cycle members from the ranked list and surfaces them in `residualIds` so the caller (US-003 gate) can name the offending ids without re-running Kahn. Design rule: pure ranking functions must never crash; surface structural problems as typed fields in the result union. The WSJF-0 + warnings-array pattern follows the same principle: `warnings: string[]` carries human-readable explanations for each degraded value, never console.log from inside the pure fn. Any future topo-sort variant should mirror both conventions.

- **Shared on-main commit-tree module (US-001, CAM-108, 2026-06-28; updated US-003, CAM-133)**: `src/git/on-main.ts` is the single home for the on-main git plumbing (`buildIndexEnv`, `commitTreeToMain`) shared by all on-main backlog writers (issue-file, issue-specify, journal, triage). The two parameters that vary per caller are: `fileGitRelPath` (e.g. `'scripts/cam/issues/CAM-0042.json'` or `'scripts/cam/journal.md'`) and `tmpPrefix` (e.g. `'cam-issue-'`, `'cam-specify-'`, `'cam-journal-'`). `SpawnFn` is exported from the module and re-exported by each caller via `import type { SpawnFn }` + `export type { SpawnFn }` so consumer import paths remain stable. Each caller keeps its own `checkMainUpToDate` and `pushMainBestEffort` (not moved to shared module). Adding a new on-main writer: import `commitTreeToMain` and `type SpawnFn` from `'../git/on-main.ts'`; pass the file-relative git path and a unique tmp prefix; the jscpd dup gate (threshold 6%, scans `src scripts`) will flag any new verbatim copy. NOTE: `commitOnMain` was removed in US-003/CAM-133 -- it is no longer exported.

- **Graph gate three-phase pattern (US-003, CAM-108, 2026-06-28)**: `src/issues/gate.ts` exports `runGraphGate(backlog: IssueEntry[]): GateResult` with shape `{ ok: true; warnings: string[] } | { ok: false; kind: 'cycle'|'integrity'; errors: string[]; warnings: string[] }`. Three phases in order: (1) `checkReferentialIntegrity` from graph.ts -- hard fail on missing-id or self-reference; (2) cross-stage blocker scan -- warn-only for a universe issue blocked by an id that is present-in-backlog but not specified+open and not shipped; (3) `rankIssues` from rank.ts -- hard fail when `residualIds.length > 0` (Kahn cycle). Cycle path reconstruction walks successor edges (blocker -> blocked) within the residual set until the starting node is revisited, yielding "CAM-X -> CAM-Y -> CAM-X". The gate is a pure check (no I/O, no writes); the transactional no-write guarantee is enforced by the caller (US-004) which must not commit rank when `result.ok === false`. Precedence: integrity beats cycle (checked first); cross-stage warnings accumulate even when the gate subsequently hard-fails on a cycle.

- **git cat-file --batch framing parser (US-002, CAM-90, 2026-06-28)**: read all blobs from main in O(1) subprocesses using `git ls-tree -r --name-only main <dir>` (enumerate) + `git cat-file --batch` with stdin `main:<path>` per line (bulk read). Frame format per object: `<oid> SP <type> SP <size> LF <contents> LF`. Parse: `indexOf('\n')` for header, split on space, check `parts[1] === 'blob'`, read exactly `parseInt(parts[2])` chars, skip trailing `\n` (`pos += size + 1`). "Missing" entries emit `<name> missing` (2 parts), which fails the `parts.length < 3` guard and are silently skipped. Return `[]` when ls-tree output is empty (skip cat-file entirely). Injectable `BacklogSpawnFn` (exported from `src/issues/backlog.ts`) supports `input?: string` for the stdin pipe. Do NOT import from `src/git/on-main.ts` in read-only primitives (that module is for on-main writers only per its own comment).

- **allocateId re-derive-inside-CAS pattern (US-003, CAM-90, 2026-06-28)**: `allocateId` (exported from `src/issues/backlog.ts`) derives `max(parsed ids on main) + 1` fresh each call (no stored counter; returns 1 for empty dir). It MUST be called INSIDE the CAS retry rebuild closure -- not before the loop -- so a CAS loser re-reads from the now-advanced main ref and gets a distinct id instead of colliding with the winner. `writeIssueFile` (in `src/issues/alloc.ts`) implements this: each retry calls `allocateId` first, builds content with the newly allocated id, then attempts `update-ref` CAS. SpawnFn adapter: when a writer function accepts `SpawnFn` (wider, with `env?`) and needs to pass it to `allocateId` (accepts `BacklogSpawnFn`, without `env`), use a one-liner lambda `const readSpawn: BacklogSpawnFn = (cmd, args, o) => spawnFn(cmd, args, o)` -- passing `{ encoding, input? }` (narrower) to `spawnFn` which accepts `{ encoding, env?, input? }` (wider) is safe because `env` is optional. Filename convention: `CAM-${String(n).padStart(4,'0')}.json` (4-digit zero-padded); the `id` field INSIDE the JSON stays unpadded (`CAM-90`, never `CAM-0090`). For N >= 1000, `padStart(4,'0')` is a no-op (already 4+ digits), so both forms are valid filenames.

- **Multi-file atomic on-main commit + CAS update-ref (US-001, CAM-90, 2026-06-28)**: `commitTreeToMain` in `src/git/on-main.ts` accepts `files: FileWrite[]` (list of `{path, content}` pairs). All files are hashed and indexed into ONE temp index before `write-tree`; if any `hash-object` or `update-index` call fails (non-zero exit), the function throws before `write-tree` so none land (atomicity). The `update-ref` step uses CAS: `git update-ref refs/heads/main <new> <old-expected>` -- on non-zero exit (CAS mismatch) the helper re-reads `git rev-parse main`, rebuilds the tree on the new base, and retries up to `CAS_MAX_ATTEMPTS` times (default 3) before throwing. The 1-element list is the single-file case and produces the same read-tree/hash-object/update-index/write-tree/commit-tree/update-ref sequence as the old API (parity guarantee). Callers always wrap their single `(serialized, fileGitRelPath)` pair into a 1-element list; the jscpd gate ensures no verbatim copy re-emerges.

- **commitTreeToMain file-deletion via optional removals (US-006, CAM-90, 2026-06-28)**: `commitTreeToMain` accepts an optional last parameter `removals?: string[]` (repo-relative paths to remove from the tree in the same atomic commit). Removals are applied via `git update-index --force-remove <path>` (using the temp index env) AFTER `read-tree` and BEFORE the file writes. Must use `--force-remove` not `--remove`: see the US-010 pattern below for why. This places the removed file's absence in the same tree object as the new blobs, so the commit atomically deletes and adds files in one operation. This is the pattern used by `migrateIssuesToDir` (in `scripts/cam/migrate-issues-schema.ts`) to delete the monolithic backlog file and write N per-issue files in one commit. All existing callers omit `removals` (it is optional) so backward compat is preserved. Idempotency guard: check `git show main:<path>` first; if exit != 0 the file is already absent, return no-op without creating any commit.

- **On-branch migration vs commit-tree-to-main (US-007, CAM-90, 2026-06-28)**: `commitTreeToMain` is designed for multi-writer atomicity when the feature branch must NOT be checked out (e.g. `cam issue --file-local` writes to main while staying on a feature branch). For on-branch working-tree migrations (Option B in CAM-90), use the simpler approach: read the source file directly from the working tree (not via `git show main:`), write new files with `writeFileSync`/`Bun.write`, delete the source file with `rm`/`unlinkSync`, then commit normally with `git add`/`git rm`/`git commit`. Do NOT use `commitTreeToMain` for on-branch working-tree work: it targets `refs/heads/main` via `update-ref`, which is wrong when HEAD is already on main (CAS would compare the same ref it is advancing). Rule: use `commitTreeToMain` when you need to write to a branch you are NOT currently on; use the working tree + normal commit when you ARE on that branch.

- **on-main.ts CAS helpers exported for callers (US-009, CAM-90, 2026-06-28)**: `src/git/on-main.ts` exports three fine-grained helpers extracted from `commitTreeToMain` for callers that run their own CAS loop: `removePathsFromIndex(cwd, paths, spawnFn, indexEnv)`, `hashAndIndexFiles(cwd, files, spawnFn, indexEnv)` (throws on failure for atomicity), and `commitAndCasAttempt(cwd, currentMainSha, commitMsg, spawnFn, indexEnv)` returning `{ success: true; shortSha }` or `{ success: false; newMainSha }`. `writeIssueFile` (alloc.ts) uses these instead of duplicating the plumbing. Any future on-main writer with its own CAS loop should import these three helpers rather than re-implementing the git plumbing steps.

- **git cat-file --batch size is BYTES, not characters: decode per-blob (US-R1-001, CAM-90, 2026-06-28)**: `git cat-file --batch` reports `<size>` as the UTF-8 BYTE length of each blob. When the git output is decoded as a JS string (via `encoding: 'utf8'` in `spawnSync`), one multi-byte code point (e.g. "ç" U+00E7 = 2 bytes in UTF-8) occupies 1 JS character. Slicing the decoded string by `<size>` (a byte count) over-reads by one character per multi-byte code point in the blob; `pos += size + 1` then desyncs the frame parser for every subsequent entry. Fix: after getting the UTF-8-decoded string from spawnSync, convert it back to a Buffer with `Buffer.from(str, 'utf8')`, then use `Buffer.indexOf(0x0A)` for newline scanning and `Buffer.subarray(pos, pos + size).toString('utf8')` for per-blob decoding. The round-trip is lossless for valid UTF-8 JSON. Add both a unit fixture with accented content AND a real-git integration test to catch regressions (ASCII-only test content hides the defect because `byteLength === charLength` for ASCII). Empirical impact on the real 129-issue repo: 38 entries silently dropped (first parsed id was CAM-25, not CAM-1).

- **git update-index: use --force-remove, NOT --remove, in off-main commit-tree plumbing (US-010, CAM-90, 2026-06-28)**: `git update-index --remove <path>` only deletes a path from the index when the file is ABSENT from the working tree. If the file still exists in the working tree (the common case when running off-main plumbing while checked out on main), `--remove` is silently a no-op (exits 0, removes nothing). Use `--force-remove` to delete unconditionally from the index regardless of working-tree state. Unit fakes return status 0 for any `update-index` call and cannot catch this class of bug. Only a real-git integration test (using a real tmpdir repo and real spawnSync) proves the deletion actually takes effect. Confirmed by checking `git ls-files` before and after: `--remove` leaves the path in the index; `--force-remove` removes it. Applied to `removePathsFromIndex` in `src/git/on-main.ts` and confirmed by `test/integration/issue-dir-real-git.test.ts` AC#1 tests.

- **recordCycleTokens cycle-close token accounting (US-001, CAM-131, 2026-06-28)**: `recordCycleTokens` in `src/commands/journal.ts` emits a durable `'cycle-tokens'` WorkerEvent at cycle-close time. `orchTokens` = input + cacheCreation + cacheRead (NOT output) from the orchestrator transcript resolved by `orchestratorTranscriptPath`; `workerTokens` = sum of all four fields (inputTokens + outputTokens + cacheReadTokens + cacheCreationTokens) from worker `'tokens'` events after the last `'cycle-tokens'` marker in the event log (per-cycle slice). `CycleTokensEventDetail` lives in `src/supervisor/events.ts`. All three seams are injectable: `readOrchTranscript`, `readEventLog`, `logEvent`. Absent transcript = orchTokens:0 (graceful, no error). First cycle (no prior marker) = all tokens events counted.

- **jq read-before-rename for consume-on-read files (US-003, CAM-131, 2026-06-28)**: when a bash wrapper must inspect a JSON file's content AND then rename/consume that file (mv to .consumed), read the field with jq BEFORE the mv so the value is in a shell variable when the file is gone. Idiom: `reason=$(jq -r '.reason // empty' '/path/to/file.json'); mv '/path/to/file.json' '/path/to/file.consumed.json'`. The `// empty` alternative operator returns nothing when `.reason` is null or absent, so `$reason` is an empty string (not the literal "null") in the subsequent `[ "$reason" = 'cycle-close' ]` test. The `-r` flag strips JSON string quotes. Applied to the orch self-handoff wrapper in `buildOrchestratorPaneCommand` (src/commands/run.ts) to distinguish cycle-close handoffs (reset n=0) from no-progress handoffs (increment n toward cap).

- **Fail-closed jq-absence guard in shell hooks (US-001, CAM-96, 2026-06-29)**: Claude Code treats any non-zero exit code other than 2 as a non-blocking error (spawn proceeds). So a hook that calls jq without guarding against jq-absence exits 127 on missing jq, and the spawn is allowed (fail-open). Fix: immediately after reading stdin into $payload but before the first jq call, add `if ! command -v jq >/dev/null 2>&1; then printf '<static deny JSON>'; exit 0; fi`. The static JSON is emitted via printf (not jq) so it is available even when jq is absent. Deny contract: exit 0 + `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PreToolUse","permissionDecision":"deny","permissionDecisionReason":"..."}}`. Test pattern: simulate jq-absence by symlinking only cat into a temp bin dir and spawning the hook via absolute /bin/bash path with that restricted PATH as the env.

- **runMetaLoopObserveFn prd-in-flight gate (US-004, CAM-132, 2026-06-29)**: `runMetaLoopObserveFn` in `RunSidecarLoopOptions` is called in the `active!==true` idle branch ONLY when `hasPendingStories()` returns false (prd.json absent or cycle complete). This double gate (active!==true AND !hasPendingStories) ensures the observer stays silent during a paused-mid-cycle state (active:false but pending stories exist). When absent (meta_loop=off), the path is a strict no-op (zero behavior change). The dedup state for the observe fn lives in a closure returned by `makeProductionMetaLoopObserveFn` (NOT persisted to disk). Extracting escalateFn production wiring to `makeProductionEscalateFn` was needed to keep `buildSidecarLoopDeps` under the biome noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity(max=15) limit after adding the new `??` + ternary for the observe fn wiring.

- **Durable merge-watch state helpers (US-002, CAM-138, 2026-06-29)**: `readMergeWatchState(filePath)`, `writeMergeWatchState(filePath, state)`, and `removeMergeWatchState(filePath)` are exported from `src/release/merge-watch.ts`. `readMergeWatchState` returns null for missing/malformed/non-object files and handles legacy 2-field seeds by omitting `pollCount`/`lastPolledAt` (stepMergeWatch defaults `pollCount ?? 0` and treats absent `lastPolledAt` as "poll immediately"). `writeMergeWatchState` always writes all four fields (pollCount defaults to 0; omits `lastPolledAt` when undefined so a fresh-read bypasses the throttle). `removeMergeWatchState` is best-effort (ignores ENOENT). These helpers take an explicit file path (never hard-code `.claude/`) so tests pass a tmpdir path. Tests use a real tmpdir (not mocks) as the oracle -- see the AC1-4 suite in `test/release/merge-watch.test.ts`.

- **syncWorktreeIfOnMain coherence invariant (US-001, CAM-137, 2026-06-29)**: `syncWorktreeIfOnMain(cwd, paths, spawnFn)` is the canonical post-commit worktree sync helper in `src/git/on-main.ts`. It self-detects the current branch via `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD`; if not `main`, it is a no-op. On main, it runs `git restore --staged --worktree --source=HEAD -- <paths>` (verified against git docs: default no-overlay mode removes tracked paths absent from HEAD, so add/mod/del are all handled uniformly). Best-effort: non-zero restore exits emit a `printWarning` (stdout) and never throw. `commitTreeToMain` calls it as its FINAL step after a successful CAS `update-ref`, passing `[...files.map(f => f.path), ...(removals ?? [])]` as the path scope. The returned short sha is computed before the sync and is unaffected by it. Integration tests that previously asserted "working-tree file unchanged" on the on-main path must now assert "working-tree file synced to HEAD" (the three updated tests: journal-append-on-main Case B, issue-specify Case B, issue-specify abandon Case). Off-main integration tests are unchanged: branch != main keeps the sync inert.

- **Drain notify fn: build inside observe factory to avoid extra ?? in buildSidecarLoopDeps (US-005, CAM-132, 2026-06-29)**: the drain notification closure (`makeProductionDrainNotifyFn`) is built INSIDE `makeProductionMetaLoopObserveFn` (not extracted into `buildSidecarLoopDeps` as a separate `??` expression) to keep `buildSidecarLoopDeps` under biome's `noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity` ceiling of 15. Each additional `??` expression counts as +1 complexity. The factory accepts `resendApiKey`/`resendRecipient` strings (not a pre-built fn) and calls `makeProductionDrainNotifyFn` internally. Tests inject `runMetaLoopObserveFn` directly (via `makeTestObserveFn` with an optional `drainNotifyFn` arg) without going through the production factory, so no `drainNotifyFn?` field is needed in `SidecarOptions`. Pattern: when a sub-factory has its own complexity-contributing deps, build them inside the factory rather than cascading them through the parent dep-builder.

- **Pure tick step function pattern for polling state machines (US-001, CAM-138, 2026-06-29)**: extract the blocking poll loop into a pure `stepXxx(state, now, pollFn, opts)` function that returns `{ kind: 'continue', state }` or `{ kind: 'terminal', outcome }`. The caller owns scheduling (sleep) and clock. Throttle via `lastPolledAt?: number` in the state: `state.lastPolledAt === undefined` means "first tick, poll immediately" (bypasses the throttle check); subsequent ticks check `now >= lastPolledAt + pollIntervalMs`. Timeout fires when `pollCount >= maxPolls` at the top of the tick (before calling pollFn). The legacy wrapper (`runMergeWatch`) uses a virtual clock: `virtualNow = 0` initially, increments by `pollIntervalMs` after each sleep -- so the throttle always passes post-sleep without needing a `Date.now()` call in product code. Applied to `stepMergeWatch` / `runMergeWatch` in `src/release/merge-watch.ts`.

- **Sidecar one-step-per-tick wiring for merge-watch (US-003, CAM-138, 2026-06-29)**: `makeProductionMergeWatchFn` in `src/commands/sidecar.ts` calls `stepMergeWatch` exactly once per idle tick (not `runMergeWatch` which blocks). Pattern: (1) `readMergeWatchState(watchFilePath)` -- null means absent or malformed (return early; malformed: best-effort `unlinkSync` if `existsSync` is true). (2) Build `ghPollFn` and `StepMergeWatchOptions`. (3) Call `stepMergeWatch(state, Date.now(), ghPollFn, stepOpts)`. (4) On `continue`: `writeMergeWatchState(watchFilePath, result.state)` to persist updated `pollCount`/`lastPolledAt` across sidecar restarts. (5) On `terminal`: `removeMergeWatchState(watchFilePath)` so idle ticks become no-ops. The eager pre-start `unlinkSync` ("Remove the watch file BEFORE starting") is explicitly REMOVED (it caused CAM-103 by making the watch non-restartable). The outer sidecar loop handles the 2s idle cadence; the 60s gh-poll cadence is enforced inside `stepMergeWatch` via `lastPolledAt` throttle. Because the closure returns promptly, `hasSessionFn` in the loop runs every tick even during a long CI wait.

- **Lifecycle events in step functions: emit on first invocation, not in the outer wrapper (US-R1-001, CAM-138, 2026-06-29)**: when a pure tick function (`stepXxx`) has a "started" lifecycle event (e.g. `merge-watch-watching`), emit it inside `stepXxx` on the FIRST invocation (identified by `state.pollCount === 0` or equivalent counter), NOT in the outer blocking wrapper (`runXxx`). Rationale: the outer wrapper may not be used in the production path (the sidecar calls `stepXxx` directly one-per-tick); emitting in the wrapper masks the regression because its tests stay green while the production path silently loses the event. Place the emit after the throttle+timeout guard (only when `shouldPoll` is true) so it fires when the real first poll occurs. Remove the duplicate emit from the wrapper to avoid double-emission. Tests: add one test for the step function asserting the event IS present when `pollCount===0`, and one asserting it is ABSENT when `pollCount>0`. Oracle: the tests exercising the legacy wrapper alone cannot catch this regression -- only a step-function-level test exposes it.

- **Always use commitTreeToMain unconditionally in on-main writers (US-001, CAM-133, 2026-06-28)**: the `branchWasMain ? commitOnMain(...) : commitTreeToMain(...)` ternary is a LATENT BUG for any writer that can be called after a prior ref-only write (such as `writeIssueFile` via `cam issue --file-local`). When `branchWasMain===true`, `commitOnMain` stages from the WORKING TREE INDEX; if a prior ref-only write advanced main's ref without touching the working tree, those files are absent from the index and `commitOnMain` silently drops them from the resulting commit tree. Fix: remove the ternary entirely and call `commitTreeToMain` unconditionally. `commitTreeToMain` does `git read-tree main` in a temp index (loading the FULL main tree, including all ref-only writes), so no prior file can be dropped. The `writeFile?` injectable can be left in the options interface for backward compat with callers/tests but is no longer invoked on any commit path (no working-tree write occurs). Regression test pattern: use `writeIssueFile` twice ref-only (so the working tree is stale), then call the mutating writer while on main, and assert `git ls-tree` count is preserved. Applied to `specifyIssueOnMain`, `abandonIssueOnMain`, and `mergeIssueOnMain` in `src/commands/issue-specify.ts` (US-001) and to `appendJournalEntryOnMain` (both force-replace and append call sites) in `src/commands/journal.ts` and `runTriage` in `src/commands/triage.ts` (US-002). After a commitTreeToMain on-main, `git status --porcelain` may show the index as stale relative to the advanced HEAD (expected: the ref-only commit does not update the working tree or the real index); the correct assertion is via `git show main:<path>`, not via working-tree state or git status.

- **Real-tmux pane-geometry oracle pattern (US-002, CAM-80, 2026-06-29)**: to assert that a worker pane was born from the orchestrator column (not from the active dashboard column), use `tmux display-message -p -t <paneId> '#{pane_width};#{pane_height};#{pane_left};#{pane_top}'` with a SEMICOLON separator (TAB is mangled to `_` by tmux in -F output, CAM-55 rule). Parse with `out.split(';').map(Number)` and guard each value with `?? 0`. Discriminating assertions: (a) `worker_width >= orch_width - 1` (same column), (b) `worker_width > dash_width + 5` (the core oracle: wide not narrow), (c) `worker_left === orch_left` (same x origin). Allow ±1 col tolerance for border rounding. If splitting from an explicit `-t <orchPaneId>`, the worker inherits the orch column regardless of which pane is currently active; a bare `-t <session>:0` would inherit the ACTIVE pane's dimensions instead. The test must activate a non-orchestrator pane via `select-pane -t <dashPaneId>` BEFORE calling openPaneInSession to exercise the discriminating scenario.

- **claude auth status JSON behavior and sibling-module extraction pattern (US-001, CAM-82, 2026-06-29)**: (1) `claude auth status` outputs JSON by default (no `--json` flag needed; `--text` for human-readable); exits 0 when logged in, 1 when not. JSON shape (claude 2.1.195+): `{ loggedIn, authMethod, apiProvider, email, orgId, orgName, subscriptionType }`. Gate ONLY on `loggedIn === true`; `subscriptionType` is informational. Fail-closed: treat any error path (spawn error, ENOENT via `result.error`, non-zero exit, empty stdout, unparseable JSON, wrong shape) as a fail. `spawnSync` sets `result.error` on ENOENT without throwing -- always check `result.error != null` before checking `result.status`. (2) Sibling-module extraction to stay within file-size budget: move implementation to `src/commands/run-auth-preflight.ts`, then add `export { checkClaudeAuth, type ClaudeAuthOk, ... } from './run-auth-preflight.ts'` in `run.ts`. All existing import paths continue to work via the re-export; update the budget for the new file and the modified ceiling for the original file (tracker ref required in staged diff per ratchet rule).

- **stdin raw-mode gate pattern for Ink commands (US-001, CAM-129, 2026-06-29)**: Ink's `useInput` (and `Select`) require `process.stdin` to be in raw mode (`setRawMode`). If the gate checks only `process.stdout.isTTY`, the Ink path is entered even when stdin is piped from `/dev/null` (build-smoke shape), crashing with "Raw mode is not supported on the current process.stdin". Fix: gate on BOTH `process.stdout.isTTY` AND `process.stdin.isTTY`. Pattern: extract the gate into an exported pure predicate `isXxxInteractiveGate(stdoutIsTTY, stdinIsTTY, ci)` so tests can assert the non-interactive branch is selected without touching real process streams. The production caller passes `Boolean(process.stdout.isTTY), Boolean(process.stdin.isTTY), process.env.CI`. Ink docs confirm: `useStdin().isRawModeSupported` is the Ink-native equivalent; `process.stdin.isTTY` is the correct command-level proxy (undefined/falsy in non-TTY stdin contexts). This pattern applies to ANY Ink screen that uses `useInput` or mounts input-consuming components.

- **Extend SpawnFn fakes when wiring a new preflight into a function (US-002, CAM-82, 2026-06-29)**: when a function's injectable SpawnFn acquires a new command call site (e.g., adding `checkClaudeAuth` which invokes `claude auth status --json` via the same spawnFn), ALL existing fakes for that function must be updated to handle the new command. A fake that does not handle a command returns `{ status: 0, stdout: Buffer.from('') }` silently, which can trigger incorrect behavior (empty stdout = fail-closed in `checkClaudeAuth`). Pattern: add a named option to the fake factory (e.g., `claudeAuth: 'loggedIn' | 'notLoggedIn' | 'error'`) with a safe default (`'loggedIn'`) so all prior callers work without modification. Test the new preflight with: (1) a dedicated test asserting non-zero exit and no session/sidecar creation on failure; (2) a regression test asserting zero exit and session creation on success.

- **Build-release soft-check: probe prerequisite presence before tolerating non-zero (US-003, CAM-129, 2026-06-30)**: a build soft-check that runs a command whose prerequisites may be absent should NEVER use a blanket `|| true` or a silent else. Instead, probe the missing prerequisite with `command -v <tool> >/dev/null 2>&1` in the else branch and branch explicitly: absent = tolerate + log; present + crash = fatal error + `exit ${rc}`. This makes CI failures visible when the tool IS installed (real crash) without false-failing on machines where the tool is absent. Static-source guard tests that assert a specific log string MUST be updated when the string changes: the oracle must assert the new branching logic (e.g., `command -v <tool>` and the fatal message text), not the old blanket-tolerance comment.

- **Remote prune end-state classification via ls-remote (US-001, CAM-143, 2026-06-30)**: classify remote branch deletion success by end-state (branch existence), not by the delete command's exit code. Pattern: (1) pre-check with `git ls-remote --heads origin <branch>` via spawnFn; (2) empty stdout + exit 0 means branch already absent, set pruned=true without deleting (covers ci-gated GitHub auto-delete); (3) non-zero exit from ls-remote means network/auth error, fall through to `git push origin --delete` and classify on its exit code; (4) non-empty stdout + exit 0 means branch present, run delete, then re-check with a second ls-remote and derive result from the post-delete end-state, never from the delete exit code (even if delete exits 0, re-check confirms truth). Extract as a named helper (`checkAndPruneRemoteBranch`) mirroring the `readVersionTag`/`performTagStep` pattern to keep the main function under complexity limits. Extend all fakes in tests to genuinely model `ls-remote` for both cases; a default of empty stdout + exit 0 accidentally passes the already-pruned path and hides the present-branch code path (FAKES-LIE HAZARD, CAM-82).

- **writeIssueFile worktree-sync: call syncWorktreeIfOnMain inside the CAS success block (US-001, CAM-142, 2026-06-30)**: `writeIssueFile` in `src/issues/alloc.ts` calls `syncWorktreeIfOnMain(cwd, [filename], spawnFn)` INSIDE the `if (result.success)` block, BEFORE the return. This closes the staged-deletion footgun for the 7th on-main writer: without it, a CAS update-ref advances `refs/heads/main` without touching the real index, so the new file appears as `D scripts/cam/issues/CAM-NNNN.json` (staged deletion) in `git status --porcelain` when checked out on main. Any test that previously asserted the working tree was STALE after `writeIssueFile` (e.g., `existsSync(...).toBe(false)`) must be updated to `toBe(true)`, because the sync now runs unconditionally on the main branch. The sync is a no-op on feature branches (syncWorktreeIfOnMain self-detects the branch).

- **Local prune end-state classification via rev-parse (US-002, CAM-143, 2026-06-30)**: classify local branch deletion success by end-state (branch existence), not by the delete command's exit code. Pattern mirrors the remote prune (`checkAndPruneRemoteBranch`): (1) pre-check with `git rev-parse --verify --quiet refs/heads/<branch>` via spawnFn; (2) non-zero exit or empty stdout means branch already absent, set pruned=true without deleting (covers the common case where the previous cycle's pre-flight already pruned the local branch); (3) exit 0 + non-empty stdout means branch present, run `git branch -D <branch>` (force delete), then re-check; (4) derive result from the post-delete end-state, never from the delete exit code. Use `-D` (force) not `-d` (safe-only); `-D` is safe here because runPostMerge is only ever called from the MERGED state. FAKES-LIE HAZARD: the default happy-path fake must default `revParseLocalPreStatus: 128` (absent) so pre-existing "already pruned" tests still pass without a -D call; tests that need the delete path must explicitly pass `revParseLocalPreStatus: 0`. Confirmed by git docs: `rev-parse --verify --quiet` exits non-zero + empty stdout when ref is absent.

- **Coalesce notifyOrchestrator: never send two lines with no work between them (US-003, CAM-143, 2026-06-30)**: the orchestrator Ink prompt does not absorb back-to-back `send-keys` calls: the second line races the re-render and is swallowed. Rule: `notifyOrchestrator` must not be called twice in sequence on the same logical outcome branch. Coalesce instead: refactor any secondary narration helper into a PURE BUILDER returning a string suffix (empty when nothing to add), then concatenate the suffix into the single primary call. Applied to `warnOnPruneFailure` in `src/release/merge-watch.ts` (US-003): the function now returns `string` and does NOT call `notifyOrchestrator`; the prune sub-status is folded into the `[cam] post-merge complete: ...` line. Same pattern applies to any future sub-status that must accompany an existing narration line.`

- **DockerProbe seam for container-dependent helpers (US-004, CAM-111, 2026-07-01)**: any helper that shells out to docker must inject the docker calls via a `DockerProbe` seam: `(args: string[]) => { stdout: string; exitCode: number }`. This keeps the helper testable on CI runners with no Docker installed (macos-latest has no Docker daemon). Pair with an optional `StatFn: (path: string) => { mtimeMs: number } | null` for file-system stat operations (e.g., Dockerfile mtime for stale detection). Docker idioms: `docker info` exits 0 when the daemon is reachable; `docker image inspect <tag>` exits 0 when the image exists locally; `docker image inspect <tag> --format={{.Created}}` returns the ISO-8601 creation timestamp. The `PreflightResult` discriminated union shape is `{ ready: true } | { ready: false; reason: 'daemon-unreachable' | 'image-missing' | 'image-stale' }` (mirrors the `{ ok: false; reason }` pattern from CAM-86). The helper is intentionally uncalled in production dispatch paths until Half-B wiring (CAM-150); knip marks it reachable via the test entry point.

- **Scoped NOPASSWD sudoers for devcontainer firewall init (US-R1-002, CAM-111, 2026-07-01)**: when a devcontainer's postStartCommand runs `sudo bash /workspace/.devcontainer/init-firewall.sh`, the matching sudoers entry must use the full resolved path of bash: `bun ALL=(root) NOPASSWD: /bin/bash /workspace/.devcontainer/init-firewall.sh`. `sudo` resolves the command binary to its full path before matching rules, so specifying `/bin/bash` correctly gates `sudo bash <script>`. The entry is placed in `/etc/sudoers.d/bun-firewall` (chmod 0440) using a Dockerfile RUN layer run as root (before `USER bun`). The firewall tooling required by `init-firewall.sh` must also be apt-installed in the same layer: `sudo iptables ipset dnsutils curl` (dnsutils provides `dig`; curl is required for the self-verify step). Following the Anthropic reference Dockerfile pattern (github.com/anthropics/claude-code/blob/main/.devcontainer/Dockerfile).

- **oven/bun base image: non-root user is 'bun', home is /home/bun (US-003, CAM-111, 2026-07-01)**: the `oven/bun:1.2-slim` base image ships with a pre-created `bun` user (uid 1000) with home directory `/home/bun`. Devcontainer configurations that reference this image must set `remoteUser: "bun"` and mount volumes at `/home/bun/.claude` (not `/home/node/.claude` as in the Anthropic reference container, which uses a Node.js base image). macOS BSD grep / ugrep does NOT support negative lookaheads `(?!...)` in ERE mode (`-E`); use `-P` (Perl-compat) for that syntax on macOS. The devcontainer.json format supports both string mounts (`"source=name,target=/path,type=volume"`) and object mounts (`{"type":"volume","source":"name","target":"/path"}`); the string form is simpler for named volumes.

- **Planner poll must break on report file, not only on pane death (US-R1-001, CAM-117, 2026-07-01)**: interactive TUI workers (claude sessions) never self-exit; the driver kills them via `respawn-pane -k` AFTER detecting the report file. A `pollPlannerDeath` loop structured as `while (isPaneAlive)` will spin forever on the happy path (pane stays alive until plannerTimeoutMs = 30 min, returns planner-timeout, never spawns the auditor). Fix: add `readPlannerReportFn?: () => unknown | null` to `RunPlanPhaseOptions` and check it as the PRIMARY signal in a `while(true)` loop (sleep; check file; check pane death; check timeout). The auditor spawn (`respawn-pane -k`) implicitly kills the still-alive planner pane -- no explicit kill needed when the file is detected. Mirrors the `readReviewReport` pattern from `review.ts` exactly. Test isolation trap: tests that simulate pane death after 1 tick (`plannerAliveCount=1`) mask the bug because `isPaneAlive` returns false on the second iteration and exits the loop before timeout fires. To prove the AC, add tests with `isPaneAlive: () => true` + `readPlannerReportFn` signalling completion -- the result must be `audit-approved`, not `planner-timeout`.

- **Plan pre-flight: PlanPreflightSpawnFn (bin, args) pattern + best-effort prune (US-004, CAM-117, 2026-07-01)**: `runPlanPreflight` follows the same injectable-seam pattern as DockerProbe in preflight-container.ts but with `(bin: string, args: string[]) => { stdout: string; exitCode: number }` so a single fake records ALL binary calls (git, bun, gh) in one array. Required steps halt on first failure via `(exitCode ?? 1) !== 0`; the clean-tree check halts on non-empty stdout regardless of exit code (git status --porcelain exits 0 for dirty trees). The prune step is extracted into a dedicated helper (`pruneMergedCamBranches`) and wrapped in try/catch so it NEVER halts the required chain. Skip-when-absent check uses `Bun.which('gh') !== null` (returns null when binary is not on PATH -- confirmed in Bun docs). Test the prune step with `ghAvailable: false` to assert zero gh calls, and with `ghAvailable: true` + fake for-each-ref output to assert argv shapes. `cwd` is part of `RunPlanPreflightOptions` per the API contract but is intentionally not used in the pure function body (production caller closes over it in the spawnFn).

- **ContainerSpawnFn seam for docker build+run orchestration (US-001, CAM-150, 2026-07-01)**: pure builders (`buildDockerBuildArgv`, `buildDockerRunArgv`) return string arrays with no side-effects; a separate orchestration fn (`runWorkerContainer`) drives them through an injectable `ContainerSpawnFn: (cmd: string, args: string[]) => { stdout: string; exitCode: number }`. This is distinct from `DockerProbe` (inspect-only, args without leading binary) -- `ContainerSpawnFn` includes the binary name so a single fake can record both `docker build` and `docker run` calls. Credential threading: ALWAYS use name-only `-e VARNAME` (no `=value`) for secret env vars in docker run argv; Docker reads the value from the host process.env at run time (Docker docs confirmed: unset host var = unset container var, not an error). Bun auto-loads `.env`, so the sidecar's env already contains token values. Pattern for inert substrate: mark the module file-assert-safe by NOT importing from loop.ts/plan-runner.ts (verified by oracle `! grep -q 'worker-container'` in those files).

- **dnsmasq --ipset for CDN-rotation-safe container firewall (US-004, CAM-150, 2026-07-01)**: `dnsmasq --ipset=/domain/setname` inserts resolved IPs into a named ipset dynamically on every DNS answer (subdomain matching included: `--ipset=/github.com/s` covers `*.github.com` automatically). This survives CDN IP rotation where a dig-once frozen-ipset would drop connections after IPs rotate. Setup order: (1) `ipset create <name> hash:net` (ipset MUST exist before dnsmasq starts); (2) start dnsmasq with `--listen-address=127.0.0.1 --no-resolv --no-poll --server=<upstream> --ipset=/...`; (3) `echo "nameserver 127.0.0.1" > /etc/resolv.conf` so all container DNS queries flow through dnsmasq. Use `--pid-file` for idempotent re-runs (kill old instance before re-creating). Only allowlisted domains get `--ipset` directives; unlisted domains resolve fine but their IPs never enter the ipset, so iptables `--match-set` blocks them. LFS object host is `objects.githubusercontent.com` (not `objects.github.com`). Never mention the wrong bare subdomain in comments -- grep-based oracles scan the whole file including comments.

- **Greedy leading .* to anchor regex to the LAST occurrence (US-001, CAM-116, 2026-07-01)**: when a criterion or line may contain multiple occurrences of a pattern (e.g. prose examples of `[oracle: ...]` AND the real suffix at the end), use a greedy leading `.*` before the pattern: `.*\[oracle:\s*(.*?)\s*\]\s*$`. The greedy `.*` consumes as much as possible and forces the anchored `\[oracle:` to match the LAST occurrence in the string. This is preferable to `[^\[\]]*` (character-class exclusion) when the "inner text" of intermediate occurrences may also contain no brackets (like `...` dots in prose). Confirmed in behavioral-gate.ts where criterion text can contain `[oracle: ...]` as a prose example AND the actual `[oracle: bun test]` suffix.

- **Test the operational directives, not the decorative array (US-R1-001, CAM-150, 2026-07-01)**: when a shell script has a human-readable summary array (e.g. `ALLOWED_DOMAINS=(...)`) AND a separate set of operational directives (e.g. dnsmasq `--ipset=` lines), the test MUST validate the operational directives -- the summary array is decorative (feeds only echo/commentary) and can silently drift from the directives. Pattern: (1) add a `parseOperationalDirectives` helper that strips comment lines (`line.trimStart().startsWith('#')`) before scanning with regex, so documentation examples inside `#` comments are not counted; (2) add a cross-set drift test asserting the summary array matches the operational directives set; (3) name the REQUIRED_HOSTS constant to match the operational set, not the display set. Applied to init-firewall.sh: `parseIpsetDomains` extracts `--ipset=/DOMAIN/allowed-domains` from non-comment lines; a new test asserts ALLOWED_DOMAINS matches --ipset domain args exactly.

- **cam plan writes state-file signal, not send-keys (US-004, CAM-151, 2026-07-01)**: `runPlan` no longer injects `/cam-plan N` into the orchestrator pane via send-keys. Instead it writes `phase:planning + plan_issue:<id>` to `.claude/cam-loop.local.md` (same state-file path as `cam next`'s `active:true` write). The sidecar detects the `phase:planning` value via its readLoopPhaseFn poll and invokes `runPlanPhaseFn`. When a specific N is passed: validate the issue exists AND is plannable (stage:specified + status:open + not blocked) before writing -- do NOT fall back to top-of-queue on failure (F-01 contract). Injectable seams: `readBacklogFn?: () => IssueEntry[]` (bypasses git for tests) and `writeFn?` (captures the written body). Tests that call `runPlan` in an empty tmpdir (no git repo) MUST inject `readBacklogFn`; otherwise `readBacklogFromMain` returns [] and `runPlan` exits 1 before reaching `emitAttachHint`. See `test/attach-hint.test.ts` for the fix pattern.

- **LoopPhase enum: active derives from phase, not the reverse (US-001, CAM-151, 2026-07-01)**: `LoopPhase` (`idle|planning|implementing|awaiting-operator|shipping`) lives in `src/commands/status.ts` and is the single source of truth for the loop active flag. The `active` bool in `LoopState` is DERIVED: `active === (phase === 'implementing')`. Named `LoopPhase` (not `Phase`) to avoid collision with the orchestrator/planner/auditor model-`Phase` type in `src/config/models.ts`. Back-compat: `parseStateFile` reads the raw `active: bool` field ONLY when `phase` is absent (legacy state files). `renderStateFile` accepts `phase?: LoopPhase` and derives `active` from it; when `phase` is absent it derives phase from the legacy `active` flag so all existing callers (sidecar.ts, host.ts) work unchanged. Template placeholders: `{{PHASE}}` and `{{PLAN_ISSUE_YAML}}` were added to `vendor/cam-loop.local.md.tmpl`; always run `bun run embed-vendor` after template edits.

- **Thin slash-command stubs invalidate content-assertion tests (US-005, CAM-151, 2026-07-01)**: when a slash-command markdown file is intentionally reduced to a thin stub (removing substantial content like multi-step control-flow), tests that assert specific phrases from the OLD content must be updated. Symptom: `embedded.test.ts` test "commands/cam-plan.md embedded copy contains the clause" fails because the old Step 6 "engineering merit" text was removed together with the full 10-step flow. Fix: remove or narrow the assertion scope to only the surviving copies that still carry the clause. After any cam-plan.md stub reduction, always re-run `bun run embed-vendor` to sync `src/vendor/_generated.ts`, then `bun test test/embedded.test.ts` to catch stale content assertions early.

- **Return-value discard in factory closures is a silent production bug (US-R1-001, CAM-151, 2026-07-01)**: when a factory function builds a closure that calls a computation (e.g. `runPlanPhase`) and DISCARDS the returned result, the follow-up action never fires even though all other code is correct. This is invisible to unit tests that inject the closure directly (they never call the outer factory). Detection: grep for `runXxx({...})` (no assignment) inside factory-built closures; if the function has a non-void return type, the result must be captured and acted upon.
