Guard: add a source-text oracle test (see "Sub-state-machine optional dep injection gap" bullet) asserting the follow-up call (e.g. `runPostAuditAction(`) is present inside the factory function body. Applied: `makeProductionPlanPhaseFn` in `sidecar.ts` now captures `const planResult = runPlanPhase({...})` and passes it to `runPostAuditAction`; oracle in `test/supervisor/plan/plan-runner-postaudit.test.ts`.

- **Grouped closure factory pattern for biome 80-line compliance (US-001, CAM-155, 2026-07-02)**: when two or more related closures (e.g. `isPaneAlive` + `ensureWorkerPane`) would each push a parent function over biome's `noExcessiveLinesPerFunction` (maxLines=80) limit, group them into a single factory function that returns an object with both closures: `function makePlanPaneHelpers(claudeDir, sessionName): { isPaneAlive, ensureWorkerPane }`. The parent then uses one destructuring line instead of two multi-line closure definitions. Applied in `sidecar.ts` (`makePlanPaneHelpers`) to keep `makeProductionPlanPhaseFn` under the limit. This extends the existing "Biome cognitive complexity: use factory/helper extraction" pattern to the case where closures share state or are logically coupled.

- **pipe-pane out-log for plan-runner spawns (US-001, CAM-155, 2026-07-02)**: after each `respawn-pane -k` call in `resolveAndSpawnPlanner` and `resolveAndSpawnAuditor`, call `pipe-pane -t <paneId> 'cat >> <claudeDir>/cam-plan-out-<phase>-<uuid>.log'` when `claudeDir` is provided. This captures all pane output to a per-worker log, making future silent no-ops (where claude never started) diagnosable without attaching to the pane. Scoped to plan-runner spawns only; `loop.ts` (implementer/reviewer spawns) does NOT use pipe-pane (aspirational reference in the CAM-155 issue; do not add it to loop.ts without a dedicated story). The oracle test `grep -q 'pipe-pane' src/supervisor/plan-runner.ts` validates the log is wired.

- **Pane-death is not report-write: guard both signals separately (US-003, CAM-155, 2026-07-02)**: a planner (or any worker) can die without writing its report file (silent no-op, crash, OOM). Polling that treats pane-death as proof of report-write produces a phantom "success" and silently skips downstream phases. Pattern: after `pollPlannerDeath` returns true (pane dead), re-check `readPlannerReportFn()`. If defined and returns null, return `{ kind: 'planner-failed' }` before spawning the auditor. The dual-condition design is intentional: `isPaneAlive = false` (process exit) AND `readPlannerReportFn() !== null` (artifact written) are orthogonal signals; either can fire without the other. Backward-compat: when `readPlannerReportFn` is absent (old tests or callers that never needed the guard), skip the null-check and fall through to the auditor, so zero existing test changes are required. Applies to any file-based IPC state machine where a worker exit does not guarantee artifact production.

- **Crash-proof plan-phase closure: belt-and-suspenders try/catch pattern (US-005, CAM-155, 2026-07-02)**: the production `runPlanPhaseFn` closure (from `makeProductionPlanPhaseFn`) wraps its ENTIRE body in try/catch. On any exception: (1) log a structured `sidecar-exit` event with `reason: 'plan-phase-crash'` via the injected `logEvent`; (2) call `makeSetPhaseFn(claudeDir, cwd)('idle')` to reset the loop (non-fatal -- `makeSetPhaseFn` catches its own FS errors). The closure must NEVER rethrow. The outer `runSidecarLoop` planning branch has a second try/catch around `await opts.runPlanPhaseFn()` -- the loop guard cannot set phase (loop.ts has no claudeDir); it only catches+logs with `reason: 'plan-phase-crash-outer'`. Both guards are required (belt-and-suspenders): the closure guard handles real-world crashes; the loop guard handles any future injected fn that throws without setting phase. Tests use the ESCAPE symbol pattern to drive multiple planning ticks without real I/O and assert the loop continues despite repeated throws. Extracted `runPostPlanActions` helper keeps the closure under biome's 80-line limit while preserving the `runPostAuditAction` + `exitPhaseAfterPlan` wiring in a single place.

- **Planning phase re-entry guard: always exit phase on non-implementing outcomes (US-R1-002, CAM-151, 2026-07-01)**: when the sidecar outer loop enters the `phase:planning` branch and calls `runPlanPhaseFn`, the function MUST transition the phase out of `'planning'` for ALL outcomes other than `branch-created` (which internally calls `setPhaseFn('implementing')`). Without this guard, `readLoopPhaseFn()` returns `'planning'` on every tick and the loop re-runs the full network preflight (`git checkout main` + `git pull origin main` + `gh prune`) every 2 seconds indefinitely. Fix pattern: after `runPostAuditAction` returns, call `exitPhaseAfterPlan(result, makeSetPhaseFn(claudeDir, cwd))` where `exitPhaseAfterPlan` maps `awaiting-operator-approval` to `'awaiting-operator'` and all other non-`branch-created` results to `'idle'`. Existing loop tests masked this bug by injecting `readLoopPhaseFn: () => phaseSeq[phaseIdx++]` with a pre-computed `['planning','idle','idle']` sequence. Source-text oracle tests (Group A + B in `plan-runner-postaudit.test.ts`) catch the regression independently of injection tests.

- **Post-selection resource resolution: resolve issue-dependent values AFTER the null-check (US-001, CAM-157, 2026-07-02)**: any resource that depends on a selected value (e.g. a task prompt built from the selected issue id) MUST be resolved AFTER the selection call AND after its null-check. Resolving it before the selection (or before the null-check) produces the default/fallback value even when a specific issue was selected, silently ignoring the selection. Pattern in `runPlanPhase`: `plannerTaskPrompt` is now resolved on the line immediately after `if (issue === null) return { kind: 'no-plannable-issue' }`, using `opts.plannerTaskPrompt ?? buildPlannerTaskPrompt(issue.id)`. Export the builder as a pure helper for unit testing. The fakes-mentem class (CAM-55) makes this class of bug invisible to unit tests that mock `selectIssueFn`: the fake returns the expected issue, but the DEFAULT was already captured before the call. Guard with a real-tmux E2E test that intercepts the respawn-pane shell string and asserts it contains the target id.

- **None-backend gate on stashFn in finalizeCycleClose (US-R1-001, CAM-121, 2026-07-02)**: `stashFn(issueId)` in `finalizeCycleClose` MUST be gated behind `issueSystem === 'none'`. github and linear close their own issues via the external system, not via `closeIssueOnMain` on the local `scripts/cam/issues/` store. Without the gate, the sidecar reads `issueId` from `.cam-merge-watch.json` and passes `closeIssueId: state.issueId` to `runPostMerge`, which calls `closeIssueOnMain` against the none-backend store and gets a `not-found` error for github/linear projects. The existing AC5 tests used a no-op default `stashFn` and did not assert its absence, so they could not catch this regression: add explicit `stashedIds.toHaveLength(0)` assertions for each non-none backend.

- **Stash-before-rm pattern for deferred close (US-004, CAM-121, 2026-07-02)**: when a close operation must survive a squash-merge (which clobbers branch-side writes to shared backlog files), split the finalize step into two: (1) stash the resolved id into a sidecar state file (`.cam-merge-watch.json` via `stashIssueIdInMergeWatch`) BEFORE the git rm on the branch, so the id survives the merge; (2) run the actual close (`closeIssueOnMain`) on-main after the squash-merge lands. The interface-contract change: remove `readIssues`/`writeIssues` from `FinalizeCycleCloseOptions` and replace with a single `stashFn: (issueId: string) => void` injectable. Widen `PrdShape.issueNumber` to `number | string` (resolveIssueId accepts both; the old `typeof === 'number'` guard silently rejected string ids written by `/cam-next` anchoring). Unresolvable but non-null issueNumbers must fail loud (throw) - never stash a phantom `<prefix>-0`. The integration test for the "missing-files variant" now asserts `noOp: true` (no issue-file staging = nothing to commit), not a commit.

- **Recycle watcher PID persistence pattern (US-R1-001, CAM-162, 2026-07-02)**: the recycle watcher (spawned alongside the sidecar in `run.ts`) must have its PID persisted to `.claude/.cam-watcher.pid` (WATCHER_PID_FILE in `src/supervisor/sidecar-pid.ts`) immediately after `spawnWatcherFn` returns. This mirrors the sidecar PID pattern (SIDECAR_PID_FILE, writeSidecarPid). `cam stop` (performStop) reads the watcher PID via `watcherPidReader`, probes liveness with signal-0 (`sidecarPidAlive`, shared generic liveness probe), SIGTERMs only when alive, and always removes the pid file (idempotent). Injectable deps: `watcherPidReader`, `watcherPidAliveFn`, `watcherPidRemover` in `StopOptions` (same shape as the sidecar triplet). The SIGINT/SIGTERM cleanup handler in `run.ts` also calls `removeWatcherPidIfExists` before `watcherProc.kill()` so the file is cleaned on abnormal exit. Without persistence: a watcher started per `cam run` survives every normal detach -> `cam stop` cycle and leaks one process per cycle, each polling `.claude/.cam-orch-recycle` every 2s indefinitely.

- **Orchestrator wrapper pid marker pattern (US-001, CAM-173, 2026-07-03)**: the bash wrapper produced by `buildOrchestratorPaneCommand` writes its own stable pid (`$$`) to `.claude/.cam-orch-pid` (ORCH_PID_MARKER, exported from `src/tmux/session.ts`) ONCE, before the `while true` loop. Use `printf '%s' "$$"` NOT `$BASHPID`: macOS `/bin/bash` is 3.2.57 and `$BASHPID` is empty on that version. `$$` is the pid of the bash process that runs the while loop; it is stable across respawns because the same shell persists the entire tmux session. The file is removed in the teardown branch (alongside readyMarker; stateFile is deliberately NOT removed there since CAM-195/US-002, Defect 3) and by `cam stop` (ORCH_PID_MARKER added to the marker-removal loop). A downstream reader (e.g. the recycle watcher) can resolve the live claude child from this pid without string-matching the giant inlined argv. CAM-67 invariant applies: named export constant required, never a bare string literal.

- **Options-level injection bypass: export inner helpers and add direct unit tests (US-R1-001, 2026-07-03)**: when a module exposes an options-level seam (e.g. `resolvePidFn?: () => number | null` on `OrchRecycleWatchOptions`), tests that inject at this level bypass the inner production helpers (`readWrapperPid`, `resolveChildViaPgrep`) entirely. Their parsing guards (empty file, non-finite pid, pid<=0, non-zero pgrep exit, empty/multi-line stdout) then have zero coverage and can regress silently. Fix: (1) export the inner helpers; (2) add an injectable seam to any helper that calls a system function (e.g. `spawnFn?: PgrepSpawnFn` on `resolveChildViaPgrep`); (3) write a dedicated test file that imports the exported helpers directly and exercises every guard branch with real temp files (file-based helpers) or fake spawn output (spawn-based helpers). The options-level injection test suite is still needed for the outer loop; the per-helper tests are an additional layer, not a replacement.

- **ensureWorkerContainer 4-branch reconciliation (US-003, CAM-152, 2026-07-03)**: idempotent container lifecycle uses DockerProbe (inspect-only, args without binary) for ALL read calls and ContainerSpawnFn (cmd+args) for ALL mutation calls: `spawnFn('docker', ['rm', '-f', name])`, `spawnFn('docker', ['start', name])`, and via `runWorkerContainer` for build+run. Branch priority: (1) image-stale (checked first via `preflightWorkerContainer`; container removed then rebuilt); (2) absent (inspect exits non-zero; runWorkerContainer called); (3) running (inspect stdout==='true'; no-op); (4) stopped (inspect stdout==='false'; docker start). When `statFn` is absent, the stale check is skipped and the image-stale branch never fires. Production factory `makeProductionEnsureContainerFn(cwd)` builds spawnFn (wraps spawnSync), probe, and statFn (wraps statSync); returns a no-arg closure for sidecar boot. Tests inject fake probe+spawnFn pairs and never touch a real daemon (CI: macos-latest, no Docker installed). Wired in `runSidecar` ONLY when `readWorkerIsolation() === 'container'`; host mode = zero docker calls.

- **Fail-closed default enum reader idiom (US-001, CAM-152, 2026-07-03)**: the standard pattern for a new boolean-ish config flag in `src/config/models.ts` is: (1) declare `export type FlagName = 'safe-default' | 'opt-in-value'`; (2) implement `readFlagName(configPath?: string): FlagName` mirroring `readMetaLoop`/`readPlanApproval`/`readMergeMode` exactly: read the TOML section+key inside try/catch, return the opt-in value ONLY on exact string match, return the safe default on every other path (missing file, missing section, missing key, malformed TOML, non-string, wrong string); (3) test file under `test/config/` with 5 describe blocks covering explicit opt-in, absent, wrong string, non-string, malformed TOML. The configPath override param is the DI seam for tests (never mutate the real `scripts/cam/project.toml`). Applied to: `readWorkerIsolation` (default `'host'`, opt-in `'container'`, key `[loop] worker_isolation`); same `[loop]` section as `meta_loop`.

- **Shared-dep hoisting before multiple callers in host.ts (US-005, CAM-152, 2026-07-03)**: when a variable in `buildSupervisorOptions` (host.ts) needs to be threaded into BOTH `makeReviewDispatch` (mid-function) AND the final `RunSupervisorOptions` opts bag (end-of-function), declare it BEFORE the `makeReviewDispatch` call. TypeScript enforces block-scoped variable order: using a `const` before its `let`/`const` declaration in the same block scope is a TS2448/TS2454 error even if the value wouldn't be read at runtime before assignment. Fix: hoist `preflightContainerFn` and `workerIsolation` declarations to the position just before `makeReviewDispatch`. The same instance is then referenced in both the review dispatch options and the opts bag (no duplicate production wiring). Applied to US-005 when reviewer needed the same `preflightContainerFn` + `workerIsolation` that the implementer dispatch already used.

- **resolveHostIds: injectable seam for POSIX id -u / id -g (US-002, CAM-178, 2026-07-04)**: `resolveHostIds(spawnFn: ContainerSpawnFn): { uid: number; gid: number }` is exported from `src/supervisor/worker-container.ts`. Calls `spawnFn('id', ['-u'])` and `spawnFn('id', ['-g'])`; trims stdout; guards for empty string (noUncheckedIndexedAccess); parseInt base 10; NaN or non-zero exitCode -> fallback 1000 per component. POSIX spec: `id -u` / `id -g` always output a decimal integer + `\n` (confirmed against pubs.opengroup.org). Production callers reuse the same `ContainerSpawnFn` that wraps `spawnSync`, so no extra seam is needed. Tests inject recording fakes to verify fallback paths without a real shell. Named-volume upgrade caveat: the `claude-code-config` volume retains uid-1000 ownership after a HOST_UID rebuild; `docker volume rm claude-code-config` is the one-time fix (see docs/recovery-runbook.md).

- **Jq-free in-container node JSON merge pattern (US-002, CAM-185, 2026-07-04)**: when a container may not have jq (pre-fix volumes), use an inline node script passed via `docker exec <container> node -e '<script>'` to perform a read-modify-write on a JSON config file. Script pattern: `let c={}; try{c=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(p,'utf8'))||{};}catch(e){} /* mutate specific keys */ fs.writeFileSync(p,JSON.stringify(c,null,2))`. The `||{}` guard handles `JSON.parse` returning null for a null-literal file. This is a MERGE (not an overwrite): unrelated fields (machineID, userID) are preserved because only the targeted keys are overwritten. Two docker exec steps are ALWAYS DISTINCT: `docker exec -u root <container> chown -R bun:bun /path` (dir ownership) vs `docker exec <container> node -e <script>` (file content). Chowning the .claude directory does NOT repair the sibling .claude.json content. The `applyContainerConfig` orchestrator runs chown first, config-assert second, returns a discriminated union {ok:true} | {ok:false,exitCode,stderrTail}, and never throws (same pattern as applyContainerFirewall). The ContainerConfigError is thrown by the CALLER, not the orchestrator.

- **configSpawnFn seam + applyConfigIfPresent extraction pattern (US-003, CAM-185, 2026-07-04)**: add a new injectable seam to `EnsureWorkerContainerOptions` following the exact firewallSpawnFn precedent: optional, structurally identical to FirewallSpawnFn, wired in `makeProductionEnsureContainerFn` as a separate closure (not aliased to firewallSpawnFn) so each seam remains independently injectable in tests. Extract both the firewall and config apply steps into private `applyFirewallIfPresent` / `applyConfigIfPresent` helpers BEFORE the main function body: this drops `ensureWorkerContainer` biome cognitive complexity from 17 to 13 without adding a `noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity: off` override. Thread HOST_UID/HOST_GID via `opts.build?.hostUid` / `opts.build?.hostGid` (already set by `resolveHostIds` in the factory) into the config apply call so the chown target is numeric uid:gid, not hardcoded `bun:bun`. ContainerConfigError is thrown by the helper (not by applyContainerConfig) mirroring the FirewallError pattern. The file-size gate uses `content.split('\n').length` (= wc -l + 1 for files with trailing newline): set budget ceilings to actual line-count + 1.

- **Dockerfile uid/gid re-homing pattern for bind-mount write access (US-001, CAM-178/183, 2026-07-04)**: when a container process must write a bind-mounted directory owned by the host user, re-home the non-root image user at build time via `ARG HOST_UID=1000` + `ARG HOST_GID=1000` (declared AFTER FROM, BEFORE the RUN that consumes them per Docker ARG scoping rules). Use a getent-branch guard: `if getent group "$HOST_GID" >/dev/null 2>&1; then usermod -u "$HOST_UID" -g "$HOST_GID" bun; else groupmod -g "$HOST_GID" bun && usermod -u "$HOST_UID" bun; fi && chown -R "$HOST_UID:$HOST_GID" /home/bun`. WHY: `groupmod -n` renames a group's NAME but NEVER frees its GID; calling `groupmod -g $HOST_GID` afterwards still fails with 'GID already exists'. The getent branch avoids the problem entirely: if the group already exists, `usermod -g <gid>` switches to it directly (usermod(8) requires the group to already exist, which getent confirms); if not, groupmod claims the GID first. `chown -R` re-owns home files AFTER usermod (usermod does NOT re-own existing files). COPY baked config BEFORE the ARG HOST_UID directive so the trailing chown covers it. The corresponding `buildDockerBuildArgv` emits `--build-arg HOST_UID=<uid> --build-arg HOST_GID=<gid>` only when BOTH hostUid and hostGid are present; absent either field emits no token (default ARG values apply, uid stays 1000). DO NOT use the old groupmod -n rename guard: it does not free the GID.

- **bun test double-prints each failing test name in verbose mode (US-001, CAM-186, 2026-07-04)**: in default verbose mode, `bun test` prints each failing test name TWICE: once in the per-file output with a duration suffix (`   ✗ test name (2ms)`) and once in the failure-detail block below without a duration (`   ✗ test name`). Any parser that captures both occurrences must deduplicate. Pattern: use a `Set<string>` to collect names and spread to an array at the end -- `Set` preserves insertion order and collapses duplicates in one pass. When `CLAUDECODE=1` is set in the container env, bun suppresses the per-file output and only the failure-detail block headers appear (no duration, no duplicates). The dedup Set is a no-op in that mode (idempotent). The summary lines ` N pass` / ` N fail` / ` N skip` (single leading space + number + space + word) are printed once and are unambiguous in both modes. Do NOT re-derive the exit code from the raw docker exec exit code: `bun test` may exit non-zero on skips; always re-derive from the parsed `fail` count (`fail > 0 ? 1 : 0`).

- **Bun.spawnSync throws ENOENT on missing binary; module-level availability probes must use Bun.which or node:child_process.spawnSync (US-002, CAM-186, 2026-07-04)**: `Bun.spawnSync(['tool', '--version'])` throws an Error with `ENOENT` message when the binary is not found on PATH. `node:child_process.spawnSync('tool', ['--version'], { stdio: 'pipe' })` returns `{ status: null, error: ... }` gracefully -- it never throws. A module-level `Bun.spawnSync` probe (outside a function) causes an "Unhandled error between tests" crash that fails the entire test file even when the probe result would have triggered a `test.skipIf`. Canonical fix: prefer `Bun.which('tool') !== null` (returns null on missing binary, does not throw); alternatively switch to `node:child_process.spawnSync` if already imported; as last resort wrap in a try-catch IIFE. NEVER use `Bun.spawnSync` for module-level availability probes.

- **bun 1.2.x macrotask scheduling differs from bun >=1.3 for ink-testing-library stdin-driven state (US-002, CAM-186, 2026-07-04)**: `stdin.write(char)` fires the `useInput` handler synchronously, but flushing the React state update into `lastFrame()` requires one macrotask tick (`await tick()`) on bun >=1.3 and TWO ticks on bun 1.2.x. Tests that write to stdin and assert `lastFrame()` after one tick will fail on bun 1.2.x (oven/bun:1.2-slim container). The canonical fix is `test.skipIf(!bunVersionOk)` with a module-level version probe: `const [maj, min] = Bun.version.split('.'); const bunVersionOk = parseInt(maj) > 1 || parseInt(min) >= 3`. This is env-specific (the container image is locked to bun 1.2.x; the production orchestrator and workers run on the host with bun >=1.3); the skip is justified and does not represent a production code gap.

- **finishTerminal wrapper pattern for unconditional teardown at supervisor terminal exits (US-001, CAM-188, 2026-07-05)**: `notifyTerminal` in `runSupervisor` (loop.ts) has an early-return guard `if (!onProgress) return` that fires when onProgress is absent. Any side-effect that must run unconditionally on terminal exit (e.g. kill-pane teardown) CANNOT be folded inside `notifyTerminal`. Instead, wrap it in a separate `finishTerminal` helper that calls `notifyTerminal` first, then the unconditional fn. All 16 terminal-exit call sites use `finishTerminal`; the single remaining `notifyTerminal` call is inside `finishTerminal` itself. Injectable as `teardownWorkerPaneFn?: () => void` in `RunSupervisorOptions` (defaults to no-op so all existing callers are unchanged). Production wiring in host.ts: `spawnSync('tmux', ['-L', 'cam', 'kill-pane', '-t', id])` using `readWorkerPaneMarker` for an up-to-date pane id (the marker file is rewritten on each ensureWorkerPane, so it reflects the current pane even after a self-heal respawn). Ordering invariant on the complete path: `autoShipFn` fires BEFORE `finishTerminal('complete')`, so teardown happens only after auto-ship. `kill-pane` (not `respawn-pane -k`) is essential: it removes the pane from the session, reducing count from 3 to 2 so `paneCountMutex` reports 'available'.

- **Isolation-aware default resolution requires reordering dependent declarations, not just editing the fallback expression (US-003, CAM-187, 2026-07-05)**: when a `const x = opts.x ?? DEFAULT` fallback needs to become aware of another option (`workerIsolation`) that is resolved LATER in the same function body, the fix in `host.ts` is to physically relocate the dependent computation to after the resolution it depends on, not to forward-reference a not-yet-declared `const`. Before moving, grep every usage of the dependent const (`grep -n perWorkerTimeoutMs src/supervisor/host.ts`) to confirm no site between the old and new declaration position reads it; if none exist, the move is safe. In `loop.ts` (the pure function, no such ordering constraint since all `opts.*` fields are available on the same `opts` object from the start), the isolation-aware default was expressed inline as `opts.perWorkerTimeoutMs ?? (opts.workerIsolation === 'container' ? DEFAULT_CONTAINER_WORKER_TIMEOUT_MS : DEFAULT_PER_WORKER_TIMEOUT_MS)`, reading `opts.workerIsolation` directly rather than the later-declared local `workerIsolation` const, avoiding any reorder inside `runSupervisor` itself.

- **Verify the ACTUAL commit convention against real git log, not just the docs, before writing an anchored matcher (US-R1-001, CAM-187, 2026-07-05)**: `scripts/cam/CLAUDE.md` step 8 and the implementer agent prompt both say `feat: [Story ID] - [Story Title]` (bracketed), but the pure matcher `commitSubjectMatchesStory` (added in US-001) was written against a bracketless mental model (`feat: <id> - <title>`) and never checked against a real commit on the branch, so it silently could not confirm any real commit. When writing a regex matcher for a documented text convention, run `git log --format=%s <branch>` and test the matcher against an actual sample line before trusting the docs prose. Fix: use an alternation `(?:\[${id}\]|${id})` (never `\[?${id}\]?`, which would allow a mismatched single bracket) so both the bracketed convention and older bracketless history match, anchored the same way on both sides.

- **`git log --format=%s <branch>` walks the FULL ancestry to root, not commits unique to the branch: scope with `<upstream>..HEAD` (US-R1-002, CAM-187, 2026-07-05)**: a bare `git log --format=%s <branch-name>` (no range) lists every commit reachable from that branch's tip all the way back to the root commit (git-scm.com/docs/git-log, confirmed via WebFetch). For any gate that must confirm "a commit was made ON THIS BRANCH for this PRD" (e.g. `commitExistsForStory` in host.ts), a bare branch-name arg silently matches ancient unrelated commits from prior PRDs that reused the same story-id naming convention (US-001, US-002, ... are recycled every PRD). Fix: use the double-dot range `origin/main..HEAD` (git's own docs: "shows commits reachable from ref2 but not ref1", equivalent to `git log HEAD ^origin/main`) after a best-effort `git fetch origin main` to keep it fresh; fall back to local `main..HEAD` when `git rev-parse origin/main` fails (no remote configured, e.g. real-git tmpdir integration tests). This is the general pattern for any future "commits unique to this branch" gate: never pass a bare ref to `git log`, always scope with `<upstream>..HEAD`.

- **Real-git integration test for a host.ts adapter that needs a branch fork point (US-R1-003, CAM-187, 2026-07-05)**: extends the "Real-git integration test pattern" (US-004, CAM-72) bullet above for adapters whose behavior depends on the branch's fork point from `main` (e.g. `commitExistsForStory`'s `main..HEAD` fallback range). Setup: `git init`, then `git symbolic-ref HEAD refs/heads/main` (pins the default branch name regardless of `init.defaultBranch`, same trick as `write-issue-file-worktree-sync.test.ts`), commit an ancient/unrelated commit on `main` (`git commit --allow-empty` works even as the very first commit, no files needed), `git checkout -b <feature-branch>`, then commit the story-relevant commit unique to the branch. Call the real adapter via `buildSupervisorOptions(dir).opts.<field>` directly (no need to fake `RunSupervisorOptions`); this is safe because `buildSupervisorOptions` only builds closures at call time (no eager fs/mkdir side effects until an individual adapter is invoked). Assert both directions in one test: the branch-unique commit IS found, and the pre-fork ancient commit (whose subject textually matches too) is NOT found, since with no `origin` remote configured `git fetch origin main` fails silently and `git rev-parse origin/main` fails, so the adapter falls back to local `main..HEAD` scoping.

- **Pure PRD-snapshot composer pattern for the ship path (US-001, CAM-149, 2026-07-05)**: `src/release/pr-body.ts` (`composePrTitle`/`composePrBody`) mirrors the `generateReleaseBody` precedent (`src/release/changelog.ts`): a pure string-in/string-out function with zero filesystem/git/network I/O, so it is trivially unit-testable and needs no LLM at ship time. Define the input shape (`PrdSnapshot`) locally in the composer module rather than importing the command-layer `PrdShape`/`PrdStory` types (`src/commands/status.ts`, `src/commands/ship-finalize.ts`) or the supervisor's `PrdSnapshot` (`src/supervisor/decide.ts`): those three shapes already diverge from each other by field set and by consumer concern, and a fourth pure-composer consumer should not couple to any of them. Callers MUST capture this snapshot (project, description, issueNumber, userStories) from prd.json BEFORE `cam ship --finalize` runs, since finalize deletes prd.json (see `ship-finalize.ts` cycle-close). prd.json has no top-level `notes` field today; the composer accepts an optional `notes?: string` for forward-compat PRD-level remarks and falls back to `'None.'` when absent, without inventing a writer for it.

- **Negative-grep file-assert oracles ban the substring anywhere in the file, including comments (US-002, CAM-149, 2026-07-05)**: an AC like `[oracle: file-assert ! grep -q 'force' src/supervisor/ship-runner.ts]` matches the bare substring, not a whole word. Common English words silently contain banned substrings: "enforced"/"reinforce"/"workforce" all contain "force" (`en-FORCE-d`). When a story's AC bans a token via grep, proofread every comment and docstring in the touched file for the substring, not just the executable code; a documentation sentence like "step order is enforced" trips the oracle exactly like a real `--force` flag would. Same caution applies to filename-shaped bans (e.g. `! grep -q 'file-size-budget'`): don't reference the literal path even in prose, describe it generically instead ("ratchet budget files").

- **TS index-signature assignability: named interface values need a spread to satisfy a `Record<string, unknown>` catch-all (US-002, CAM-149, 2026-07-05)**: when a union type includes a `Record<string, unknown>` fallback member (e.g. `WorkerEventDetail` in `src/supervisor/events.ts`), TypeScript only accepts a FRESH object literal there, not a pre-typed named variable/interface value, even when every property is structurally compatible (`unknown` accepts anything). `logEvent({ ..., detail: bumpResult })` where `bumpResult: ShipBumpResultLike` fails with "Index signature for type 'string' is missing in type 'ShipBumpResultLike'"; spreading into a fresh literal (`detail: { ...bumpResult }`) fixes it with no runtime difference. Applies symmetrically in tests: `expect(x).toEqual(NAMED_CONST)` hits the same error if `x` is typed as the union and `NAMED_CONST` is a named interface-typed const; spread it too (`toEqual({ ...NAMED_CONST })`).

- **Threading a value through the caller's opts avoids merge-watch's issueId-only-seed null gotcha (US-003, CAM-149, 2026-07-05)**: `readMergeWatchState` returns `null` for a file that has only `{issueId}` stashed (prNumber absent, see the "Durable merge-watch state helpers" bullet above), so any downstream step that needs to preserve that stashed issueId while enriching the file with `prNumber`/`mergedBranch` CANNOT recover it from `readMergeWatchState` alone. `ship-pr.ts`'s `runShipPrStep` sidesteps this entirely: the caller (`ship-runner.ts`) already carries the same issueId in memory from `finalizeCycleClose`'s result, so it is threaded straight through the `ShipPrStepInput.issueId` field instead of being re-read from the merge-watch file. The `readMergeWatchState` call before the enrichment write is then only a defensive idempotency check (preserves `pollCount`/`updateBranchCount` on an accidental re-run), not the source of truth for the issueId. General lesson: when a durable-state reader has a documented null-on-partial-seed gotcha, prefer threading the value through the call chain from wherever it was already resolved, rather than adding a second raw-JSON reader to recover it.

- **Injectable writer seam for commands built on src/logging's emit\* helpers (US-002, CAM-190, 2026-07-05)**: every `emit*` helper in `src/logging/screen.ts` (`emitTitle`, `emitSectionHeading`, `emitContent`, `emitMutedHint`, etc.) writes straight to `process.stdout.write` with no writer parameter, unlike `printConfigShow`'s injectable-writer pattern (`src/commands/config.ts`). A new linear-print command that both (a) must reuse the shared emit\* helpers for visual consistency, and (b) needs a genuine injectable writer seam for deterministic tests, can reconcile the two by temporarily monkey-patching `process.stdout.write` to route through the injected writer for the duration of the command, restoring the original in a `finally` block (see `withWriter` in `src/commands/issue-list.ts`). This is not a new idea: `test/next.test.ts` already monkey-patches `process.stdout.write` from the TEST side to capture emit\*-based output; `withWriter` just moves the same technique into the command as an official optional dependency, so tests inject a plain writer function instead of hand-rolling the monkey-patch/restore dance themselves. When `writer` is omitted, the command is a byte-identical passthrough to every other emit\*-based command.

- **git ls-tree/cat-file recording-spawn fixture pattern generalizes cleanly (US-002, CAM-190, 2026-07-05)**: any command that consumes `readBacklogFromMain` (or another `BacklogSpawnFn`-shaped reader) can be unit-tested deterministically by building a fake spawn that (1) returns one placeholder path per fixture entry for the `ls-tree` call (path CONTENT does not need to match the real `issueFilePath` convention -- only the entry COUNT and ORDER matter, since `cat-file --batch` output is consumed positionally, not by matching path strings back to content) and (2) concatenates `<oid> blob <size>\n<content>\n`-framed JSON blobs for the `cat-file --batch` call, in the same order as the fixture array. Recording every `(cmd, args)` call lets a single test assert BOTH the row-level behavior AND the zero-tmux/zero-claude determinism claim (grep the recorded args for `send-keys`/`list-panes`/`tmux`/`claude` and assert none match) without needing a second, separate integration test.

- **Reserved-word positional subcommand ordering in a mixed flag/positional CLI parser (US-003, CAM-190, 2026-07-05)**: when a `parseXxxArgs` function already has a flag-triggered mode (e.g. `--file-local`) plus a positional free-text fallback (`args[0]` becomes the created item's text), adding a new positional subcommand (`list`) that must NOT be swallowed by the free-text fallback requires checking `args[0] === '<reserved-word>'` as its OWN branch, ordered after the `--help`/flag branches but strictly BEFORE the free-text read at the bottom of the function. This makes the reserved word un-creatable as free text (`cam issue list` can never become an issue titled "list") by construction, not by a runtime disambiguation heuristic. Extend `ParsedIssueArgs`-style discriminated unions with a new `{ mode: 'list'; all: boolean; help: false }` member (mirrors the CAM-86 `parseXxxArgs` discriminated-union pattern already documented above) and route it in `dispatchXxx` via its own injectable dep (`issueListFn`) so a unit test can assert the branch NEVER calls the other mode's thin-proxy deps.

- **Backlog-derivation instruction must live in BOTH the boot-prompt string AND the persona doc, not just one (US-004, CAM-190, 2026-07-05)**: `buildOrchestratorBootPrompt()` (src/commands/run.ts) is the literal first message fed to the orchestrator claude process; the persona file it tells the agent to read next (`.claude/agents/subagent-orchestrator.md`) is a SEPARATE text surface. A binary/branch-coherence instruction (here: derive the backlog via the real `cam issue list` shell command, with an explicit fallback to reading `scripts/cam/issues/*.json` filtered by stage when the command is unavailable or exits non-zero, e.g. a pre-rebuild binary) must be asserted in both places independently: a unit test on `buildOrchestratorBootPrompt()`'s return string, AND a doc-gate test on the embedded `templatesContents['agents/subagent-orchestrator.md']` persona copy. Putting the instruction in only one surface leaves the other one silently stale. The doc-gate test pattern (CAM-61 style, mirrors `test/vendor/cam-ship-hygiene.test.ts`) is the right home for the persona-side assertion; a plain `toContain` unit test is the right home for the boot-prompt-side assertion. A greeting-spec invariant ("no per-issue enumeration") is best phrased as a literal negative string the persona doc itself states (e.g. "no per-issue list, ever"), so the doc-gate test can assert its presence directly instead of trying to prove a negative about the greeting template's shape.

- **Line-anchor heading matches in Keep-a-Changelog transforms; never `text.indexOf` a Markdown heading string (US-001, CAM-98, 2026-07-06)**: `text.indexOf('## [Unreleased]')` matches the FIRST occurrence of that literal substring anywhere in the file, including inside intro prose (e.g. a backtick-quoted format description like `` Format: `## [Unreleased]` for pending work ``). This is exactly what silently corrupted the live CHANGELOG.md since 0.2.0: every roll spliced the new version into the prose line instead of the real heading further down. Fix: find the heading with a multiline-anchored regex, `/^## \[Unreleased\][ \t]*$/m`, via `.exec(text)`, and use `match.index` / `match[0].length` (guard `match[0] ?? fallback` for `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`) instead of `idx` / `heading.length`. Generalize this to ANY Markdown-heading-locating transform: substring search over Markdown text is unsafe the moment prose is allowed to mention the heading text; always require the match to occupy a full line.

- **Stale platform-specific optionalDependency in a committed lockfile breaks `bunx`-shelled tools in a differently-arched container (2026-07-06)**: in this sandboxed container (linux-arm64), `bunx biome lint` failed with `Cannot find module '@biomejs/cli-linux-arm64/biome'` even though `biome.json` and `package.json` were untouched; `node_modules/@biomejs/` only had `cli-darwin-arm64` installed (the lockfile/node_modules snapshot was materialized on a different host arch). Fix: plain `bun install` (no flags) re-resolves and installs the correct platform optionalDependency without touching the lockfile's logical content. Confirmed via `git stash` that this failure predates and is unrelated to any story diff; treat a `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` for an `@biomejs/cli-<platform>` (or similarly arch-suffixed) package as an environment/container issue to fix with `bun install`, not a code regression to chase.

- **Red-gate guard: key the "no-flaky-evasion" check on the RECORDED gate string, never on worker prose (US-001, CAM-202, 2026-07-06)**: `worker-report.json`'s `gates.tests` field ("41 pass / 1 fail", "ok", "n/a", "40 pass / 3 skip / 0 fail", "fail: <detail>") is the only trustworthy signal that a story's test gate actually passed; a worker's free-text narration ("this failure is pre-existing/flaky/unrelated") must never be allowed to override it. `gateTestsIndicateFailure(tests: string)` (src/supervisor/result.ts) is a pure classifier: FAILING iff the string starts with "fail" or contains an "`<N> fail`" count with N>0; legitimate skip-only strings (0 fails) are NOT failing, so OS/capability-gated skips (e.g. real-tmux tests off-macOS) never false-positive. Wire the guard into `readWorkerOutcome`'s PRIMARY (worker-report-present) DONE path only, checked BEFORE `confirmCommitGate`, returning the existing `kind:'fail'` (no new `WorkerOutcomeKind` needed -- loop.ts already routes `'fail'` to the blocked terminal branch). The guard deliberately does NOT run in the fallback (no-worker-report) path: that path has no recorded gates to check. `report.gates` is untrusted worker-authored JSON: always runtime-check its shape (`extractRecordedTestsGate`) rather than trusting a blind TS cast.

- **Raising a file-size budget ceiling requires a tracker ref in the SAME staged diff of `scripts/file-size-budget.json` (2026-07-06)**: `checkFileSizes` (scripts/check-file-sizes.ts) fails the diff-tracker check when a ceiling is raised without a `CAM-NNN`/`#N`/URL reference anywhere in the staged diff of the budget file itself; the project convention (see the `_ref` field) is to prepend one narrative sentence to `_ref` naming the branch/story/CAM ticket and the old->new line counts for every file whose ceiling moved, before the per-file ceiling entries below it, so both the size increase and its justification land in the same diff hunk.

- **Wiring a deterministic writer into an operator-interview slash-command doc: safe-quoting prose is part of the acceptance criteria, not an implementation detail (US-004, CAM-118, 2026-07-06)**: when a `.claude/commands/*.md` doc must instruct the orchestrator to pipe LLM-assembled JSON (built from free-text interview answers) into a `cam <sub> --write-*` stdin entrypoint, the doc itself must show the exact safe form (`cam ... <<'EOF'` with quoted `EOF`, or single-quoted `echo`) and explicitly forbid the unquoted form (CAM-106: an unquoted heredoc/echo lets the shell interpolate `$`/backticks that may appear verbatim in an operator's interview answer, corrupting the JSON or executing arbitrary content). This is prose-only guidance (the write channel itself, e.g. `runSpecWriteDocs`, has no way to enforce how its caller quotes stdin), so the file-assert oracle for this kind of story greps for the literal quoted heredoc marker (e.g. `grep -q "<<'EOF'"`) in the command doc, not in any `.ts` source. Pair it with an explicit "never silently skip a non-zero exit" instruction and an explicit "never hand-write the target file via Edit/Write/NotebookEdit" prohibition: both close the exact CAM-109 dogfood gap (a second write silently dropped after the first one succeeded).

- **Dogfooding the no-flaky-evasion rule while implementing it: fix, don't dismiss, the gates it just made stricter (US-002, CAM-202, 2026-07-06)**: bringing `bun run check:all` green for a docs-only story surfaced two real, reproducible (not story-caused) gate failures; both were fixed rather than waived, per the rule this story adds. (1) `bunx knip@6.24.0` crashes with `SyntaxError: ... node:util ... styleText` on a sandbox whose system `node` is v18 (styleText needs Node >= 20), because `bunx` respects a package bin's `#!/usr/bin/env node` shebang and delegates to the system interpreter by default. Fix: `bunx --bun knip@6.24.0` in the `dead-code` gate (`scripts/check-all.ts`) forces Bun's own JS runtime instead (confirmed against Bun's official `bunx` docs: `--bun` overrides the shebang delegation; this is a general fix for any `bunx`-invoked Node-only CLI crashing on an old system Node, not knip-specific). (2) `test/dashboard.test.ts`'s "k after j returns to first row" flaked under full-suite CPU load: a single `setTimeout(r, 0)` tick after each of two back-to-back keypresses was not always enough for React to flush both `useInput`-triggered re-renders before the assertion ran (reproduced ~1-in-3 full-suite runs, 0-in-6 when run in isolation -- a load/scheduling-jitter flake, not a logic bug). Fix: replace the second blind tick with a small poll helper (`waitForAccentLine`, 5ms interval, 500ms bounded timeout) that retries the assertion condition instead of sampling `lastFrame()` exactly once. Generalize: prefer a bounded poll over a single fixed-delay tick for any assertion that depends on more than one re-render committing. Both fixes required touching the gate's own regression test (`test/check-all.test.ts` argv assertion) and the file-size ratchet (`scripts/file-size-budget.json`) in the same commit -- verify the ratchet's line-count convention is `content.split('\n').length` (`wc -l + 1` for a trailing-newline file), not bare `wc -l`, before setting a new ceiling, or `check:file-size` will still fail by exactly one line after the "fix".

- **Durable marker module template + a gitignore-coverage gap in the precedent it mirrors (US-002, CAM-204, 2026-07-06)**: `src/supervisor/plan-escalation.ts` (`PLAN_ESCALATED_FILENAME` + read/write/remove) is a byte-for-byte structural clone of the `ShipStalledMarker` precedent in `src/release/merge-watch.ts` (JSON reader shape guard on every required field, never-throws read, best-effort write, best-effort no-op remove). When mirroring a durable `.claude/`-dir marker this way, don't assume the precedent's own `.gitignore` entry exists: `.cam-ship-stalled.json` (the precedent this story was told to mirror "verbatim") turned out to have NO entry in either `.gitignore` or `templates/.gitignore` despite being durable runtime state -- an upstream oversight, not a new-story gap. Always `grep` both gitignore files for the literal marker filename before assuming coverage; add the new marker's own entry (in both files, mirroring the self-hosting convention for `templates/`) regardless of what the precedent did. A future story could retroactively add the missing `.cam-ship-stalled.json` entry.

- **Narrow default-parameter spawn seam beats an options-bag for validator-shaped `spawnSync` call sites (US-001, CAM-205, 2026-07-06)**: when only a HANDFUL of pure-ish validator functions (`lookupOnPath`/`validateClaude`/`runVendoredSmoke` in `src/commands/init.ts`) call `spawnSync` and tests only ever need to stub the return shape (`{ status, stdout, stderr }`), define a narrow local type (`SpawnFn = (cmd: string, args: string[]) => {...}`) rather than reusing `typeof spawnSync`'s much wider overloaded signature, and thread it as a DEFAULT PARAMETER (`spawnFn: SpawnFn = defaultSpawnFn`) through each function down to the top-level `InitOptions.spawnFn`. Default parameters mean any caller that never mentions the seam (e.g. the interactive Ink path, `buildInteractiveChecks`, which calls `validateClaude()`/`runVendoredSmoke(path)` with zero args) is untouched byte-for-byte in production, while `bun test` (which always routes through `runInitLinear` because `isInitInteractiveGate` is false under piped stdin/stdout) gets full determinism for free by injecting `spawnFn` at just the `runInit({ spawnFn })` entrypoint. This is cheaper than threading an options-bag or a DI container through every call site, and it generalizes to any CLI command whose interactive/linear paths already fork on a TTY gate: only the linear path needs the injectable seam, since tests never reach the interactive path anyway.

- **BLOCK->re-plan loop driver: wrap, don't inline, the capped-rounds control flow (US-003, CAM-204, 2026-07-06)**: `runPlanPhaseWithReplan` (plan-runner.ts) wraps the existing `runPlanPhase` in a `while (result.kind === 'audit-blocked' && roundsCompleted < MAX_REPLAN_ROUNDS)` loop rather than inlining the round logic into `runPlanPhase` itself; this keeps `runPlanPhase` byte-for-byte compatible for its ~50 existing tests while the new loop gets its own test file. Three mechanics matter for any future capped-round loop of this shape: (1) capture the blocked round's `issue`/`report.findings` into local variables BEFORE calling `runPlanPhase` again -- the next round's `clearStalePlanArtifactsFn` (invoked at the top of `runPlanPhase`) deletes the verdict file the captured values came from, so read-then-clear ordering only works because the values already live in JS variables, not because of any read-order guarantee inside the callee. (2) pin the re-plan round to the SAME issue by overriding `selectIssueFn: () => blockedIssue` (a closure over the captured issue) rather than trusting the real selector to return the same issue twice; combine this with `plannerTaskPrompt: buildReplanPlannerTaskPrompt(...)` as another override on the SAME opts object passed to the nested `runPlanPhase` call. (3) an exhausted-but-still-blocked result must be re-packaged into a NEW terminal kind (`plan-escalated`) before returning -- if the wrapper just forwarded the raw `audit-blocked` result unchanged, a caller (e.g. `runPostAuditAction`) that already has an `audit-blocked` branch would treat first-round-blocked and rounds-exhausted identically, silently skipping the durable-escalation-marker seam a later story wires in. Both `teardownPlanPanesFn` and the marker-writer seam are optional with no-op defaults (`?? ((): void => {})`) for backward compat, mirroring the `teardownWorkerPaneFn?` pattern in loop.ts, even though production wiring (a later story) always supplies both.

- **Operator-input-error terminal handling: notify + log, never escalate; the idle-exit write already clears paired state (US-003, CAM-203, 2026-07-06)**: for a new discriminated-union terminal that represents a bad OPERATOR INPUT (e.g. `plan-target-invalid`: an explicit `/cam-plan <id>` named an unplannable issue) rather than an infra failure, mirror the existing `planner-failed` precedent's shape in the post-result dispatcher (`runPostAuditAction`): call `notifyFn` (orchestrator-pane push naming the bad input) and `logEvent` (structured flight-recorder event), but deliberately do NOT call `escalateFn` -- an operator typo is not a pager-worthy alert. Return the generic `{ kind: 'no-action' }` so the SAME idle-exit code path used by every other non-implementing terminal handles the phase transition; no new phase-write branch is needed. When the paired transient state that caused the bad input (here, `plan_issue` in `cam-loop.local.md`) was written by a PREVIOUS tick, check whether the idle-exit writer already clears it as a side effect of its normal call shape before adding a bespoke clear: `makeSetPhaseFn(claudeDir, cwd)('idle')` (no second arg) renders `plan_issue: null` because the writer's `plan_issue` field comes ONLY from its own optional second argument, never from the previously-parsed state file's value -- calling it with one arg is already a full clear, no separate "clear stale target" step required. To keep the branch's line count under biome's 80-line-per-function ceiling, extract the notify+log body into its own named helper (`handlePlanTargetInvalid(targetId, notifyFn, logEvent)`) rather than inlining it alongside the other terminal branches in the same function.

- **A caller-supplied "target was invalid" terminal is a NEW union member, not an overload of the generic empty-result kind (US-002, CAM-203, 2026-07-06)**: when a discriminated-union result (here `PlanPhaseResult`) already has a generic "nothing selected" terminal (`no-plannable-issue`), and a new caller-labeling input arrives (`planTargetId?: string`, an explicit `/cam-plan <id>` target), add a DISTINCT terminal kind (`{ kind: 'plan-target-invalid'; targetId: string }`) rather than adding an optional field to the existing generic kind. This keeps every existing consumer's narrow-on-`kind` switch exhaustive-by-construction (no silent fallthrough) and lets the new kind carry exactly the payload its callers need (the target id, for a target-naming error message). Resolve the branch point with a one-line guard helper (`resolveNoIssueResult(planTargetId)`) called at the SAME spot the old null-check already lived, so the calling function (`runPlanPhase`) grows by only one line net (patterns.md 'Biome cognitive complexity: use factory/helper extraction'). The new option field is a pure LABEL: it does not change how `selectIssueFn` is invoked or what it returns -- the caller must independently wire `selectIssueFn` to a target-aware selector (e.g. `() => selectPlanTargetFromFile(cwd, planTargetId, spawn)`) so the label and the actual selection logic stay in sync; nothing in `runPlanPhase` enforces that pairing. A non-audit passthrough branch keyed on `result.kind !== 'audit-blocked' && result.kind !== 'audit-approved'` (as in `runPlanPhaseWithReplan`) needs NO code change to pass the new kind through untouched; only its enumerating doc comment needs the new kind name added so it stays accurate.

- **Add a new ci-parity assertion as a separate function merged only at the `FromFile` boundary, not into the shared `checkParity` core (US-002, CAM-201, 2026-07-07)**: `scripts/check-ci-parity.ts` already had a `checkParity(workflowYaml, gates)` core covered by fixtures (`aligned.yml`, `unknown-script.yml`, `missing-gate.yml`) that deliberately have NO `oven-sh/setup-bun` step. Adding a second, unrelated assertion (the `bun-version-file: .bun-version` pin check) as a new top-level `checkBunVersionPin(workflowYaml, pinnedBunVersion)` function, merged into the results ONLY inside `checkParityFromFile` (`{ ok: parity.ok && bunPin.ok, errors: [...parity.errors, ...bunPin.errors] }`), means the pre-existing `checkParity` fixtures and tests need zero changes even though they'd otherwise fail the new pin check (they have no setup-bun step at all). Any future "one more CI-shape assertion" story should follow this shape: new pure function taking the already-injectable inputs (never re-reading the pin file itself; the caller passes `readPinnedBunVersion()` so tests inject `null` for the malformed-file case without touching disk), merged only at the file-loading boundary that real callers (the CLI entrypoint, `ci-workflow.test.ts`'s real-file test) go through.

- **Always-on vs conditional `--build-arg` pairs in the same `buildDockerBuildArgv` builder (US-003, CAM-150, 2026-07-07)**: `HOST_UID`/`HOST_GID` are CONDITIONAL (only emitted when both fields are present on `DockerBuildArgvOptions`, since host-mode callers never pass them), while `BUN_VERSION`/`NODE_VERSION` are UNCONDITIONAL (always resolved and emitted, defaulting to `readPinnedBunVersion()`/`readPinnedNodeVersion()` from `src/config/toolchain.ts`, US-001) because the Dockerfile's `FROM` line and Node-install `RUN` step hard-require them to exist for ANY build, host or container. When a builder function mixes both shapes, give each family its own resolver (`resolveBunVersion`/`resolveNodeVersion`, throwing loudly on a missing/malformed pin file rather than silently omitting the arg) instead of reusing the optional-pair-presence-check pattern for both; a test asserting "no `--build-arg` at all when opts are absent" must be narrowed to "no `HOST_UID=`/`HOST_GID=` token" once a second, unconditional `--build-arg` family is introduced, or it will fail for the wrong reason. The one production call site (`runWorkerContainer` inside `src/supervisor/worker-container.ts`, reused by `ensure-container.ts`) means both `test/supervisor/worker-container.test.ts` (pure-builder unit tests, inject explicit `bunVersion`/`nodeVersion` overrides so assertions never depend on the real repo-root pin files) and `test/supervisor/ensure-container.test.ts` (integration-level argv assertions through the full `ensureWorkerContainer` orchestration) need updating together whenever the argv shape changes.

- **Uncommitted prior-session work sitting in the tree at story start must be independently re-verified against the story's ACs before trusting it, not blindly committed (2026-07-07)**: a fresh implementer session found `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`, `src/supervisor/worker-container.ts`, and `test/supervisor/worker-container.test.ts` already fully edited but uncommitted (a previous session's work that never reached a commit, likely interrupted). Rather than assuming it was correct, this session re-ran every oracle from the story's `acceptanceCriteria` (all 4 file-assert/`bun test` oracles) plus the full `bun run check:all` spine from scratch, which surfaced one real regression the prior work introduced (a stale `ensure-container.test.ts` assertion asserting NO `--build-arg` at all, now false since toolchain args are unconditional) and one gate never run by the prior session (`check:file-size`, needing a budget-ceiling raise). Treat any uncommitted working-tree state found at session start as an unverified draft, not a completed handoff, even when it looks complete on read-through.

- **The CAM-186 bun-version-conditioned skip is RETIRED by CAM-201 (US-004, 2026-07-07)**: the version-probe + `skipIf` guards documented in the CAM-186 bullets above (`test/dashboard.test.ts`, `test/ui/init-setup-screen-keys.test.ts`) are gone; those tests now run unconditionally on every toolchain. What actually made them flaky was NOT primarily bun-version macrotask scheduling as CAM-186 assumed: `ink-testing-library`'s fake `Stdout` defines a `columns` getter (100) but no `rows` getter (`node_modules/ink-testing-library/build/index.js`), so `stdout.rows` is always falsy and Ink's `getWindowSize` (`node_modules/ink/build/utils.js`) falls through to the `terminal-size` package on EVERY render, which shells out to `tput` synchronously. That synchronous spawn is fast and invisible on a quiet machine but stalls unpredictably (measured: 1 to 3000+ ms) under the CPU contention of a full `bun test` run, which is what actually produced the "how many macrotask ticks are enough" variance. The real fix (`test/helpers/mock-terminal-size.ts`, `installTerminalSizeMock()`) stubs `terminal-size` via `mock.module` so ink-testing-library renders never spawn a subprocess; once that root cause is removed, a single macrotask tick reliably flushes a stdin-driven state update on bun 1.2.x too (verified: 15/15 trials). `mock.module` is process-global and NOT undone by `mock.restore()` in this bun version (empirically confirmed): call the installer idempotently from every file that needs it rather than trying to scope/reset it. As defense in depth (not a substitute for the mock), `test/helpers/flush-ink.ts` still exports `flushInk` (two ticks, for inter-keypress pacing) and `waitForFrame` (poll `lastFrame()` against a content predicate, bounded by a generous timeout) instead of asserting readiness after a fixed tick count: prefer polling on content over counting ticks for any future ink-testing-library assertion that follows a `stdin.write`.

- **Toolchain-version-skip guard gate: two-pass derived-variable tracing, not a name-only grep (US-005, CAM-201, 2026-07-07)**: `scripts/check-version-skips.ts` mirrors `check-debt-markers.ts`'s structure (exported pure `scanForVersionSkips(files)` + injectable file-list CLI seam) but detects a harder anti-pattern than a literal-token grep: the real cam/pr-66 incident (`bunVersionOk`) was TWO hops from `Bun.version` (`const [_bunMajorStr, _bunMinorStr] = Bun.version.split('.')` then `const bunVersionOk = parseInt(_bunMajorStr...) > 1 || ...`), and a differently-named future probe would slip through matching only the literal identifier `bunVersionOk`. Fix: `collectDerivedVars(text)` runs a small fixed-point loop (2 iterations) over every `const/let/var` declaration in the file, adding the LHS identifier(s) (handles destructure patterns `[a,b]` and `{a,b}` too) to a `derived` set whenever the RHS references `Bun.version`/`process.version`/`process.versions` directly OR references an identifier already in `derived` from a prior iteration; this generalizes to any rename or intermediate-variable chain up to depth 2. `findMatchingParenEnd` (balanced-paren scan) extracts each `.skipIf(`/`.skipUnless(`/`.todoIf(`/`.skip(` call's argument text so the derived-var/version-token check only looks inside the actual condition, not the whole line. Does NOT flag `process.platform`/`process.env`/capability probes (`spawnSync('tmux', ...)`, `Bun.which(...)`) since those never produce a `VERSION_TOKEN_RE` hit. Self-reference gotcha (CAM-60 precedent): the gate's OWN unit test file must not embed literal `Bun.version`/`process.version` substrings inline (even inside a JS string in a `.test.ts` file), since the real CLI scan globs `test/**/*.ts` and only excludes the `test/fixtures/` directory, not this test file itself; move every positive-case input into a `readFileSync`'d fixture under `test/fixtures/version-skip-guard/` instead of an inline string literal. One fixture (`process-versions-skipunless.ts`) needed a `// @ts-expect-error` since `test.skipUnless` is not part of bun:test's real public API (the guard treats skip-form names as textual patterns per the story's ACs, not runtime-verified calls); tsc still typechecks every file under `test/` (no tsconfig include/exclude for fixtures), so a fixture using a nonexistent API must suppress the resulting type error rather than being excluded from typecheck.

- **`bunfig.toml`'s `[test].timeout` (and the `setDefaultTimeout()`-in-preload workaround) is silently ignored on bun 1.2.23 (US-006, CAM-201, 2026-07-07)**: empirically confirmed by writing a throwaway fixture (a test that sleeps 6s with `bunfig.toml` setting `[test] timeout = 8000`): the test still times out at exactly 5000ms. A `preload` script calling `setDefaultTimeout(N)` also only takes effect for the FIRST test file bun loads in a run (module-state caching bug), not subsequent files. The only reliable way to give a specific real-subprocess/real-git integration test more headroom is the per-test third argument: `test('name', fn, { timeout: 20_000 })` (also works as the third arg to `test.each([...])('name', fn, { timeout: 20_000 })`). Root cause discovered while gate-keeping this story (CAM-202 dogfooding precedent, US-002 bullet above): a handful of real-subprocess integration tests (`test/integration/journal-append-on-main.test.ts`, `test/integration/issue-file-on-main.test.ts`, `test/integration/ship-finalize-dirty-prd.test.ts` Case A/B pairs, `test/commands/setup-merge-mode.test.ts` AC-7's `test.each`, `test/interactivity/interactivity.test.ts`'s Esc-keypress test) were measured failing the default 5000ms bun-test timeout in roughly 30-50% of full-suite runs under this environment's transient CPU contention (confirmed via ~30 repeated `bun test` runs before the fix, 0 fail in 15 consecutive runs after adding `{ timeout: 20_000 }` to each). This is the same flake CLASS as the CAM-186/CAM-201 `terminal-size`/`tput` subprocess-stall root cause above, just manifesting in real `git`/`claude`-PATH-probe subprocess calls instead of ink renders; the fix pattern generalizes: any newly-written or newly-discovered real-subprocess integration test should get an explicit generous `{ timeout: 20_000 }` up front rather than relying on the 5000ms default, since `bunfig.toml` cannot raise the floor globally in this bun version.

- **Fail-closed container toolchain assert lands pure/injectable and inert, mirroring the CAM-150 preflight-container.ts precedent (US-006, CAM-201, 2026-07-07)**: `assertContainerToolchain` (`src/supervisor/toolchain-assert.ts`) probes `docker exec <containerName> bun --version` / `... node --version` via an injectable `ToolchainExecFn` (mirrors `ConfigSpawnFn`'s `{stdout,stderr,exitCode}` shape from `container-config.ts`) and compares against `readPinnedBunVersion`/`readPinnedNodeVersion` (`src/config/toolchain.ts`, US-001). Comparison: bun is exact-match (no `v` prefix in `bun --version` output, confirmed against Bun's own docs/GitHub issues); node strips a leading `v` before comparing (node always prefixes `v`). A missing/malformed pin file (`readPinnedXxxVersion` returning `null`) is folded into the mismatch as `expected: null` rather than treated as "nothing to check" -- this is the fail-closed behavior the story's AC requires (an unreadable pin must never silently pass). Never throws: a non-zero `docker exec` exit becomes `actual: null` in the mismatch. The `'toolchain-mismatch'` reason string was added to `PreflightResult`'s reason union in `preflight-container.ts` (satisfying the file-assert oracle) but the branch that actually COMPUTES it inside `preflightWorkerContainer` is deferred to US-007, exactly the same "tested-but-inert, wired later" shape `preflight-container.ts` itself used before its own CAM-150 wiring story.

- **Auto-rebuild-on-mismatch wiring is TWO seams, not one: boot-once AND per-cycle (US-007, CAM-192/CAM-201, 2026-07-07)**: `ensureWorkerContainer` (ensure-container.ts) now calls `assertContainerToolchain` unconditionally after its 4-branch reconcile (mirrors the firewall/config unconditional-apply pattern): on a mismatch it does `docker rm -f` + `runWorkerContainer` (rebuild, reusing the SAME `opts.build` HOST_UID/HOST_GID/BUN_VERSION path as the image-stale branch) + re-assert, upgrading `action` to `'rebuilt'`; a rebuild failure or a still-mismatching re-assert throws the new `ToolchainMismatchError` (toolchain-assert.ts, same `{mismatches}`-carrying shape precedent as `FirewallError.stderrTail`/`ContainerConfigError.stderrTail`). The GOTCHA this story exists to fix: `ensureContainerFn` (the mutating reconcile+rebuild closure) was previously invoked ONLY ONCE, at sidecar boot (`runSidecar` in sidecar.ts) -- `preflightWorkerContainer` (the READ-ONLY per-cycle checker, called every dispatch via `RunSupervisorOptions.preflightContainerFn`, wired in host.ts) never mutates, so a staleness/mismatch detected mid-loop would block forever until an operator restarted the sidecar (this WAS the CAM-192 bug). Fix: added a second seam, `RunSupervisorOptions.ensureContainerFn` (loop.ts), invoked at the SAME per-dispatch site immediately BEFORE `preflightContainerFn` so any reconcile/rebuild lands before the read-only check observes it; production wiring in host.ts's `buildSupervisorOptions` reuses the exact same `makeProductionEnsureContainerFn(cwd)` closure sidecar.ts calls at boot (single source, never two independently-drifting reconcile implementations). `FirewallError`/`ContainerConfigError`/`ToolchainMismatchError` thrown from this per-cycle call are caught in `runSupervisor` and treated exactly like a preflight not-ready result (blocked status, container-named detail string, best-effort escalateFn fire-and-forget, respawn-pane never called) -- any OTHER thrown error type is rethrown, not swallowed. Lesson for future container-fail-closed features: a boot-time-only guard is NOT equivalent to a fail-closed guard for a long-lived sidecar process; anything that must react to a merged main-branch change without an operator restarting `cam run` needs the per-cycle seam, not just the boot seam.

- **Docker exec exit-code propagation confirmed via web search (US-007, 2026-07-07)**: per the `docker-container-exec` manpage and current community docs, `docker exec` is specified to propagate the exit code of the executed command (chroot-standard semantics on failure, e.g. 126 for "could not be invoked"); documented historical bugs where specific Docker/Compose versions returned 0 regardless of the inner command's real exit code are platform/version-specific, not the current spec. This is the same assumption `container-firewall.ts`/`container-config.ts` already relied on (their `FirewallSpawnFn`/`ConfigSpawnFn` seams treat a non-zero `docker exec` exit as a real failure); `ToolchainExecFn` in `toolchain-assert.ts` follows the identical convention.

- **Renovate config: describe the CI-gating invariant in prose, never repeat the literal escape-hatch token (US-008, CAM-201, 2026-07-07)**: `renovate.json`'s `packageRules[].description` fields explaining "why automerge stays safe without `ignoreTests`" must NOT contain the literal substring `ignoreTests` in a negated form (e.g. "no ignoreTests") -- the story's own negative file-assert oracle (`! grep -q 'ignoreTests' renovate.json`) greps the whole file byte-for-byte, including description prose, not just active config keys. Phrase the invariant without naming the forbidden key (e.g. "gated on the required CI status check") instead. `config:recommended` already enables the `bun-version` and `asdf` managers by default (confirmed via WebSearch against docs.renovatebot.com), so the four-manager story only needed one `packageRules` entry per manager (matchManagers + matchUpdateTypes:[patch,minor,major] + automerge:true), not a manager-enablement block; `enabled` was set explicitly to `true` per rule anyway so the file-assert "none of them disabled" AC is self-evidently satisfied by grep/read, not just by the recommended-preset default. One-time OPERATOR follow-up (out of scope for the autonomous implementer, call out at ship time): installing the Renovate GitHub App on the repo, and confirming the CI workflow is a required branch-protection check on main (Renovate automerge only proceeds once required status checks are green).

- **A pinned `ARG NODE_VERSION` tarball install shifts npm's global-bin dir, silently taking a LATER `npm install -g` off PATH (US-R3-002, CAM-150, 2026-07-07)**: when a Dockerfile installs Node.js from an upstream `nodejs.org` binary tarball into a custom prefix (e.g. `/usr/local/lib/nodejs`) instead of via `apt`, only the tarball's OWN `node`/`npm`/`npx` binaries get manually symlinked into `/usr/local/bin`; npm's own global-bin resolution (confirmed against npmjs.com docs: "bin files are linked to `{prefix}/bin`") still points at `<prefix>/bin` (`/usr/local/lib/nodejs/bin`), not `/usr/local/bin`. Any LATER `RUN npm install -g <pkg>` step (e.g. `@anthropic-ai/claude-code`) installs its bin script into that same prefix dir, which is NOT on `PATH` unless something adds it. A `docker exec <container> claude ...` caller then fails `claude: command not found` even though the package installed successfully at build time (`npm install -g` itself exits 0; the break is PATH resolution, not installation). Fix: add `ENV PATH=/usr/local/lib/nodejs/bin:$PATH` immediately after the `npm install -g` RUN step (broader than a per-binary symlink: any future global npm package resolves too). This is a general gotcha for ANY Dockerfile that mixes "Node from a pinned tarball" with "a later ad-hoc global npm install" -- the fix must be re-applied if the prefix path or the install step's position ever changes.

- **A test that reads the real repo-root pin file must assert format, not the literal pinned value (US-001, CAM-245, 2026-07-10)**: `test/config/toolchain.test.ts`'s two "reads the real repo-root ... file with default args" tests call `readPinnedBunVersion()`/`readPinnedNodeVersion()` with NO injected fake path/reader, so they read the actual `.bun-version`/`.tool-versions` at the repo root. Asserting `toBe('1.3.13')` there couples PR CI to the exact current pin: a routine Renovate toolchain bump (CAM-201 automerge) changes the real file, and the literal-pin assertion goes red on every bump even though the function behaves correctly. Fix: assert the function's own contract instead of a snapshot -- `toMatch(/^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$/)` (exact-semver, matching what `readPinnedBunVersion`/`readPinnedNodeVersion` already reject ranges/prefixes/`'latest'` against). Every OTHER test in that file that injects an explicit fake path + reader keeps `toBe('1.3.13')`/`toBe('22.23.1')` unchanged -- those never see a real-file bump, so the literal is correct and durable there. General rule: any test exercising a function's zero-arg/default-path branch against a real, externally-bumpable repo file should assert shape/format, not a point-in-time value; fake-injected branches of the same function keep exact literals.

- **In-process CLI subcommand that must both call an already-tested `xxxOnMain` primitive AND print a machine-parseable result line: inject the primitive-level fn, not a full top-level bypass (US-002, CAM-210, 2026-07-07)**: `cam issue close <id>` reuses `closeIssueOnMain` (issue-specify.ts, tested independently since US-001) but ALSO needs its own new dispatch-level behavior tested (printHint on success, a `CAM_ISSUE_RESULT=<id>` / `CAM_ISSUE_RESULT=ERROR reason=<reason>` machine line, exit-code mapping). Giving `IssueDispatchDeps` a full top-level bypass (`closeFn: () => Promise<number>`, mirroring `fileLocalFn`/`issueListFn`) would let tests assert ROUTING ISOLATION but would bypass the printing/exit-mapping logic entirely, leaving it untested. Instead, inject at the PRIMITIVE level: `closeIssueOnMainFn?: (id: string) => CloseIssueOnMainOutcome` (same name/shape already used in `ship-pr.ts`'s `ShipPrOptions`). The dispatch branch's printing/exit-mapping code always runs, in every test, with a canned `CloseIssueOnMainOutcome` swapped in for the real git calls; this simultaneously proves routing isolation (fake never touches `runIssueFn`/`fileLocalFn`) AND exercises the real print/exit logic per outcome variant. Production default reuses `defaultCloseIssueFn(cwd, id)` (`src/release/post-merge.ts`, already the single real-spawnSync wiring reused by `sidecar.ts`) instead of duplicating a `spawnSync`+`clock` closure inline (jscpd gate). General rule: when a new CLI branch's OWN untested logic sits strictly BETWEEN an already-tested lower primitive and the dispatch return value, inject the seam at the primitive boundary, not above the new logic.

- **Two valid places for the primitive-bypass seam depending on where the print/exit-mapping logic lives (US-001, CAM-213, 2026-07-07)**: CAM-210's `cam issue close/abandon` injects the `xxxOnMainFn` primitive bypass directly in `index.ts`'s `IssueDispatchDeps` because the printing/exit-mapping code lives in `dispatchIssue` itself. `cam spec --persist` (mirroring `cam spec --write-docs`, both CAM-118-style) keeps the printing/exit-mapping code inside `spec.ts`'s `runSpecPersist`/`runSpecWriteDocs` instead, so the primitive bypass (`persistFn?: (payload: unknown) => SpecifyIssueOnMainOutcome`) lives on `SpecPersistOptions` in `spec.ts`, one level BELOW `dispatchSpec`'s own top-level bypass (`SpecDispatchDeps.persistFn?: (id: string) => Promise<number>`, routing-isolation only). Both seams are correct for their own file; the rule is: put the primitive-level bypass in whichever file owns the printing/exit-mapping code, not always in `index.ts`. `CAM_SPEC_RESULT=<id> sha=<sha>` intentionally includes `sha=` in the machine line (unlike `CAM_ISSUE_RESULT=<id>`, which omits sha from the machine line and only puts it in the human `printHint`); follow each story's own acceptance-criteria wording for the machine-line shape rather than assuming the sibling command's exact format.

- **Retiring an inline-TS-snippet persist step from a self-hosted command doc: mirror the sibling safe-quoting section, then collapse the guard-reason table into one row pointing at the CLI's own result line (US-002, CAM-213, 2026-07-07)**: when a prior story (here US-001) adds an in-process `--persist`/`--write-*` CLI mode next to an already-documented sibling mode, the FOLLOW-UP doc-rewrite story should copy the sibling section's exact safe-quoting prose (single-quoted heredoc `<<'EOF'`/single-quoted `echo`, explicit "never unquoted" warning, CAM-106 citation) rather than inventing new phrasing, and state the handback contract as the command's OWN stdout (`CAM_SPEC_RESULT=<id> sha=<sha>` success / `CAM_SPEC_RESULT=ERROR reason=<reason>` failure) instead of re-describing each guard's error text. In the doc's error-handling table, this lets every guard/validation reason the CLI's discriminated union can return (`not-found`, `wrong-stage`, `not-open`, `invalid-spec`, `invalid-wsjf`, `integrity-error`, `diverged`, `detached-head`, `missing-main`, `invalid-json`) collapse into a SINGLE row ("Stop, print the `CAM_..._RESULT=ERROR reason=<reason>` line; report to the operator, never retry silently") instead of one row per reason: the doc no longer needs to enumerate or duplicate error text the CLI already owns and prints verbatim. The file-assert oracle for "no inline snippet" only forbids the literal import line (`grep -q "! grep -q \"import { xxxOnMain\""`), so prose can still reference the underlying deterministic-writer primitive by name for context; only the executable snippet moves to a stdin pipe.

- **`payload as SomeType` on an `unknown` JSON.parse result never throws by itself; the throw comes from the FIRST property dereference, and TypeScript casts do not add a runtime guard (US-R1-001, CAM-213, 2026-07-07)**: a stdin-JSON entrypoint that does `JSON.parse` then immediately spreads `(payload as Shape).field` into a downstream call (rather than passing `payload` through wholesale for the callee to validate, the `runSpecWriteDocs`/`writeDomainDocsOnMain` pattern) crashes with an uncaught `TypeError: null is not an object` for the single valid-JSON, wrong-shape input `null` (and would also crash for `undefined`, though `JSON.parse` never produces that). Every OTHER primitive top-level JSON shape (`5`, `"hi"`, `[]`, `{}`) is silent on property access (JS autoboxes primitives; arrays/objects just yield `undefined` fields) and reaches the callee's own internal validation harmlessly, which is why only the `null` case was missed in the original review pass despite four other shapes being explicitly tested. Fix pattern: add one shape guard immediately after the `JSON.parse` try/catch, symmetric with `validateSpec`'s own opening check (`payload === null || typeof payload !== 'object' || Array.isArray(payload)`), emitting the SAME machine-readable error line/exit code the callee's own worst-case validation failure would produce (here `CAM_SPEC_RESULT=ERROR reason=invalid-spec`, reusing the existing `invalid-spec` token rather than inventing a new one) BEFORE branching into either the real callee or any full-bypass test seam (`persistFn`) -- placing the guard before the branch, not only in the real-callee arm, means a bypass-seam test can assert the callee is never even reached for a malformed top-level shape.

- **Retrofitting a machine-line convention onto a PRE-EXISTING whole-branch injectable: add the primitive-level seam alongside the old bypass, never replace it (US-001, CAM-212, 2026-07-07)**: `cam issue --file-local` predates the CAM-210 `CAM_ISSUE_RESULT` convention and already had a whole-branch bypass (`IssueDispatchDeps.fileLocalFn: () => Promise<number>`) whose existing US-003 branch-isolation tests inject bare exit-code fakes (`async () => 0/1`) and assert nothing about stdout. Retrofitting the print/exit-mapping convention (mirroring close/abandon) CANNOT replace `fileLocalFn`'s role (that would break every old test asserting routing isolation via a full-branch fake) and CANNOT be added only inside the default closure the old tests never exercise (the new logic would be untestable via per-outcome fakes, same problem CAM-210 solved for close/abandon). The fix layers two seams: (1) `fileLocalFn` (if present) still wins outright, unchanged priority, unchanged old-test behavior; (2) when absent, a new default path (`runFileLocalDefault`) owns the stdout convention and consults two FINER seams introduced in the same story -- `createLocalIssueOnMainFn` (primitive-level fake for the `createLocalIssueOnMain` call, same shape rule as the CAM-210 bullet above) and `readStdinFn` (fake stdin reader, avoids real `Bun.stdin.text()` in unit tests). A stdin-JSON-parse error is NOT a member of the primitive's own outcome union (`CreateLocalIssueOnMainOutcome` has no `invalid-json` reason) so its `CAM_ISSUE_RESULT=ERROR reason=invalid-json` token is emitted directly in the dispatch-level code, before the primitive fake is ever invoked -- provable by asserting the injected primitive fake's call-flag stays false. General rule: when a NEW cross-cutting convention (a machine-readable exit protocol) needs to reach an OLD branch that already has a top-level bypass seam predating the convention, add the fine-grained seam underneath the existing coarse one instead of removing or repurposing it; the coarse seam becomes "full override," the fine seam becomes "default-path customization," and both must be documented as mutually exclusive in the same doc comment.
