- **Regression-locking a deliberate absence-of-convention decision: comment at the branch, test with a realistic fake, negative-grep the read-only internals (US-002, CAM-212, 2026-07-07)**: once a cross-cutting machine-line convention exists (`CAM_ISSUE_RESULT`, CAM-210/212) and covers most-but-not-all branches of a dispatcher, the branch that deliberately opts OUT (`cam issue list`, a read over many issues with no single id to report) needs its own regression lock or a future auditor will "fix" the perceived omission. Three cheap, durable artifacts do this without touching the excluded branch's internals: (1) an in-code comment at the dispatch branch itself naming the general rule the exclusion follows (here: "CAM_ISSUE_RESULT is a mutation-outcome contract"), satisfying a `grep -qi` oracle; (2) a `bun test` regression test that injects a realistic fake for the excluded branch's own seam (one that writes ordinary human-readable output) and asserts the captured stdout contains no trace of the convention's token, proving the DISPATCHER wrapper itself adds nothing, not that the excluded branch's real implementation is silent; (3) a negative grep oracle (`! grep -q '<TOKEN>' <read-only-internals-file>`) that checks the excluded branch's own implementation file directly, without editing it. Splitting the proof this way (comment for intent, dispatcher test for wrapper behavior, negative grep for internals) keeps the change surgically confined to the dispatcher + its test file even though the invariant nominally spans both.

- **Fakes-only regression suites cannot prove a real state-file clobber chain; extract the inline production closure into a named exported factory so a test can wire it standalone (US-002, CAM-191, 2026-07-07)**: `test/supervisor/auto-chain.test.ts` proved the CAM-191 ordering fix at the UNIT level (fake `runSupervisorFn` returns a canned `SupervisorResult`, fake `clearActive`/`readLoopPhaseFn` are no-ops or hard-coded closures), which is exactly why it never caught the original bug: the clobber only reproduces when `clearActive`'s real implicit `phase:idle` rewrite and the real `onProgress` unlink-on-complete actually touch the SAME file on disk in the SAME order the production code runs them. Two things made a real-writer test practical without dragging in the whole tmux-dependent `buildSupervisorOptions` bag: (1) `makeClearActive`/`makeSetPhaseFn`/`makeReadLoopPhase` (`sidecar.ts`) were already plain `(claudeDir, cwd) => closure` factories with no tmux dependency, so they could be imported and driven directly against a temp dir; (2) `onProgress` was NOT previously a standalone factory (it was inlined in `buildSupervisorOptions`), so it was surgically extracted into an exported `makeOnProgress(stateFilePath, stateFileBase)` in `host.ts` (zero behavior change -- `buildSupervisorOptions` now just calls it) specifically so the test could get the real unlink-on-complete write without also constructing `teardownWorkerPaneFn`/`spawn`/`capturePane` (which, left real, would risk sending a stray `tmux -L cam kill-pane` to a live developer session). General rule: a "real writer" regression test is only tractable when every writer it needs is already a small parameter-scoped factory; if one production writer is still inlined in a larger factory, extract it (verbatim, not rewritten) as its own export rather than reimplementing its logic by hand in the test (a hand copy can silently drift from the real code and stop being a regression lock). Driving TWO consecutive `runSidecarLoop` ticks inside ONE call (rather than two separate invocations) works because the shipping-phase branch check (`loopPhase === 'shipping'`) runs unconditionally at the top of every `while(true)` iteration, before the `active !== true` idle branch; bound the loop with a `sleepFn` that no-ops on its first call and throws an escape sentinel on the second (same pattern as `test/sidecar-ship-phase.test.ts`), letting the natural next iteration observe the previous tick's real state-file write.

- **Gate a config-driven dispatcher at its ONE-TIME builder, never inside its per-tick closure; distinguish "permanent boot-time config mismatch" from "transient per-tick precondition" (US-001, CAM-208, 2026-07-07)**: `buildMetaLoopFn` (`sidecar.ts`) resolves `meta_loop`/`worker_isolation` ONCE per sidecar boot and returns either a dispatch closure or `undefined`; it is never re-invoked per idle tick (that would re-read the toml file every ~2s for a value that cannot change mid-run). This makes it the correct home for a "config X doesn't support mode Y" gate: reading `worker_isolation` here and returning `undefined` (skipping `buildProductionDispatchFn` entirely) when it isn't `'container'` guarantees the boot-time warning fires exactly once, with zero extra plumbing, since the function itself only runs once. The opposite gotcha: do NOT add this kind of gate inside a per-tick closure (`evaluateDrainPreconditions`, `handlePreconditionRefuse`) even though that closure also has isolation-aware logic -- that closure's job is the TRANSIENT case (container present but preflight not yet ready), which legitimately re-checks every tick and must keep re-checking. Conflating the two would either spam the permanent-mismatch warning every tick (if gated per-tick) or silently drop the transient refuse-and-retry semantics (if the permanent gate absorbed the transient one). Rule of thumb: a config value read once at process/loop-builder construction time gates with a one-shot warn + `undefined`; a config value (or external state) that can legitimately change between ticks gates inside the per-tick evaluator with its existing dedup/refuse machinery, and the two must never share a code path. Test-wise, since the builder only runs once, the only way to assert "warns exactly once" is to export the builder (`buildMetaLoopFn` was previously module-private) and call it directly against a tmpdir project.toml fixture (real fs read via the existing `readMetaLoop`/`readWorkerIsolation` DI-by-path convention), then separately drive the returned (here `undefined`) result through N ticks of the real `runSidecarLoop` to prove the per-tick path adds no further events/warnings.

- **Vocabulary-only stories: mirror the sibling optional-counter field verbatim, and expect the file-size ratchet to trip on the doc-comment-heavy events.ts even with a tiny diff (US-001, CAM-170, 2026-07-07)**: when a story adds a new `WorkerEventKind` + detail interface that is purely additive (no wiring, no emit call site yet; the emit is a later story), the fastest correct implementation is to find the most recently added sibling of the same shape (`updateBranchCount` for `MergeWatchState`, `MergeWatchStalledEventDetail` for the event-detail interface family) and copy its three-site pattern exactly: (1) interface field as `optional?: number` with a doc comment stating "absent = 0 (fresh state)", (2) a `typeof obj['field'] === 'number'` guard in the reader, (3) `field: state.field ?? 0` (always written) in the writer. Because `src/supervisor/events.ts` carries a long descriptive JSDoc block above `WorkerEventKind` that every new merge-watch event kind must extend, even a ~15-line additive diff reliably pushes the file past its `file-size-budget.json` ceiling; treat hitting that ratchet as expected for this file specifically, not a sign of scope creep, and follow the raise-and-retry procedure (bump the ceiling to the new `wc -l` count, prepend a dated entry to `_ref`, stage before re-running `bun run check:all`).

- **Migrating an injectable poll function from `T | null` to a discriminated result: wrap at the test-helper boundary, not at every call site; extract the branch body into a named helper to dodge the biome complexity ratchet (US-002, CAM-170, 2026-07-07)**: when a story upgrades `type PollFn = (arg) => T | null` to `type PollFn = (arg) => { ok: true; value: T } | { ok: false; stderr: string }`, the test suite's dozens of `pollFn: makeSeqPollFn([FIXTURE_A, FIXTURE_B])` call sites do NOT need touching if `makeSeqPollFn` itself wraps each raw fixture in `{ ok: true, value: s }` internally (parameter type becomes `T[]`, not `Array<Result>`); only the handful of inline `const pollFn: PollFn = () => RAW_FIXTURE` sites (bare arrows returning the raw success/failure value directly, bypassing the sequence helper) need a `ok(RAW_FIXTURE)` wrapper, found exhaustively via `bunx tsc --noEmit` (every stale site surfaces as a `TS2322: Type 'X' is not assignable to type 'Result'` error, which is a more complete audit than grepping arrow-function patterns by hand). Add tiny `ok(value)`/`err(stderr)` test-local wrapper functions once, next to the sequence helper, instead of writing `{ ok: true, ... }` object literals inline at every site. On the production side, adding the discriminated branch (increment-a-counter + edge-triggered-event-emit) directly inline in the state-machine tick function is exactly the kind of ~20-line branch that trips `noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity`/`noExcessiveLinesPerFunction`; extract it as a named `handleXxxError(state, ..., opts): Result` helper BEFORE running `bun run check:all` (the file already has 3-4 sibling `handleYyyStatus` helpers extracted for the same reason -- follow that existing convention rather than discovering the ratchet by trial and error). The same extraction is needed on the production spawnSync-parsing side (`parseGhPollResult`) when the inline arrow also grows a stderr-surfacing branch; a hygiene test that regex-matches the old inline arrow body (`ghPollFnMatch?.[0]).toContain(...)`) must be re-pointed at the extracted helper's own source text (`source.toContain('function parseGhPollResult')`) once the logic moves out, not left asserting against the now-empty delegating call.

- **`content.split('\n').length` (file-size gate) is `wc -l + 1`, not `wc -l`, when raising a budget ceiling to match a freshly-edited file's exact new size (US-002, CAM-170, 2026-07-07)**: reconfirms the existing bullet above with a concrete trip: after editing `src/commands/sidecar.ts`, `wc -l` reported 2186 but `bun scripts/check-file-sizes.ts` still failed with `2187 exceeds budget ceiling of 2186` because the gate counts `content.split('\n').length`, which is one more than `wc -l` for any trailing-newline-terminated file (the standard case). Always run the actual gate script (`bun scripts/check-file-sizes.ts`) to read back the exact number to write into the budget, rather than trusting `wc -l` and guessing +0.

- **Fixed truncation budget decoupled from terminal width; and lastFrame() line-matching must disambiguate a shared substring across sections (US-001, CAM-66, 2026-07-07)**: when a row layout packs multiple segments sharing horizontal space (icon + id + title + tokens column, `StoryRow` in `Dashboard.tsx`), truncating the title against the LIVE terminal width (e.g. Ink's `<Text wrap="truncate-end">`) is wrong whenever the segment is a plain string concatenated into one `<Text>` (the selected/accent-bg row here): container-width truncation would eat the trailing tokens text too, since Ink has no way to know where the title segment ends inside the combined string. The correct fix is a small exported pure helper (`truncateTitle(title, maxWidth)`, string-in/string-out, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`-safe via `.slice`, never indexed) applied ONLY to the title segment, with a fixed numeric budget constant (not derived from `useStdout().columns`): this guarantees the tokens column always survives regardless of pane width, and keeps the helper directly unit-testable with zero Ink render needed. Separately, an ink-testing-library assertion that searches `lastFrame().split('\n')` for a line containing the story id (e.g. `'US-001'`) can false-match a DIFFERENT section that also prints the id verbatim (here, the Loop panel's `story    US-001` summary row printed above the Stories list) whenever the current/selected story equals the row under test; disambiguate by filtering on a marker unique to the target row (e.g. `line.includes(STORY_TOKENS_PLACEHOLDER)`, which only the Stories-list row renders), not on the id substring alone. A related gotcha for asserting an UNTRUNCATED (intentionally full-length) string that Ink word-wraps across the fake terminal width: normalize with `frame.replace(/\n\s+/g, ' ')` before the `toContain(fullString)` assertion, since Ink's wrap re-breaks at word boundaries with a newline + indent, not by dropping characters.

- **`ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>` is unsafe as an injectable timer-handle type in this repo: use `unknown` + narrow default wrappers (US-002, CAM-66, 2026-07-07)**: this project has BOTH `bun-types` (tsconfig `types: ["bun"]`, ambient global `setTimeout`/`clearTimeout` returning Bun's `Timer`) and `@types/node` installed in `node_modules/@types` (ambient `NodeJS.Timeout`) in scope at once, purely because a file happens to `import` from a `node:*` module. Typing an injectable-timer option as `(cb, ms) => ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>` / `(id: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>) => void` compiles inconsistently depending on which ambient overload TS resolves at each usage site, producing `TS2739: Type 'Timer' is missing ... from type 'Timeout'` at the call site that assigns the real `setTimeout`'s result into the typed variable. Fix: type the handle as `unknown` in the public option shape (the factory never inspects it, only round-trips it to the paired `clearTimeoutFn`), and fence the one unavoidable cast inside two tiny single-signature default wrapper functions (`defaultSetTimeoutFn(cb, ms): unknown { return setTimeout(cb, ms); }` / `defaultClearTimeoutFn(id: unknown) { clearTimeout(id as Parameters<typeof clearTimeout>[0]); }`) rather than casting at every call site or unioning the default into the injected option's inferred type (the latter produces `TS2769: No overload matches this call` because `??` against an overloaded ambient function infers a union call signature, not a normalized one). Pattern proven in `makeResizeClearer` (`src/commands/dashboard.ts`).

- **A per-cycle lock's `startedAt` cannot double as a per-process session-start timestamp; persist a dedicated once-per-process marker written before the poll loop starts (US-001, PR-83, 2026-07-07)**: `acquireSupervisorLock` (`src/supervisor/lock.ts`) is called once per ACTIVE cycle inside `runSidecarLoop`'s `while(true)` (acquired right before dispatching `runSupervisor`, released in a `finally` right after), and it unconditionally rewrites `SupervisorLockInfo.startedAt` to "now" whenever the caller's own pid re-acquires its own prior lock (the `info.pid !== pid` guard only protects against a DIFFERENT pid). So the lock's `startedAt` resets on every fresh story-implementation cycle within the same sidecar process and cannot be reused as "how long has this sidecar session been running". Fix: a tiny dedicated module (`src/supervisor/session-start.ts`, mirrors `sidecar-pid.ts`'s file-per-concern shape: one named constant + write/read, `JSON.parse` + discriminator-field shape guard, never throws) whose write call site lives at the very top of the sidecar entrypoint (`runSidecar` in `sidecar.ts`), BEFORE `runContainerEnsureGuard` and BEFORE `buildSidecarLoopDeps`/the loop starts. Because `runSidecar()` executes exactly once per OS process (the `cam sidecar` subcommand dispatch, see `index.ts`), an unconditional write there is trivially "once at process startup" with no idempotency guard needed; the value is stable across every subsequent active/idle cycle and state-file rewrite simply because nothing inside the poll loop ever touches that file. `readSnapshot` (`dashboard.ts`) must resolve the marker path as `join(cwd, '.claude', ...)` (the PROJECT-local `.claude/`), not the `claudeDir` option already in scope there (that one points at the transcript HOME dir, `~/.claude`, a same-named but unrelated concept -- confirm which is which by grep, do not assume). Test the "does not reset" property at the `writeSessionStartFn` call-count level (assert exactly 1 call per `runSidecar()` invocation regardless of how many cycles an injected loop fn simulates), not by trying to drive multiple real active/idle transitions through the real loop.

- **Session-window token summation: `undefined` must mean "no total to show", collapsing both "log absent" and "log present but nothing matched" into one signal (US-002, PR-83, 2026-07-07)**: when a pure helper sums `.claude/cam-worker-events.jsonl` 'tokens' events bounded by a timestamp (`ts >= sessionStartIso`, the CAM-131 `extractTokensFromLine`-style formula: `inputTokens + outputTokens + cacheReadTokens + cacheCreationTokens`), do NOT default the accumulator to `0` and return it unconditionally -- track a `matched` boolean alongside the running total and return `undefined` when nothing matched. This makes "event log missing" (`jsonl === null`) and "log present but no event falls in the session window" (freshly-started session, no worker has run yet) collapse into the SAME renderer signal (omit the row), rather than the second case silently showing a literal `0` that looks like a real (if boring) measurement. `'cycle-tokens'` aggregate events (CAM-131) are excluded for free by filtering `kind === 'tokens'` before summing -- no separate exclusion check is needed since the aggregate event's own `kind` never matches. `readSnapshot` should gate this summation behind the SAME `sessionStartIso !== null && Number.isFinite(sessionStartMs)` check already used for `sessionStartedAtMs` (US-001 precedent) rather than re-deriving the bound a second way; reuse the raw ISO string (not the parsed ms) as the helper's second argument so the helper does its own `Date.parse` once, consistent with how it parses each event's own `ts`.

- **PRD-authoring hazard: a consolidation story's occurrence-cap AC can silently contradict a pre-existing pinned-count static-grep test (US-001, CAM-215, 2026-07-08, blocked 4x)**: when a story's acceptance criteria say "reduce call-sites of X to at most N" (a grep-count oracle over the *source* file) AND separately require an existing test file to stay byte-identical vs origin/main and fully pass, always check whether that unchanged test file contains a static file-assert pinning the PRE-refactor occurrence count (e.g. `src.split('someFn(').length - 1 === 3`, guarding a *different*, now-superseded property from an earlier story). A consolidation that legitimately satisfies the new low-count AC will deterministically fail the old pinned-count test, and no code-level workaround exists: the two ACs are mutually exclusive by construction, not a difficulty to engineer around. This is a planning-time defect, not an implementation one -- an implementer session must not "interpret" its way past it (e.g. by claiming the old test is superseded and editing it anyway, which violates the literal "unchanged" AC). Verify the contradiction is real (re-run the pinned test against the would-be-consolidated source, read the exact failing assertion) before declaring `BLOCKED_AMBIGUITY`, and require a PRD amendment (explicitly scope the old pinned test as editable, or convert its pin to a runtime spy-count assertion so it survives consolidation by construction) rather than re-dispatching another implementer session at the same contradiction.

- **Removing a catch-all swallow from an I/O seam requires auditing EVERY caller reached from an unguarded `await` in a long-lived loop tick, not just the ones the PRD note names by line number (US-001, CAM-115, 2026-07-08)**: when a seam like `selectPlannableFromFile`/`selectPlanTargetFromFile` (`src/issues/select.ts`) stops swallowing read/parse errors into a false `null`, every caller that used to rely on that swallow becomes a new crash surface. `runSidecarLoop`'s idle tick (`src/supervisor/loop.ts`) wraps `runPlanPhaseFn`/`runShipPhaseFn` in a local try/catch but calls `await opts.runMetaLoopObserveFn()` completely unguarded; BOTH the `meta_loop=observe` closure (`makeProductionMetaLoopObserveFn`) and the `meta_loop=auto` dispatch closure (`makeProductionMetaLoopDispatchFn`, via `buildProductionDispatchFn`'s `selectFn`/`selectTargetFn`) share that exact same slot, so a fix scoped only to the function the PRD note explicitly names leaves a structurally-identical sibling call site exposed. Fix pattern: make the seam's caller closure accept an injectable selector param (`selectFn: () => T | null = () => realSeam(cwd)`) so a test can inject a throwing fake without needing a real corrupted-file fixture (spawnSync does NOT throw for ENOENT/bad-cwd/git-missing in practice -- it returns `.error`/non-zero exit, so triggering a REAL production throw through the actual git plumbing is impractical; DI is the only practical test path), wrap the call in a local try/catch that logs via the existing `WorkerEventLogger` (reuse the `'sidecar-exit'` event kind already used for the sibling plan/ship outer-guards -- it means "a safety-net boundary caught+logged a crash without rethrowing", not "the process exited"), and return before calling any downstream decision function (e.g. `observeDecide`) so the corrupted-vs-drained conflation the story is fixing can never happen even transiently. When a file-assert oracle greps a source file for a literal word (e.g. `! grep -q 'catch' src/issues/select.ts`), a docstring merely MENTIONING the word "catch" in prose (not code) still trips the oracle -- reword to a synonym ("guarding their own boundary") rather than assuming the oracle only matches code constructs.

- **Closing the sibling gap the US-001 bullet above predicted: `dispatchOrDrain`/`handlePendingTarget` needed their OWN try/catch, not a shared one (US-R1-001, CAM-115, 2026-07-08)**: `makeProductionMetaLoopObserveFn`'s selector-error boundary (previous bullet) only guards the `meta_loop=observe` closure; `makeProductionMetaLoopDispatchFn`'s `dispatchOrDrain` (`deps.selectFn()`) and `handlePendingTarget` (`deps.selectTargetFn?.(targetId)`) are a structurally separate closure reached through the SAME `opts.runMetaLoopObserveFn()` slot in `loop.ts`'s idle tick, so each needed its own local try/catch even though the fix is a near-verbatim copy (catch, log a `'sidecar-exit'` event with a distinct `reason` string per call site -- `meta-loop-dispatch-select-error` for `selectFn`, `meta-loop-dispatch-select-target-error` for `selectTargetFn` -- then `return` before touching `ctx.observeState`/`setPhaseFn`). For `handlePendingTarget` specifically, the early return on catch means a transient read error leaves the stale `plan_issue` UNCLEARED (unlike the genuine not-found-refusal branch, which clears it via `setPhaseFn('idle')`) so the next tick retries the same target instead of silently dropping an operator's explicit `/cam-plan <id>` request. Regression test technique for "the outer sidecar loop survives a throw in this shared seam": build the dispatch closure via `makeProductionMetaLoopDispatchFn` with a throwing `selectFn`, then inject the RETURNED closure as `runMetaLoopObserveFn` on `makeIdleLoopOpts` (the same options-shape helper the sibling `meta_loop=observe` boundary test uses) and drive `runSidecarLoop` for N ticks -- proves the fix at the real wiring seam, not just at the unit level of the closure in isolation.

- **`...(prd.review ?? {})` at a review-write terminal branch silently carries forward stale array fields (e.g. `findings`) written by an EARLIER round; clear by destructuring the field out before spreading, key-removal not empty-array, and scope the fix to the ONE branch the PRD names (US-004, CAM-115, 2026-07-08)**: `makeReviewDispatch`'s CLEAN write path (`src/supervisor/review.ts`) spread the entire previous `prd.review` object forward, so a `findings` array written by a prior `FIXES_PENDING` round (its own write path sets `...(fileFindings !== undefined ? { findings: fileFindings } : {})`) survived into the CLEAN round's persisted state even though CLEAN has zero findings. Fix: `const { findings: _stale, ...rest } = prd.review ?? {}` then spread `rest`, matching the tag-based FIXES_PENDING path's existing convention of key-ABSENCE (not `findings: []`) for "no findings available" (see the `toBeUndefined()` assertion the sibling test already made for that path) -- keep both no-findings representations consistent across the file. Scope discipline: the sibling `MAX_ROUNDS_DEBT` write paths (same file, plus `src/supervisor/loop.ts`) deliberately keep the last round's findings to document the debt visible to the operator; a PRD note that says "CLEAN only" means literally only that one `if (verdictKind === 'CLEAN')` block, never the debt branches even though they share the same `...(prd.review ?? {})` spread shape. Regression-test the invariant across TWO sequential `makeReviewDispatch` calls in one test (round 1 FIXES_PENDING via `readReviewReport` returning findings, feed round 1's captured `writePrd` output as round 2's `prd` fixture, round 2 CLEAN), not a single-call assertion -- a one-shot CLEAN-from-empty-review test cannot prove the carry-over bug is fixed since there was nothing to carry over.

- **Durable marker family convention: no-issueId marker + shared detail-truncation helper (US-002, CAM-215, 2026-07-08)**: when a new durable `.claude/.cam-*.json` marker (mirroring `plan-escalation.ts` / `merge-watch.ts` ship-stalled) represents an environment-level failure rather than an issue-scoped one (e.g. plan-preflight-failed: dirty tree / failing typecheck / failing tests, before the planner even spawns), drop `issueId` from the marker interface entirely -- this is a deliberate divergence from the `PlanEscalatedMarker` shape, not an oversight, and it drives the removal policy too (Option B: remove on ANY subsequent non-matching-kind result, never gated on issueId match, unlike the escalation marker's convergence-gated removal). Field naming: use `writtenAt` (not `ts`, which only `merge-watch.ts` ship-stalled uses) to match the newer `plan-escalation.ts` precedent when a PRD says to follow it. Ship the writer-params type as `Omit<Marker, 'writtenAt'>` (mirrors `PlanEscalationWriterParams`, `plan-runner.ts:788`) so the pure calling loop stays clock-free. When a PRD note says a "detail truncation helper" will be referenced by a LATER story's notify-line prose and a boot-blocker doc section, put the pure helper in the SAME marker module (not a shared utils file) so both later stories import one thing; the contract is: single-line detail unchanged, multi-line detail becomes `firstLine + ' (+N more)'` where N is the count of REMAINING lines (`detail.split('\n')`, destructure `[firstLine, ...rest]`, return early on `rest.length === 0`).

- **A new distinct terminal `kind` added to an existing discriminated-union result type is safe against a catch-all mapper without touching it, when the mapper's fallback branch is already the correct behavior for the new kind (US-003, CAM-215, 2026-07-08)**: `exitPhaseAfterPlan` (`sidecar.ts`) maps `PostAuditActionResult` to a phase via `if (kind === 'branch-created') return; setPhase(kind === 'awaiting-operator-approval' ? 'awaiting-operator' : 'idle')` -- adding a new `{ kind: 'preflight-failed' }` member to the union needed ZERO changes to this function, since it already falls into the generic `'idle'` else-branch. Don't add a redundant explicit arm "for clarity" when the existing fallback is provably correct (a PRD note phrased as "X handles the new kind by reverting to idle" can be satisfied by an existing catch-all, not just by a new `if`); write a positive regression test asserting the fallback covers the new kind instead. Placement discipline for a new arm in the SOURCE union's handler (as opposed to the sidecar mapper): when a PRD says "place the new arm before the generic fall-through", and the function is already at biome's `noExcessiveLinesPerFunction` line ceiling, extract the arm's logic into a small named helper (mirrors the existing `handlePlanTargetInvalid` precedent in the same file, `plan-runner.ts`) rather than inlining -- biome only counts through `bunx biome lint`, not a manual line-count of the function body, so measure with the real tool before assuming a ceiling breach (it did NOT breach here despite the naive line-range subtraction suggesting otherwise).

- **Even a 2-line conditional can trip biome's `noExcessiveLinesPerFunction` when it carries a multi-line explanatory comment; extract a ONE-LINE-CALL helper, not just "the arm's logic" (US-004, CAM-215, 2026-07-08)**: adding `if (planResult.kind !== 'preflight-failed') { removePreflightFailedMarkerFn?.(); }` plus its 5-line doc comment to the TOP of `runPostAuditAction` (before the existing kind-dispatch chain) pushed the function from 80 to 83 lines. The prior "extract the arm's logic into a named helper" pattern (bullet above, US-003) assumed the arm itself was substantial; here the arm was trivial but the COMMENT explaining why it's unconditional (contrast with `removeEscalationMarkerFn`'s convergence-gated sibling) was not. Fix: move the ENTIRE guard + comment into a tiny named helper (`removePreflightMarkerUnlessPreflightFailed(kind, fn)`) so the call site in `runPostAuditAction` shrinks to one line (`removePreflightMarkerUnlessPreflightFailed(planResult.kind, removePreflightFailedMarkerFn);`) with a one-line pointer comment, and the full rationale lives in the helper's own JSDoc instead. Applies generally: when a new seam call is "just an if plus one call" but needs real prose to justify why it's NOT gated the way a similar-looking sibling seam is, budget for the comment's line cost, not just the code's.

- **Testing an "unconditional except one excluded kind" removal seam requires per-kind tests proving it ALSO fires on paths where a sibling seam does NOT (US-004, CAM-215, 2026-07-08)**: `removeEscalationMarkerFn` (existing) only fires on the single proceed-branch convergence path; the new `removePreflightFailedMarkerFn` (Option B) fires on every `planResult.kind` except `'preflight-failed'` -- including `pause-operator`, `audit-blocked`, and `plan-escalated`, all of which are exactly the paths the OLDER, narrower seam explicitly does NOT fire on (see the existing `'... removeEscalationMarkerFn NOT called'` tests for those same three paths). A naive test suite that only asserts the new seam fires on the 2-3 kinds the PRD names by example (`audit-approved`, `planner-failed`, `mutex-busy`) would not catch a regression where a future refactor accidentally copies the OLDER seam's convergence-gating onto the new one. Write explicit `removePreflightFailedMarkerFn is still called (not gated on convergence)` tests for `pause-operator`/`audit-blocked`/`plan-escalated` specifically, sitting right next to the pre-existing `removeEscalationMarkerFn NOT called` assertions for the same three fixtures, so the contrast between the two policies is visible in the same test file.

- **Doc-only boot-step additions to a dual-copy agent persona: assert both raw copies AND the embedded vendor bundle in one test file, anchored by adjacency to the sibling step, not just presence anywhere in the doc (US-005, CAM-215, 2026-07-08)**: adding a new numbered boot step + opening-blocker section to `subagent-orchestrator.md` (both `.claude/agents/` and `templates/agents/`, per the cam-dual-copy-is-per-file gotcha) is pure prose, so the regression test cannot exercise runtime behavior; instead it must prove structural placement. Read the dev copy directly via `readFileSync` (it is never embedded in `templatesContents`, only `templates/` is) and the template copy via `templatesContents['agents/subagent-orchestrator.md']` (post `bun run embed-vendor`), in the SAME `describe.each` block so both copies get identical assertions without duplicating the test body. Use `content.indexOf(markerA) < content.indexOf(markerB)` chains (not just `toContain`) to prove the new step 9 was inserted AFTER steps 7/8, not merely present somewhere in the file, since a misplaced insertion (e.g. accidentally landing before step 7) would still pass a bare `toContain` check. `bun run embed-vendor` must run (and `embed-vendor:check` re-verified clean) before `bun test` on any story that edits `templates/`, or the new prose is invisible to the embedded-bundle half of the assertion even though the raw dev-copy half still passes.

- **`renderHelp`'s entry-name column silently loses its separator space when a `HelpEntry.name` exceeds `NAME_COL_WIDTH` (24 chars) (US-001, CAM-217, 2026-07-08)**: `renderEntry` does `entry.name.padEnd(NAME_COL_WIDTH)` with no fallback separator, so a name already >= 24 chars gets zero padding and the description string concatenates directly onto it (e.g. `append [--force] [--cycle-close]Read a JSON cycle entry...`, no space). This only bites when editing an existing `*_HELP` block's `Subcommands`/`Options` entries to append a new flag token to an already-long `name` field; check the rendered `bun index.ts <cmd> --help` output (not just the source diff) before committing, and either keep the name under 24 chars or use a shorter alias, since `renderEntry` itself is out of scope for a doc-only story. Root cause of CAM-217 (the bug this story exists to prevent): `--cycle-close` had shipped in `parseJournalArgs`/`dispatchJournal` (CAM-162) with zero mention in `JOURNAL_HELP`, so an agent grepping `--help` output legitimately concluded the flag "doesn't exist"; the fix pattern (extend the `Options` entries array + append a non-restructuring "Exit-code contract:" paragraph to the existing numbered `Behaviour` body, per the story's own "extend, do not restructure" note) generalizes to any future silently-undocumented flag.

- **Deduping 3 near-identical CLI arg-parse loops with a byte-identical-error-message constraint: make the divergent bit an OPTIONAL callback, not a config flag (US-002, CAM-220, 2026-07-08)**: `parseIssueArgs`/`parsePlanArgs`/`parseSpecArgs` (`index.ts`) cloned the same 4-part idiom (`--help`/`-h` detection, unknown-option rejection, too-many-arguments rejection, single-positional capture) but each owns its own exact `printError` message text (asserted byte-identical by existing tests) and its own post-capture validation (plan's integer parse, spec's empty-id check). The extracted `parseSubcommandArgs(args, { onTooMany, onUnknownOption? })` helper takes the error-reporting as caller-supplied callbacks (so messages stay untouched, zero test edits) and returns only the raw `{ help, positional }` shape, leaving all further validation to the caller. The one REAL behavioral divergence among the three (issue's free-text mode must accept a token starting with `-` as the title, e.g. a title typed as `-foo`, never reject it as an unknown option) is threaded through by making `onUnknownOption` optional: omitting it disables that branch entirely rather than adding a `permissive: boolean` flag, so the call site self-documents which idiom variant it wants. Also safe to fold `parsePlanArgs`' per-token help handling (which previously did NOT short-circuit -- `cam plan --help --bogus` would have hit the unknown-option branch before this refactor) into the shared up-front `args.includes('--help')` short-circuit already used by the other two parsers, since no test exercised the old combined-flags edge case and unifying it is the explicit point of the dedup story.

- **Dedup a byte-identical private helper pair by extracting a value-agnostic shared module, unifying the value-pattern instead of parameterizing it (US-001, CAM-120, 2026-07-08)**: `check-coverage.ts`'s `parseDiffKv`/`TRACKER_REF_RE`/`makeDefaultGetStagedDiff` and `check-file-sizes.ts`'s identically-shaped trio differed only in one regex literal (`KV_RE`: `[\d.]+` for float coverage floors vs `\d+` for int file-size ceilings). Rather than adding a `valueRe` injection parameter to `parseDiffKv` (defensive but unused complexity per Simplicity First), unify to the float-permitting pattern (`[\d.]+`, a strict superset: integers match it unambiguously too) and prove behavior preservation by running both scripts' existing test suites unchanged rather than asserting it in prose. New shared module `scripts/ratchet-diff.ts` exports `parseDiffKv`, `TRACKER_REF_RE`, `GetStagedDiffFn`, and `makeGetStagedDiff(cwd)`; both callers `export type { GetStagedDiffFn }` (re-export, not re-declare) since nothing outside each file imported the old locally-declared alias. This is the DI-pattern-mirror precedent (`src/git/on-main.ts`, CAM-108/CAM-133) applied to the two independent ratchet-gate scripts instead of the on-main writers.

- **Two output paths that print "the same list" must share ONE constructed variable, not two separately-named sources that happen to look alike (US-001, CAM-224, 2026-07-08)**: `runTriage` (`src/commands/triage.ts`) had a no-op branch printing `gateResult.warnings` and a commit branch printing `rankIssues`' `warnings`, and the two silently diverged because nothing tied them together -- each branch read its own local, correctly-named variable, so the bug was invisible at the call site and only visible by reading both branches side by side. Fix: build the union (`gateResult.warnings` concatenated with the rank warnings) ONCE, above the `if (changed === 0)` branch, into a single `warnings` binding, and pass that same binding into `printTriageOutput` on both paths. This makes divergence a type/reference-level impossibility rather than a discipline problem: a future editor touching one branch cannot forget the other because there is only one variable to touch. Test-wise, prove it with a single backlog fixture that yields both a gate warning (cross-stage blocker: an in-universe issue blocked by an out-of-universe, non-shipped issue) and a rank warning (missing `wsjf` field), asserted present in BOTH a ranks-already-match no-op run and a ranks-differ commit run of the same fixture.

- **Genuinely-dead optional field: drop it, don't give it a runtime default (US-002, CAM-224, 2026-07-08)**: `spec.ts`'s `clock?: ClockFn` convention pairs the optional field with `options.clock ?? (() => new Date().toISOString())` because that clock IS read later in the function. `RunTriageOptions.clock` (`src/commands/triage.ts`) is never read anywhere in `runTriage` -- so making it optional needed ONLY the signature change (`clock?: ClockFn`) plus deleting the injection at the one production call site (`dispatchTriage` in `index.ts`); no `??` fallback was added, since a fallback for a value nothing consumes is dead code by definition. Existing test call sites that still pass `clock: () => ...` (e.g. `test/integration/triage-real-git.test.ts`) do not need touching just because the field went optional -- passing an extra optional field remains valid under `--strict`, so only the file the story's AC named (`test/triage.test.ts`) needed its 16 dead call sites deleted.

- **Two functions over the complexity budget in one file need the SAME extract-helper treatment, not just the one the story title names (US-003, CAM-108-review, 2026-07-08)**: `rank.ts's `noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity` override covered the whole file, so removing it surfaced TWO violations -- `runKahn` (20, named in the story title) AND `buildGraph` (17, not mentioned). Always re-lint the file with the override stripped BEFORE assuming the named function is the only offender. Extraction pattern for `runKahn`: split into `collectZeroInDegreeLayer` (loop+filter), `sortLayerByWsjf` (the comparator, unchanged logic), and `drainLayer` (push+delete+decrement); each becomes a single-purpose top-level function with base complexity ~1-3. A useful sub-trick: when a loop counter (`nextRank`) is monotonically `ranked.length + 1` at every increment site, drop the counter entirely and compute `rank: ranked.length + 1` inline in the push -- one fewer piece of mutable state threaded through the extracted helper's signature. For `buildGraph`, the nested double-loop (init pass + edge-recording pass with a 3-way `isSatisfied` OR and a get-or-create branch) split into `initGraphEntries` (single loop, no branching) and `recordBlockedByEdges` (one issue's blockedBy loop, called from the outer loop in `buildGraph`), dropping both call sites to well under 15. Verify the actual score with `bunx biome lint <file>` on a scratch copy of the config with the override removed (`jq` a temp file, restore after) rather than guessing from the AST by eye.

- **A "block" extracted for verbatim relocation should be the semantic unit (header+body), not the raw line-span including incidental separator whitespace (US-001, CAM-125, 2026-07-08)**: `archiveJournalOnMain` (`src/commands/journal-archive.ts`) needed to move `## `-delimited entries from `journal.md` to `journal.archive.md` "byte-identically". Rather than preserving the exact raw substring between two headers (which bakes in an arbitrary trailing-blank-line ownership decision between the moved block and its neighbor), extract each block via `entriesLines.slice(start, nextStart ?? end)` then `trimTrailingBlankLines` it, so every block is just `header + '\n\n' + bullets` with no trailing separator. Reassembly (`buildJournalContent`/`buildArchiveContent`) then owns ALL separator insertion explicitly (`blocks.join('\n\n')`), the same convention `renderJournalBlock`/`appendBlock` already use in `journal.ts`. This makes "byte-identical" mean "the entry's own text is unchanged," which is both the testable claim and the one a reviewer will actually check, rather than a fragile claim about incidental file-formatting whitespace. Same trick applies to the marker-relative counting rule from the CAM-90/US-006 lineage: `scripts/cam/patterns.md` line ~ (grep "marker-relative scan") already establishes reading real state via `git show main:<path>` (never the working tree) and treating a lazily-created file's absent-from-main read as `null`, not an error (`readArchiveFromMain` mirrors `readContextMdFromMain` from `domain-docs.ts` exactly).

- **New CLI mode on an existing multi-mode dispatch: branch before any stdin/side-effect and re-export the core fn's default so the parser and the core stay in sync (US-002, CAM-125, 2026-07-08)**: adding `cam journal archive [--threshold N]` as a sibling of `cam journal append` on the same `dispatchJournal` required two easy-to-miss ordering/consistency rules. (1) `dispatchJournal` reads stdin unconditionally on the append path; the new `mode === 'archive'` branch MUST be checked and return BEFORE `const readStdin = ...` / `await readStdin()`, otherwise a caller that never intends to pipe JSON hangs waiting on stdin. (2) `parseJournalArgs` must bake a concrete default `threshold: number` into the returned union member (AC requires a number, not `number | undefined`) so the CLI layer and the core `archiveJournalOnMain` agree on the no-`--threshold` behavior; rather than hardcoding the magic number `50` twice, `journal-archive.ts` exports its own `DEFAULT_THRESHOLD` constant (one-line `export` addition) and `index.ts` imports it aliased (`DEFAULT_THRESHOLD as JOURNAL_ARCHIVE_DEFAULT_THRESHOLD`) instead of re-declaring the literal. General shape for any future third `cam <noun> <verb2>` subcommand sharing a dispatcher: (a) widen the parsed-args discriminated union with a new `mode` member, (b) an injectable `<verb2>Fn` dep on the `*DispatchDeps` interface mirroring the existing `<verb1>Fn` (defaults to the real core-fn + real spawnSync), (c) the new mode's branch sits ahead of any I/O the OTHER mode needs but this one doesn't.

- **Auto-invoking a best-effort side-effect on a success path: share the injectable dep, place the call by mutation-safety not by dependency-order convenience, and update EVERY existing test that now reaches the new call site (US-003, CAM-125, 2026-07-08)**: wiring `cam journal append --cycle-close` to auto-invoke the US-002 archive check reused the SAME `archiveFn?: (threshold) => ArchiveJournalOnMainResult` dep already on `JournalDispatchDeps` (factored its production default into a module-level `defaultArchiveFn`, used by both the explicit `archive` mode branch and the new auto-invoked call) rather than adding a second dep for the same underlying capability. Placement: the call sits AFTER the `handoffExists`/`watcherAlive` guards (so it only fires on the sub-path that will actually arm the marker -- guard-fails exit 3/4 without archiving, matching "at cycle close" semantics) and BEFORE `armMarker()` (load-bearing: once the marker is armed the watcher can SIGTERM this process mid-archive, so any git-mutating side effect must complete before that point, never after). Best-effort means wrap in `try/catch`, and on either a thrown error or `ok:false` call `printWarning` (not `printError`) and swallow it: never change the return code, never skip `armMarker()`/`CAM_ORCH_HANDOFF_DUE`. Gotcha that would have silently broken 2 pre-existing tests: any `--cycle-close` test with `handoffExistsFn: () => true` and `watcherAliveFn: () => true` now reaches the new call site, so omitting an injected `archiveFn` in those tests falls through to `defaultArchiveFn` -- the REAL `archiveJournalOnMain` hitting real git (network fetch via `checkMainUpToDate`, and potentially a real commit to main if the real journal.md exceeds the threshold). Any story that auto-invokes an existing injectable-by-default core fn on a path a prior story's tests already exercise must grep for every test reaching that path and inject a fake explicitly, not rely on the pre-existing dep list being exhaustive.

- **Never type a `\u0000` (NUL) escape into a Write/Edit content string as a field-join delimiter for hashing: it lands as a raw NUL byte and turns the source file into "data" (US-002, CAM-189, 2026-07-08)**: a fingerprint helper joining normalized `[file, line, text]` fields before hashing needs a delimiter that cannot appear in a trimmed file path or line number, so a NUL separator is the theoretically "safest" choice. In practice, authoring it as the literal `'\u0000'` inside a tool-authored `.ts` file writes an actual NUL byte (not the two-character escape sequence) into the file: `file <path>` then reports `data` instead of a text/source type, `grep` silently returns no matches without `-a`, and the subsequent `Edit` tool's string match fails because its literal `old_string` no longer byte-matches what landed on disk. Symptom chain when this happens: `Edit` reports "String to replace not found" even though a `Read` of the same region visually looks identical (terminal renders NUL as blank/space). Fix once diagnosed: `xxd`/`cat -v` the suspect line to confirm (NUL shows as `^@` in `cat -v`), then repair via a `python3` script operating on raw `bytes` (not text-mode) to replace the exact byte sequence. Prevention: use a plain printable ASCII delimiter (e.g. `'::'`) for this kind of join-then-hash helper instead of a NUL escape.

- **Real-writer regression tests for `runSidecarLoop` terminal hooks: a canned `runSupervisorFn` never invokes `onProgress`, and `clearActive`'s implicit `phase:idle` rewrite always recreates the state file (US-003, CAM-189, 2026-07-08)**: `onProgress` is called from INSIDE `runSupervisor` (at `notifyTerminal`/`finishTerminal`), never by the outer `runSidecarLoop`. A test that overrides `runSupervisorFn` with a fake returning a canned `SupervisorResult` (the common pattern for gate-matrix tests, mirroring `auto-chain.test.ts`'s `driveOneTick`) completely bypasses `onProgress`, including its unlink-on-complete write -- so any test that needs the REAL `onProgress`/`makeOnProgress` unlink behavior (e.g. an ADR-0013-style real-writer regression, precedent `test/supervisor/auto-ship-real-writers.test.ts`) MUST leave `runSupervisorFn` undefined so `runSidecarLoop` defaults to the real `runSupervisor`, and seed a prd where `decideNextAction` resolves the desired terminal (e.g. `complete`) on the very first tick with zero worker dispatch (all stories `passes:true`, `review.lastVerdict` already terminal). Second gotcha in the same test shape: `makeClearActive`'s rewrite is unconditional -- it always re-writes the state file with `phase:idle` (no explicit `setPhase('idle')` call exists; it derives from `active:false` + no `phase` field) -- so after a real `onProgress` unlink-on-complete AND a real `clearActive`, the state file EXISTS again with `phase:'idle'`; asserting `existsSync(stateFilePath) === false` after both real writers ran is always wrong. Assert `parseStateFile(readFileSync(stateFilePath))?.phase === 'idle'` instead. Third gotcha (test-harness only, not production): `driveOneTick`-style helpers MUST make `hasPendingStories()` return `true` on its FIRST call (the pre-dispatch "active but nothing pending" early-exit gate at the top of the active branch, BEFORE `runSupervisorFn` is even invoked) and `false` on later calls (the post-terminal `flipActiveFn` auto-chain re-check); a naive `hasPendingStories: () => false` silently short-circuits the whole tick before the code under test ever runs, and every downstream assertion reads as "never called" with no obvious error.

- **Layered plannability predicate: `src/issues/plannable.ts` is the sole owner of `stage === 'specified'` (US-001, CAM-222, 2026-07-08)**: `isSpecifiedOpen(entry)` (universe core: `stage === 'specified' && status === 'open'`) and `isPlannable(entry, backlog)` (`isSpecifiedOpen` plus `!isBlocked`) live in a LEAF module importing only `types.ts` + `graph.ts`, breaking the `select.ts` (imports `computeWsjf` from `rank.ts`) <-> `rank.ts` cycle that would otherwise form if `rank.ts` imported from `select.ts`. `select.ts` re-exports both for its public-surface contract; `rank.ts` (`rankIssues`) and `gate.ts` (`runGraphGate`'s universe build) route through `isSpecifiedOpen` ONLY -- they intentionally retain blocked issues for topological ranking and cycle detection, never through `isPlannable`. Only `select.ts` (`selectPlannableIssue`/`selectPlannableById`) and `plan.ts` (`resolveSpecificIssue`) use the blocked-excluding `isPlannable`. Gotcha: a spine guard grepping `=== 'specified'` outside `src/vendor/` catches DOCSTRING comments too, not just code -- rephrase any doc comment restating the filter (e.g. "stage === 'specified' AND status === 'open'") to name the predicate function instead of re-typing the literal comparison, or the guard flags the file even with zero inline reimplementation.

- **Seeding a new `templates/scripts/cam/<file>` needs zero manifest wiring, but embed-vendor CAN push a file over its size ceiling (US-002, CAM-232, 2026-07-09)**: `materializeTemplates` (`src/templates/embedded.ts`) already routes any `scripts/cam/<x>` key generically, so dropping a new file under `templates/scripts/cam/` and running `bun run embed-vendor` is sufficient for the file to flow to a downstream `cam init` -- no target-path or SUBTREES change needed. The regen rewrites `src/vendor/_generated.ts` as one big object literal, so adding even a small new template can tip `src/vendor/_generated.ts` (and, if you add a matching test describe block, the touched test file) past their `scripts/file-size-budget.json` ceiling. `check:file-size`'s ceiling-raise gate additionally requires the STAGED diff of `scripts/file-size-budget.json` itself to contain a tracker ref (`CAM-\d+`, `#\d+`, or a URL) -- edit the `_ref` prose field (prepend a new dated note, don't touch history) in the same edit that raises the numeric ceilings, or `bun run check:all`'s `file-size` gate fails even though every individual file passes its own ceiling.

- **`deriveBacklogJson` (`src/issues/list.ts`, US-002, CAM-222, 2026-07-08): "membership" and "ranking" are two DIFFERENT ordering questions over the same predicate.** `isPlannable` (imported from `select.ts`, which re-exports `plannable.ts`) answers pure membership (specified+open+not-blocked); ordering the resulting array reuses `deriveBacklogView`'s existing `compareBacklogEntries` (ranked-first / ascending-rank / numeric-id tie-break, now exported), NOT `selectPlannableIssue`'s champion-selection logic (that mixes WSJF-vs-rank via a non-total-order comparator to pick a single winner, CAM-203) -- a `plannable[]` LIST needs a total order across every candidate, a single champion pick does not. Second gotcha: `isPlannable(entry, backlog)` must be evaluated against the FULL backlog array (every stage), not a pre-filtered `status==='open'` subset, because `isBlocked` needs to see blocking dependencies that may sit in ANY stage (including `idea`/non-open) to correctly compute unmet-blocker status; only the separate `counts`/`byStage` per-stage grouping filters to `status==='open'` first. Third: a JSON-shaped row type that must have a literal, LLM-graded exact-shape contract (`{ id, title, rank }`) should normalize `rank` to `null` for unranked entries rather than omitting the key -- `JSON.stringify` silently drops `undefined`-valued keys, which breaks a reviewer oracle asserting the row always has exactly 3 keys.

- **Spine guard tests (`test/spine/`, US-003, CAM-222, 2026-07-08): a self-contained single test file, not a separate `scripts/check-*.ts` + `test/*.test.ts` pair, is the right shape when `bun test` (already a mandatory gate) is the only enforcement surface the guard needs.** The `scripts/check-debt-markers.ts` / `check-file-sizes.ts` pattern (pure functions exported from a `scripts/` CLI entrypoint, unit-tested from `test/`) is for gates that also need a standalone `bun scripts/check-X.ts` invocation wired into `check-all.ts`'s `GATES` manifest. A guard whose only job is "fail `bun test` if a banned pattern reappears in `src/`" doesn't need that split: put the pure scanner function, its synthetic unit tests, AND a real-tree assertion test (`new Glob('src/**/*.{ts,tsx}')` + `readFileSync`) in one file under `test/spine/`; `bun test`'s default recursive discovery picks it up with zero `check-all.ts` wiring. Keep the AC's two "cannot self-trip" invariants provable by construction: build the banned-pattern regex from string parts (never type the literal banned substring inline in the guard's own source) and confirm the scan root literal is `'src/**/...'` (never `'test/**/...'` or repo-root-wide), so the guard's own file is structurally out of its own scan scope, not just accidentally so.

- **Backlog-derivation instruction has THREE independent literal surfaces, not two (US-004, CAM-222, 2026-07-08; supersedes the CAM-190 two-surface note)**: the persona doc (`.claude/agents/` + `templates/agents/subagent-orchestrator.md`, boot-context step 5) and the inline `buildOrchestratorBootPrompt()` string in `src/commands/run.ts` both restate the same rule and must be edited in lockstep -- reversing an incident-driven instruction (here: banning the raw `scripts/cam/issues/*.json` stage-filtered fallback that resurrected abandoned issues) in only the doc copies while leaving `run.ts`'s literal array-of-strings prompt with the old wording defeats the fix, since that boot prompt is the actual first message fed to the orchestrator process. Tests pinning the OLD wording (`test/run.test.ts`, `test/vendor/orchestrator-backlog-derivation.test.ts`) must be updated in the same story, not left red or `--no-verify`'d past. Gotcha: a markdown assertion phrase spanning a soft-wrapped line break (e.g. `never grep or read`) is NOT a contiguous substring in the raw file content -- the literal `\n` between wrapped words breaks a `toContain('phrase with spaces')` check even though the phrase reads correctly to a human. When a test asserts an exact multi-word phrase against prose that will be hand-wrapped at ~72-80 chars, either keep that exact phrase on one unwrapped line or assert on shorter word-level fragments instead. `src/commands/run.ts`'s prompt is built as one string per array element joined by `\n`, so a phrase that stays within one array-literal element is always contiguous there, unlike hand-wrapped markdown prose.

- **Deterministic-value seams that trigger a downstream prd.json write need TWO injected seams, not one (US-001, CAM-236, 2026-07-09)**: when a pure module (`plan-runner.ts`) derives a value in code instead of trusting an upstream planner-authored field (branch name from `issueNumber` instead of `prd.branchName`), replace the trusted-field option with (a) the raw upstream input (`issueNumber: unknown`, not `number` -- the AC's "typeof number" gate needs to see non-number values at runtime, not just `null`/`undefined`) and (b) an optional writer callback (`writePrdBranchNameFn?: (v: string) => void`) invoked from the pure module at the exact point the derived value must be persisted (here: after `git checkout -B`, before `git add`), so the committed file stays coherent with the derived value even when the upstream source disagrees. The pure module never does its own `readFileSync`/`writeFileSync`; production wiring (`sidecar.ts`) supplies a best-effort read-mutate-write closure mirroring `host.ts`'s `writePrd` (`JSON.stringify(obj, null, 2) + '\n'`). When a story like this removes an old guard field entirely (not just adds one), grep every test file for `RunXOptions` / the removed field name across the WHOLE repo (not just the primary suite file) -- old guard-specific test files (e.g. `test/plan-runner-empty-branch.test.ts`) that exist solely to exercise the removed contract should be deleted and their coverage re-homed under the new contract's test file, not patched field-by-field.

- **Adding an optional enum property to the issue schema pair is a properties-only edit, never touch `required` or `allOf` (US-001, CAM-235, 2026-07-09)**: `scripts/cam/issues.schema.json` and `templates/scripts/cam/issues.schema.json` are intentionally NOT byte-identical (see the cam-dual-copy-is-per-file gotcha: the dogfood copy carries `specSource`/`derivedFrom`/`allOf` conditional blocks the templates copy lacks). Adding a new optional field like `type` (enum `feat|fix|chore|docs`) means appending the same `properties.type` block independently to each file's `properties` object, leaving `required` and any `allOf` conditionals untouched in both, and NOT diffing the two files against each other as a correctness check (they are supposed to differ). `IssueEntry` in `src/issues/types.ts` mirrors with a plain `type?: "feat" | "fix" | "chore" | "docs"` union field. No runtime JSON-schema validator (ajv or similar) actually parses `issues.schema.json` in this repo today, so Step 5.5 for this class of story is `no_external_lib_touched`, not a docs-fetch against json-schema.org.

- **A new optional top-level `IssueEntry` field needs a validate-then-conditionally-spread pattern, not a `?? default`, when "absent stays absent" is an AC (US-002, CAM-235, 2026-07-09)**: `src/issues/spec.ts` already has the precedent (`validateSpecSource`) for validating a top-level field (not one nested inside `Spec`) that is optional, has an enum, and must never receive a written-in default from the persist path. `validateIssueType(x: unknown)` mirrors that shape: `x === undefined` short-circuits `{ok:true}` (absence is never an error), otherwise checks membership in the literal tuple `["feat","fix","chore","docs"] as const`. In the mutation step of `specifyIssueOnMain`, the field must be added via `...(type !== undefined ? { type } : {})` spread AFTER the base object literal, never `type: type ?? 'feat'` -- the latter would write a literal default onto every specified issue, which the AC explicitly forbids (defaulting is deferred to read-time consumers: the planner and PR-title composer). The stdin-payload interface (`SpecPersistPayload` in `spec.ts`) trusts the type-cast (`payload as SpecPersistPayload`) exactly like the existing `spec`/`wsjf` fields do; real validation happens downstream in `specifyIssueOnMain`, and the new `invalid-type` guard reason must be added to the same `if` branch as `invalid-spec`/`invalid-wsjf`/`integrity-error` in `spec.ts` (the branch that prints `outcome.errors.join('; ')`), not the generic guard-failure branch (which has no `errors` array).

- **`composePrTitle`'s type-prefix defaulting stays inline, not a shared helper with `computeNextVersion` (US-005, CAM-235, 2026-07-09)**: `PrdSnapshot.type` (src/release/pr-body.ts) and `ShipPrdRecord.type` (src/supervisor/ship-runner.ts) are both plain `type?: string`, mirroring how `notes?`/`issueNumber?` already flow through `toPrdSnapshot` -- no schema/enum validation at this layer (that already happened upstream at issue-spec time, CAM-235 US-002). `composePrTitle` defaults with `prd.type?.trim() || DEFAULT_TYPE` (DEFAULT_TYPE = 'feat'), same short-circuit style as the existing description/project fallback chain, not a shared constant with `classifyBump`'s conventional-commit type parsing (src/release/bump.ts) -- the two "feat" defaults serve different purposes (title composition vs. bump signal) and coupling them would violate the pure-composer-no-cross-import rule (the "Pure PRD-snapshot composer pattern" bullet above). The issue-number suffix uses `prd.issueNumber != null && String(prd.issueNumber).trim() !== ''` (not a truthy check) so `issueNumber: 0` would still render -- loose-equality `!= null` intentionally catches both `null` and `undefined` in one check, consistent with `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`-safe style elsewhere in this file. `test/release/ship-pr.test.ts`'s happy-path fixture (`PRD_SNAPSHOT`) has no explicit `type` field, so its `--title` assertion needed updating to the defaulted `feat: ...` prefix -- any test fixture built before this story that asserts on `composePrTitle`'s bare-description output is now stale by construction, not a false failure.

- **`runShipPrStep`'s type-derived `--label` reuses a local map, never `composePrTitle`'s DEFAULT_TYPE constant (US-006, CAM-235, 2026-07-09)**: added a private `TYPE_LABEL_MAP: Record<string, string | null>` in `src/release/ship-pr.ts` (feat->'enhancement', fix->'bug', docs->'documentation', chore->null) plus `deriveGithubLabel(type)`, which defaults an absent/unrecognized type to the `'enhancement'` result (not by importing pr-body.ts's `DEFAULT_TYPE='feat'` string and then mapping it, since the map is private to this file and cross-importing a string constant just to look it up in a map defined two lines away would be needless coupling for a same-file concern). `null` in the map (chore) is a real, load-bearing runtime value, not a placeholder: `createArgs.push('--label', label)` is skipped only when `deriveGithubLabel` returns `null`, so `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`'s `Record<string, string|null>[key]` type is `string|null|undefined` and the correct narrowing is `mapped === undefined ? 'enhancement' : mapped` (falls through `null` unchanged, only substitutes the default for a genuinely-missing key) -- do NOT reach for a non-null assertion (`!`) here, it would type-erase the `null` case instead of preserving it. Gotcha: the story's `notes` field pointed at `test/ship-pr.test.ts`, but the real file (already existing from CAM-149 US-003) is `test/release/ship-pr.test.ts` -- always verify a notes-referenced test path with `find`/`grep` before assuming it matches the story text verbatim.

- **A noisy-on-invalid project.toml reader breaks the silent-default pattern on purpose, and takes an already-parsed config object, not a configPath (US-002, CAM-236, 2026-07-09)**: `readIssueSystem(config: Record<string, unknown>)` in the new `src/config/issue-system.ts` deliberately does NOT follow the `readMergeMode`/`readPlanApproval`/`readWorkerIsolation` convention (models.ts) of silently falling back to a safe default on any malformed/unrecognized value -- issue_system selects which backend owns issue-close semantics, so a typo'd or legacy value (the old `'none'`) must throw a noisy error instead of silently defaulting, otherwise the local-backend close/stash gates would skip without any signal. It also deliberately takes an already-parsed config object instead of a configPath + internal `loadConfig` call, because its only real call site (`ship-finalize.ts`) already runs `parseToml(tomlText)` once for `issue_prefix` -- re-parsing inside the reader would be redundant. `IssueSystem` (the type) moved out of `src/commands/setup.ts` into this module to avoid a commands<->config import cycle; `setup.ts` now does `export type { IssueSystem } from '../config/issue-system.ts'` so existing importers (`SetupScreen.tsx`) are unaffected. Renaming the string literal `'none'` -> `'local'` across a codebase touches three kinds of surface in the same story: the production `===`/`!==` comparisons, the doc comments describing them, AND every test fixture/toml literal that feeds those comparisons (`test/ship-finalize*.test.ts`, `test/integration/ship-finalize-dirty-prd.test.ts`, `test/release/ship-pr.test.ts`'s `issueBackend: 'none'` fixtures) -- missing the test-fixture leg leaves gates green by accident (fakes don't validate against the real enum) until the next story that touches the same branch.

- **A `grep "'none'"` sweep-completeness oracle matches inside backtick-fenced doc comments too, not just quoted string literals (US-003, CAM-236, 2026-07-09)**: `src/config/issue-system.ts`'s own doc comment (written in the prior US-002 story) said `` the legacy `'none'` `` -- backticks for markdown code-formatting, with single quotes *inside* the backticks to denote "this is the string value". A grep for the literal 3-char sequence `'none'` (apostrophe-n-o-n-e-apostrophe) matches that substring regardless of the surrounding backticks, so the file itself failed its own migration's completeness oracle (`! grep -rn "'none'" ... src/config/issue-system.ts`) despite having zero *executable* references to the old sentinel. When a story's acceptance criteria is a `grep` oracle over a set of files, treat every substring match as real (including inside doc comments describing legacy/historical values) -- rephrase without the quote pair (e.g. `` the legacy `none` sentinel ``) rather than trying to special-case "this one's just a comment" in the oracle itself. `readIssueSystem`'s explicit `=== 'local'` branch pattern (replacing an implicit fall-through past an `if (linear||github) return` block) is the same restructure now applied in `issue-list.ts`: check the interesting case explicitly and return early, then let the remaining (exhaustively-typed) union members fall through to the shared tail, instead of enumerating the non-default branches in the `if` and treating the default as "whatever's left after the if".

- **A dual-copy docs/prose sweep needs a same-enum-context scope test, not just the literally-cited lines (US-004, CAM-236, 2026-07-09)**: the story's own AC cited only `subagent-orchestrator.md:60` (the boot-context bullet documenting `issue_system`'s enum) and explicitly told the worker to leave `:102` (`current cycle: <prd cycle id or "none">`) untouched -- but there was an unlisted third `none` occurrence at `:100` (`issue system: <linear|github|none>`, the boot-greeting template) that shares the exact same enum semantics as `:60` and was migrated too, on the reasoning that "leave X alone" call-outs in an AC imply the author reviewed the surrounding block and is scoping-out only that specific line, not silently blessing every other unlisted occurrence of the same enum. The test for "is this `none` in scope" is same-referent (does it describe the `issue_system` config value) not same-line-number-as-cited-in-AC. Two occurrences were deliberately left out of this story's scope despite matching the string: `.claude/agents/subagent-reviewer.md:72` (`issue_system = none` in an edge-case checklist) and `scripts/cam/patterns.md:46` (the `finalizeCycleClose` hardening bullet) -- both already called out in the prior story's `handoff.openQuestions` as out-of-AC-scope follow-ups; a future docs-sweep story should close them for full consistency.

- **A review-round oracle-mismatch finding can be resolved by retargeting the AC's oracle, not just by changing production code (US-R1-001, CAM-236, 2026-07-09)**: US-001's AC1 pinned a named-command grep oracle to `src/commands/setup.ts`, but a later story (US-002) legitimately relocated the `IssueSystem` union literal to `src/config/issue-system.ts` (to avoid a commands<->config import cycle) and left `setup.ts` as a bare `export type { IssueSystem } from '../config/issue-system.ts'` re-export -- so the oracle's literal string search stopped matching even though the type was unchanged and typecheck stayed green. When a reviewer finding confirms the code is functionally correct and the relocation was itself the recommended design (check the *prior* story's own notes), the fix is to retarget the stale AC's oracle at the type's real, current home rather than re-inlining/duplicating the literal to satisfy the old oracle path -- duplicating would reintroduce the exact coupling the relocation was meant to avoid. General rule: an oracle mismatch surfaced in review is a defect in the AC (wrong pinned location) as often as it is a defect in the code; check which one actually regressed before "fixing" the wrong side.

- **A subsection heading inside a persona doc's enum-mapped section is a fourth surface an AC-scoped grep sweep can miss (US-R2-001, CAM-236, 2026-07-09)**: US-004's AC3 oracle pinned only the boot-context bullet at `subagent-orchestrator.md:60` (`issue_system: <linear|github|local>`), but the same doc's "Issue system integration" section further down had three `### <Backend>` subsection headings (`### Linear` / `### GitHub` / `### None`) mirroring the exact same enum -- `### None` was never grepped by the AC's line-pinned oracle and slipped through both US-004 and the round-1 review untouched, resurfacing as a round-2 reviewer WARNING. Same fix shape as US-004's own lesson (same-referent, not same-line-number, defines scope) but one layer deeper: a heading-as-enum-value (`###` markdown headers used to switch over a config union, not just prose sentences describing it) needs the identical grep sweep applied to `^### ` lines, not just body text. Fixed by mechanical 3-surface rename (`.claude/agents/` copy, `templates/` copy, then `bun run embed-vendor` to refresh `src/vendor/_generated.ts`) -- no test changes needed since no test asserted the exact heading text, only the review's own file-assert oracle.

- **`readIssueSystem`'s alias normalization sits BEFORE the enum-membership check, not folded into it (US-001, CAM-239, 2026-07-09)**: CAM-236 (US-002) deliberately made `'none'` throw as a truly-unknown value, reasoning issue_system must fail loud on typos; ADR-0018 reversed that specific sub-decision after real-world impact: `mergeIntoConfig` never rewrites an *existing* `project.toml`, so any project initialized before the none->local rename keeps `issue_system = "none"` on disk forever, and ship-finalize/issue-list/sidecar all now throw on every run until a human hand-edits the config. Fix is a single extra branch `if (value === 'none') return 'local';` inserted between the absent-key check and the `VALID_ISSUE_SYSTEMS.includes` check -- `'none'` is intentionally kept OUT of `VALID_ISSUE_SYSTEMS` (it must never become selectable/canonical again, only read-normalized), so the alias check has to be a separate literal comparison, not an addition to the enum array. Truly-unknown values (typos, non-strings) still throw unchanged. General rule: when a migration's fail-loud decision (CAM-236) turns out to break real installs because a config field is read-only-normalized rather than write-migrated, the fix is a narrow read-side alias, not reopening the enum.

- **`parseSetupArgs`'s `--issue-system none` alias check must precede the `ISSUE_SYSTEMS.includes` membership test in BOTH argument forms, and a file-size-budget raise needs a tracker ref in the same staged diff (US-002, CAM-239, 2026-07-09)**: mirroring the US-001 `readIssueSystem` fix, `parseSetupArgs` (src/commands/setup.ts) needed the identical `'none' -> 'local'` branch added twice: once in the `--issue-system <val>` two-token form and once in the `--issue-system=<val>` single-token form, each inserted before its own `ISSUE_SYSTEMS.includes` check so a legacy invocation resolves silently instead of hitting the truly-invalid-value `printError`+`null` path. `ISSUE_SYSTEMS` (the array feeding both the flag validator and the interactive `Select` at line 186) stays `['linear', 'github', 'local']` unchanged: 'none' is read-normalized only, never re-added as selectable. Adding the two 2-line branches pushed `src/commands/setup.ts` from 839 to 841 lines (the tool's line-count convention is `content.split('\n').length`, one higher than `wc -l` on a trailing-newline file) which trips `scripts/check-file-sizes.ts`'s ratchet gate; raising the ceiling in `scripts/file-size-budget.json` requires the staged diff of that file to contain a tracker reference (`CAM-NNN`, `#N`, or a URL) anywhere in the diff (checked via `TRACKER_REF_RE` against the whole staged patch, not just the changed line) or `checkDiffTrackerRef` fails the gate. `bash scripts/build-release.sh` (no `--install`) is the full end-to-end oracle for this class of story: it hermetically invokes the freshly compiled binary's `init --existing --issue-system none --merge-mode immediate --no-tmux` and prints `Issue system: local` in the setup summary when the alias resolves correctly across both the arg-parser (this story) and the `project.toml` reader (US-001).

- **`subagent-reviewer` has TWO real dispatch paths, refining the 2026-06-16 pane-vs-Task note (US-001, CAM-225, 2026-07-09)**: the earlier "Pane worker vs Task subagent" bullet above says the reviewer is exclusively a pane worker, driven by the sidecar via `review.ts`'s `makeReviewDispatch` (true for the sidecar's own autonomous post-story review pass). But `subagent-reviewer` is ALSO on the PreToolUse allowlist (`orch-agent-allowlist.sh`) precisely because `.claude/commands/cam-review.md` Step 2 spawns it via `Task(subagent_type="subagent-reviewer", ...)` directly inside the orchestrator's own session when a human (or the orchestrator) runs `/cam-review` manually -- a genuine Task-in-context path, not a pane. `subagent-implementer` by contrast has NO Task path at all (absent from the allowlist by design; it is spawned only via `respawn-pane` outside the Task tool). When truthing up the orchestrator prompt's description of the reviewer, describe both paths rather than collapsing to one; either way, in both paths the worker itself never self-pushes its summary via `send-keys` -- the SIDECAR (`notifyOrchestrator` in host.ts / loop.ts) is the sole pusher of `[cam] ... ` lines to the orchestrator pane, confirmed by `formatWorkerReportSummary`/`formatReviewVerdictLine` callers in `src/supervisor/loop.ts`.

- **`journal.md` is newest-at-bottom, so "tail" (not "head") is the grep-on-demand entry point (US-003, CAM-225, 2026-07-09)**: `scripts/cam/journal.md`'s own header states "one entry per completed (or abandoned) cycle, appended in chronological order -- newest at the bottom", so a boot-time "read only the recent tail" instruction has to mean `tail -n <N>` (last lines of the file), not `head`. The old boot-context wording ("read every entry") burned an unbounded, ever-growing read every session; replaced with a tail-only step-3 read plus explicit grep-on-demand guidance for older cycles. Also folded `CAM_ORCH_REHYDRATE` in as an explicit boot **step 0** (it already existed as a Bash-run instruction in `buildOrchestratorBootPrompt`, `src/commands/run.ts:171-176`, but the persona doc's own "Boot context" numbered list never named it), documented `meta_loop=auto` (sidecar auto-dispatches `phase:planning` on idle ticks when `worker_isolation=container`; gated off in host mode per `buildMetaLoopFn`, `src/commands/sidecar.ts:2111-2138`) so the orchestrator doesn't mistake an autonomous planning tick for a stray external command, and noted CAM-189's terminal-verdict SUGGESTION-follow-up-filing hook (`src/supervisor/loop.ts:1966-2010`) files backlog issues the orchestrator itself never created. All four truth-ups landed in the SAME numbered list edit since they share the "Boot context" section and a per-file dual-copy sweep is cheapest done once.

- **CLI thin-proxies split into two mechanisms, not one uniform "inject via send-keys" (US-002, CAM-225, 2026-07-09)**: `cam plan`, `cam next`, and `cam ship` are signal-writers -- they write a `phase`/`active` field directly to `.claude/cam-loop.local.md` (`plan.ts`, `next.ts`, `ship.ts`) and return immediately; nothing touches the orchestrator's pane. The sidecar (`runSupervisor`) polls that file and runs the phase itself (`runPlanPhase`, the implement/review loop, `runShipPhase`). Only `cam review` and `cam issue` are true inject-commands: they detect the live session, wait for idle, then `send-keys` the literal slash-command text (`/cam-review`, `/cam-issue create ...`) into the pane, which the orchestrator then executes in its own context and returns a result line for (`CAM_ISSUE_RESULT=...`, or the reviewer's `<review>` tag). The `/cam-plan` and `/cam-ship` slash-command *files* (`.claude/commands/cam-plan.md`, `cam-ship.md`) mirror the CLI: they write the same signal and narrate, they do NOT run the planning/shipping control-flow themselves. `cam next` has no CLI-mirrored slash-command *content* at all (`.claude/commands/cam-next.md` is architecture reference prose for the orchestrator, not an executable write-the-signal step); `cam next` is one-shot per invocation (flips `active:true` once), the sidecar is what loops across worker dispatches -- never re-issue `/cam-next` in a loop waiting for a `CAM_LOOP_STATUS=COMPLETE` sentinel, that sentinel does not exist anywhere in the codebase. Separately, `issueNumber` in `prd.json` is resolved and consumed at ship time only (`resolveIssueId` in `src/commands/ship-finalize.ts`, called from `runShipPhase`) -- it is pre-flight hygiene guidance for the orchestrator/human, never a gate the supervisor itself enforces before dispatching `/cam-next`.

- **Orchestrator persona `tools:`/`disallowedTools:` frontmatter is advisory-only, not enforced, because the orchestrator is spawned without `--agent` (US-001, CAM-227, 2026-07-09)**: `run.ts:255` and `setup.ts:443/520` launch the orchestrator `claude` process without an `--agent` flag, so nothing reads the persona doc's frontmatter at spawn time to gate tool access (unlike a subagent invoked via `Task()`, which does get `--agent`-enforced allowlisting). Before this story, `templates/agents/subagent-orchestrator.md` listed `Write` under `disallowedTools:` while `.claude/agents/subagent-orchestrator.md` (the dogfood copy, per the cam-dual-copy-is-per-file gotcha) already granted it under `tools:` -- the template's version, if a model actually self-restricted off frontmatter it read, would have blocked the orchestrator's own required self-handoff `Write` (body instruction, "Write the handoff first") and manifested as an exit-3 self-handoff-loop failure. Fixed by moving `Write` to `tools:` in the template (matching the dogfood copy) and adding an explicit advisory-only sentence to the Identity section of BOTH copies pointing at CAM-240 (the tracked follow-up for making the list `--agent`-binding). `WebFetch` stayed in `tools:` deliberately: it is load-bearing for grill-with-docs' spec-interview doc fetches, not dead weight despite an earlier tool-matrix audit's claim.
