- **`readOrchHandoff`'s real required-field set is a strict subset of what a persona doc can plausibly claim, and "close on ship" is backend-conditional, not universal (US-004, CAM-225, 2026-07-09)**: `scripts/cam/orch-handoff.schema.json`'s `required` array and `src/orchestrator/handoff.ts`'s `readOrchHandoff` both enforce exactly `schemaVersion`, `writtenAt`, `reason` -- `projectContext`/`currentCycle`/`keyDecisions`/`openState`/`openQuestions`/`nextActions` are all optional (`additionalProperties: true`, no runtime check on any of them). A persona doc that lists all of them under "the payload must include" over-declares the contract; the fix is to name the 3 real required fields explicitly and reframe the rest as "populate when you have material," not delete them (they are genuinely useful optional fields, `projectContext` was even missing from the enumerated list entirely despite being schema-defined). Separately, `CAM_ORCH_HANDOFF_DUE=true` is emitted from exactly one call site (`index.ts`'s `dispatchJournal`, the `--cycle-close` branch only, confirmed by grep for the literal string) -- a plain `cam journal append` without the flag appends the narrative entry but can never arm the recycle marker, so a doc phrase like "when `cam journal append` emits ..." (flag-less) is misleading even though technically the emitting function is still named `journal append`. For close-on-ship: `runShipPrStep` (`src/release/ship-pr.ts`) already closes the issue for the `local` backend (`closeIssueOnMainFn`) and the `github` backend (`gh issue close`) as part of every `/cam-ship`; only the `linear` backend has no cam-owned auto-close path today (issue closing on Linear stays a manual, primary step, not a fallback). A persona-doc instruction to unconditionally close-on-ship for local/github is a double-close bug waiting to happen, not a safety net -- reframe those two backends' close step as a fallback gated on "check state first, skip if already closed," and single out Linear as the one backend where manual-close-on-ship is still the real, non-fallback path.

- **A negative-grep oracle over a prose rewrite can trip on a coincidental substring match even when the rewrite's intent is satisfied (US-001, CAM-228, 2026-07-09)**: the oracle `! grep -nE "browser verification AND E2E|browser verification.*E2E test"` for `.claude/agents/subagent-auditor.md` C.8 is meant to forbid the OLD requirement ("browser verification AND E2E test"), but `grep -E`'s `.*` has no notion of "this is the old sentence" -- any NEW sentence that happens to contain the literal substring "browser verification" followed anywhere later on the same line by "E2E test" (e.g. as a substring of "E2E tests", plural) re-trips the same regex, even while correctly stating that browser/E2E checks are inapplicable. Fix: when rewriting prose to negate a banned phrase pair, don't reuse the banned phrases verbatim in the negation (swap "browser verification" for "any check that opens a browser", "E2E test(s)" for "end-to-end tests") -- verify with the exact oracle command, not just eyeballing the sentence, since a semantically-correct rewrite can still fail a literal negative-grep.

- **`vendor/README.md`'s `reporter:scripts/smoke/check-agent-frontmatter.ts` source-path notation is the upstream repo's path, not where the file lives in cam-cli (US-001, CAM-247, 2026-07-09)**: a PRD note claimed "Frontmatter smoke lives at scripts/smoke/check-agent-frontmatter.ts" and an AC oracle pinned `bun scripts/smoke/check-agent-frontmatter.ts`, but cam-cli has no `scripts/smoke/` directory at all -- the vendored copy lives at `vendor/check-agent-frontmatter.ts` (confirmed via `vendor/README.md`'s own file table) and `cam init` (`src/commands/init.ts`) invokes it from there via `vendorScriptPath()`, never from a `scripts/smoke/` path in the consuming repo. The `scripts/smoke/` notation in `vendor/README.md`'s "Source" column and the vendored `.sh` wrapper's comments describes the upstream `reporter` monorepo's own layout, not cam-cli's. When an AC oracle path doesn't resolve, check `vendor/README.md`'s file table before escalating as ambiguity -- the intent ("frontmatter smoke passes for both copies") is satisfiable by running `bun vendor/check-agent-frontmatter.ts <path> <path>` against the actual on-disk location.

- **A warn-level runtime guard collapses ALL of one entry's issues into a single event, keyed by array index (US-002, CAM-229, 2026-07-09)**: `validateOfficialDocsValidated` (`src/supervisor/events.ts`) checks each `handoff.officialDocsValidated[]` entry against two independent rules (bad `status` not in the handoff.schema.json enum, and any object key outside `{lib, status, url, fetchedAt, summary}`) but emits at most one `'handoff-schema-warning'` WorkerEvent per offending entry, with both violation kinds folded into that one event's `issues: string[]` array -- this matches the PRD AC's literal wording ("a schema-invalid entry ... produces exactly one warning"), which is per-entry, not per-issue. The guard is called from `loop.ts`'s existing `emit('result', ...)` call site (reusing the same `_readHandoff()` read, not a second fs hit) and never throws or returns a hard-failure outcome -- it is purely observability, wired downstream of `readWorkerOutcome`, so a malformed Step-5.5 entry never blocks a healthy loop. The narrow `Array<{lib?, status?, url?}>` TS type on `HandoffSnapshot.officialDocsValidated` and `HandoffForResult.officialDocsValidated` does NOT strip extra keys at runtime (`host.ts`'s `readHandoff` does a raw `JSON.parse` + type-cast, no field-by-field mapping), so `Object.keys(entry)` inside the guard still sees an `additionalProperties`-violating key like `version` even though the TS type never declared it -- the same "loose read, cast not filter" shape that made the original bug (CAM-229's motivating issue) silent in the first place.

- **`decideNextAction`'s `storyId` was only advisory pre-CAM-224; it is now the authoritative selector for the implement dispatch, with the per-story prompt built by the supervisor, not the worker (US-001, CAM-224, 2026-07-09)**: `runSupervisor`'s implement branch (`loop.ts`) matches `action.storyId` against `prd.userStories` in the same snapshot `decideNextAction` chose from, then calls the new pure `buildImplementerTaskPrompt(story, branchName)` (`src/supervisor/task-prompt.ts`) to embed that exact record's `id`/`title`/`description`/`priority`/`requires`/`acceptanceCriteria` into the worker's task prompt, instead of sending a generic "pick a story yourself" string and letting the worker re-read the full `prd.json`. `PrdSnapshot.userStories` entries needed a new `description?: string` field (`decide.ts`) since the prior shape only had `title`+`notes`. The fallback to the generic `opts.taskPrompt` is kept ONLY for the defensive case where the matched story is absent from the snapshot (should be unreachable: `decideNextAction` picked the id FROM that same snapshot) -- do not delete it, it is an assertion-can't-happen guard, not dead code. The existing `outcome.storyId`-vs-`advisoryStoryId` reconciliation and no-progress-streak guard further down in `loop.ts` (~1330-1350) were deliberately left untouched: the worker still owns the targeted `passes: true` prd.json write; the sidecar does not gain that responsibility just because the story it told the worker to implement is now unambiguous.

- **`scripts/cam/CLAUDE.md` vs `templates/scripts/cam/CLAUDE.md` "Your Task" drift predates any single story and isn't caught by `embed-vendor:check` alone (US-002, CAM-224, 2026-07-09)**: before this story, the two copies' "Your Task" numbered lists already differed in wording ("you're" vs "you are") and in a stale cross-reference (scripts copy said "Step 5.5 in the agent SYSTEM PROMPT", templates copy said "Step 5.5 in `.claude/commands/cam-next.md`" -- a file that no longer documents Step 5.5 at all). `embed-vendor:check` only catches drift between the templates copy and its embedded snapshot in `src/vendor/_generated.ts`; it does NOT compare the two source-of-truth copies against each other, so this class of divergence is silent until a `diff <(sed -n '/## Your Task/,/## Stop Condition/p' ...)` oracle (or a human) checks both files side by side. When a story's AC pins an exact-match diff oracle over a heading-delimited section, rewrite that whole section identically in both files in one edit rather than patching only the lines the story description calls out -- pre-existing unrelated drift in the same section will otherwise fail the oracle even though your targeted edit was correct.

- **`specSource`/`derivedFrom` are real fields on the issue JSON (`src/issues/types.ts`, `scripts/cam/issues/<id>.json`), never fields the orchestrator injects into a planner Task prompt (US-001, CAM-230, 2026-07-09)**: `buildPlannerTaskPrompt`/`buildReplanPlannerTaskPrompt` (`src/supervisor/plan-runner.ts`) only ever embed the target issue id (and, on re-plan, prior auditor findings) into the spawn string -- never a `specSource`/`Spec`/`derivedFrom` field. Both `subagent-planner.md` copies previously described those as if the Task prompt carried them; the fix removed the literal words `specSource`/`derivedFrom` from the prompt file entirely (an AC oracle negative-greps for both) and instead tells the planner to read `scripts/cam/issues/<id>.json` itself (title+description is the spec) and to read parent/ancestor issue files if the issue's own metadata links to any. When an agent `.md` prompt names a Task-prompt field that a grep of the actual `buildXTaskPrompt` builder doesn't show being passed, treat it as a `/cam-plan Step N`-style staleness bug, not a real contract -- check the builder function, not just the field's existence elsewhere in the codebase (it may legitimately exist on a different object, like the issue record here).

- **On-main marker-relative archivers: key blocks on the bullet's own prefix, not a positional threshold (US-001, CAM-231, 2026-07-09)**: `archivePatternsOnMain` (`src/commands/patterns-archive.ts`) reuses `archiveJournalOnMain`'s exact guard/read/commit plumbing (`checkMainUpToDate` -> `git show main:<path>` -> parse -> `commitTreeToMain([2 FileWrite])` -> `pushMainBestEffort`, all shared from `src/git/on-main.ts`) but swaps the selection criterion entirely: journal.md blocks are delimited by `## ` headers after a fixed `ENTRIES_BELOW` marker and selected by "oldest third" (age/count-based); patterns.md has no such marker and its bullets are flat `- ` lines, so blocks are delimited by finding every line starting with `- ` (block = from one such line up to, not including, the next) and selection is a pure per-block regex test (`RESOLVED_MARKER_RE = /\[resolved \d{4}-\d{2}\]/`, exported), never position/age/count. Everything before the first `- ` line is the preamble and is always retained verbatim. When porting an on-main archiver to a file with a different structural convention (line-bullets vs headers-under-a-marker), keep the plumbing (guards, commit, push) byte-identical and only replace the parse/select functions -- do not let the new selection criterion leak into the shared plumbing.

- **A single-subcommand CLI dispatcher can make bare-invocation print help instead of erroring, unlike a multi-subcommand one (US-002, CAM-231, 2026-07-09)**: `parseJournalArgs(['journal'])` treats a missing subcommand as an error (`printFatalHint` + return `null`, exit 1) because `journal` has two real subcommands (`append`, `archive`) and a bare `cam journal` is ambiguous about which one the caller wanted. `parsePatternsArgs` (`index.ts`, mirrors the same shape) deliberately diverges: with only one subcommand (`archive`), `args.length === 0` returns `{ help: true }` (same branch as `--help`/`-h`), so `cam patterns` alone prints usage instead of erroring -- there is nothing ambiguous to disambiguate. `dispatchPatterns` also has no stdin-reading branch at all (unlike `dispatchJournal`'s archive-branches-before-stdin-read comment), since patterns archive is marker-based with zero flags (no `--threshold`, no `--force`, no `--cycle-close`); when mirroring a dispatcher for a strictly-simpler sibling command, drop the now-irrelevant guard comments/branches rather than leaving stub deps that will never be exercised.

- **Sharing a sibling dispatcher's production default fn relies on `function`-declaration hoisting across a module, not textual order (US-003, CAM-226, 2026-07-09)**: `dispatchJournal`'s `--cycle-close` branch (index.ts) auto-invokes `deps?.patternsArchiveFn ?? defaultPatternsArchiveFn` even though `defaultPatternsArchiveFn` is declared ~140 lines LATER in the same file, inside the `cam patterns` dispatch section. This works because `function defaultPatternsArchiveFn() {...}` (not a `const` arrow) hoists to the top of module scope, so referencing it earlier is a normal, safe JS pattern -- no re-declaration or import juggling needed to reuse one dispatcher's real-implementation default from another dispatcher's best-effort call site. The new `patternsArchiveFn` best-effort block sits in its own `try/catch` immediately AFTER the existing journal `archiveFn` best-effort block and strictly BEFORE `armMarker()` (same load-bearing ordering: once the marker is armed, the watcher can SIGTERM the process mid-archive). Tests for this cycle-close auto-invocation live in `test/commands/patterns-cli.test.ts` (not `journal-append.test.ts`), importing `dispatchJournal`/`parseJournalArgs` alongside the patterns exports, since the oracle for the wiring story was pinned to the patterns test file.

- **A PRD story's "dead code" premise can be one day stale relative to a same-day patterns.md correction; verify against source before deleting (US-006, CAM-233, 2026-07-09)**: US-006's acceptance criteria (authored from a 2026-07-08 audit) asserted that `.claude/commands/cam-review.md`'s Step 1 ("Gather context") + Step 2 ("Spawn the reviewer agent", the `Task(subagent_type="subagent-reviewer", ...)` block) no longer runs because the reviewer is spawned by the sidecar via `buildReviewerWorkerArgv`. Cross-checking `src/supervisor/review.ts` (`makeReviewDispatch`/`buildReviewerWorkerArgv`), `.claude/agents/subagent-orchestrator.md` ("Inject commands: review, issue" + "Implementer and reviewer workers" sections), and this same file's own patterns.md bullet from CAM-225 (written 2026-07-09, one day AFTER the audit that produced US-006) showed the premise was wrong: the manual/on-demand `/cam-review` path (triggered by a human via `cam review` or by the orchestrator itself) genuinely still runs Step 0-5 inside the orchestrator's own context via `Task()` -- `subagent-reviewer` is deliberately kept on the `orch-agent-allowlist.sh` PreToolUse allowlist precisely for this path. Only the *autonomous* sidecar review pass (between story batches, in the titled 3rd worker pane) bypasses this file entirely via `buildReviewerWorkerArgv`. Resolution: kept Step 0-5 intact (they are load-bearing for the manual path) and added a short "Relationship to the autonomous sidecar review pass" paragraph (both `.claude/commands/cam-review.md` and `templates/commands/cam-review.md`) naming `buildReviewerWorkerArgv`/`makeReviewDispatch` as the separate autonomous mechanism, rather than deleting the manual dispatch steps as the AC literally requested. When an AC's factual premise about "dead" code contradicts a more recent, independently-verified patterns.md entry or persona doc, re-verify against the actual source file before deleting anything -- deleting a still-load-bearing manual code path to satisfy a stale audit would have been a regression disguised as a hygiene fix.

- **`Agent` vs `Skill` in subagent `tools:` frontmatter, confirmed against code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents (US-001, CAM-240, 2026-07-09)**: when a persona is spawned as the main thread with `claude --agent`, `tools:` becomes runtime-binding (unlike a plain `claude` invocation, where it is advisory-only per the pre-existing note in `subagent-orchestrator.md`'s Identity section). Two gotchas verified live against the official docs during CAM-240's US-001: (a) "If `Agent` is omitted from the `tools` list entirely, the agent can't spawn any subagents" -- the load-bearing grant is the literal string `Agent`, not `Task` (a legacy alias that may additionally be listed but must not stand alone); `subagent-orchestrator.md` had been missing `Agent` from its `tools:` list even though its own body already documented Task/Agent-gated subagent spawning via a PreToolUse hook -- the frontmatter had drifted from the body's documented runtime behavior. (b) **Correction (US-001, CAM-255, 2026-07-09):** the original CAM-240 read of this line was wrong. "To preload Skills into context, use the `skills` field rather than listing `Skill` here" is a caveat about *preloading* (injecting full skill content at startup), not a statement that `Skill` is invalid as a `tools:` entry. Re-verified against both docs pages: `/en/tools-reference` lists `Skill` in its built-in tools table ("Executes a skill within the main conversation"), and `/en/sub-agents` itself says "the subagent can still discover and invoke project, user, and plugin skills through the `Skill` tool during execution... To prevent a subagent from invoking skills entirely, omit `Skill` from the `tools` list or add it to `disallowedTools`" -- which only makes sense if `Skill` is a real, grantable/denyable `tools:` entry. CAM-255's US-001 restored `Skill` to `subagent-orchestrator.md`'s `tools:` list (self-invoking `/cam-review`/`/cam-issue` under `--agent`) on this corrected understanding. `skills:` (plural) remains a separate frontmatter field for preloading full skill content at startup; the two are not mutually exclusive.

- **Threading a new provenance field through the SUGGESTION-follow-up pipeline touches 3 layers, not just the filing site (US-001, CAM-263, 2026-07-10)**: `PrdSnapshot.issueNumber` (`src/supervisor/decide.ts`, read verbatim by `readPrd`, never parsed out of the `cam/issue-<n>` branchName string) -> `FollowUpProvenance.parentIssue` (`src/supervisor/suggestion-followups.ts`, populated by the `loop.ts` terminal-verdict hook alongside the existing `source`/`round` fields) -> `makeProductionFileSuggestionsFn` (`src/commands/sidecar.ts`), which resolves it via `resolveIssueId(provenance.parentIssue, prefix)` (prefix read the same way `createLocalIssueOnMain` itself reads `issue_prefix`, via `parseToml`+`readProjectToml`) and passes `derivedFrom:[<id>]` into `createLocalIssueOnMain` only when non-null. `createLocalIssueOnMain`/`writeIssueFile` already supported `derivedFrom` end-to-end from the CAM-144 fast-track work -- no new writer field or schema edit needed. Passing `derivedFrom` alone (no `specSource`) is schema-valid and deliberately keeps the filed issue at `stage:'idea'`: the `specSource:'derived'` coupling (stage->'specified' + derivedFrom-required guardrail) only fires when `specSource` is explicitly set, so bare `derivedFrom` bypasses `applyFastTrackGuardrails` entirely. `resolveIssueId` already guards `undefined`/`null`/`<=0` and returns `null`, so "parent issue genuinely unavailable" naturally omits `derivedFrom` via the writer's existing spread-when-non-empty pattern -- no extra guard needed at the call site.

- **`SlashCommand` was never a real tool name and slipped through CAM-240 US-001 undetected (US-001, CAM-254, 2026-07-09)**: CAM-240 flipped `subagent-orchestrator.md`'s `tools:` frontmatter to runtime-binding (`--agent` spawn) but left `SlashCommand` in the list and in the body prose ("use the `SlashCommand` tool when available", under "Inject commands: review, issue"). Neither `/en/sub-agents` nor `/en/tools-reference` documents a tool named `SlashCommand`; the real inject mechanism for `/cam-review` and `/cam-issue` was already, correctly, described one sentence later as CLI thin-proxy `send-keys` into the orchestrator's pane. Once `--agent` made `tools:` enforced, the dangling entry surfaced as a live `No such tool available` error the first time the orchestrator body's own prose nudged it to reach for the non-existent tool. Fix was pure deletion (one frontmatter line, one prose clause referencing it) with no replacement needed, since the sanctioned path was already fully documented alongside it. Lesson for future frontmatter audits: cross-check every `tools:` entry against `/en/tools-reference`'s actual tool name list, not just against what the persona's own body prose assumes exists.

- **Flipping the orchestrator spawn to `--agent` shrinks the boot-nudge to a config-driven fork, not a content migration test (US-002, CAM-240, 2026-07-09)**: once `claude --agent subagent-orchestrator` auto-loads the persona body as the system prompt, `buildOrchestratorBootPrompt()` (src/commands/run.ts) no longer needs to instruct reading the persona file, writing `.cam-orch-ready`, rehydrating from `CAM_ORCH_REHYDRATE`, or deriving the backlog -- all of that already lives in the agent body's "Boot context" section (folded in by CAM-240 US-001). The function keeps exactly one job: forking on `readMetaLoop()` so the first user turn either announces autonomous mode (no closing question) or asks "What would you like to do?" under `off`/`observe`. Gotcha: don't literally quote the prohibited phrase inside the "do NOT ask ..." instruction string for the `auto` branch -- a test asserting `.not.toContain('What would you like to do?')` will false-fail on the instruction's own negation clause; phrase the prohibition without repeating the literal banned string. The two describe-block casualties of this shrink (`test/run.test.ts`'s old boot-prompt suite and `test/vendor/orchestrator-backlog-derivation.test.ts`'s boot-prompt half) had to be rewritten/pruned in the same story, not left red -- their old assertions pinned content that is now correctly absent from the boot-prompt string (it survives only in the embedded persona doc, asserted by the surviving describe block). The `--agent subagent-orchestrator` flag itself has TWO independent spawn sites that must move together: `run.ts`'s `buildOrchestratorPaneCommand` bash-wrapper claude invocation, and `setup.ts`'s `buildSetupMenuScript()` generated `handoff()` bash function (the fresh-init orchestrator split) -- missing the second leaves a fresh `cam init` boot with no system prompt at all, since the boot-nudge is now insufficient on its own once the imperatives moved to the body. `test/setup-menu.test.ts`'s existing `bash -n` syntax-check test is not a stand-in for behavioral coverage; add a direct `toContain('--agent subagent-orchestrator')` assertion. Confirmed against a `claude --agent` community FAQ + docs cross-check (2026-07-09): "the main thread's tool access... only tools allowed by the agent are available" -- i.e. `tools:`/`disallowedTools:` genuinely gates at the tool-call layer once `--agent` is the spawn mode, not merely at persona-doc read time.

- **`git diff --name-only main | wc -l` file-count oracles are structurally unsatisfiable in the cam workflow (US-R1-001, CAM-256, 2026-07-10)**: the cam-loop itself commits `scripts/cam/prd.json` and `scripts/cam/handoff.json` to the working branch alongside the story's real edit, so an AC oracle asserting "exactly N files changed vs main" will always overcount by the scaffolding writes even when the story's actual diff is scoped correctly. Reviewer-caught in CAM-256's Layer B re-run (3 files vs. expected 1). Fix pattern for any future "no file other than X is modified" oracle: scope the `git diff` with a pathspec exclusion before the grep/count, e.g. `git diff --name-only main -- ':(exclude)scripts/cam/' | grep -qx '<path>' && [ $(git diff --name-only main -- ':(exclude)scripts/cam/' | wc -l) -eq 1 ]`. Any planner-authored AC that counts changed files against `main` must exclude `scripts/cam/` this way, or it will hard-fail Layer B on every story regardless of correctness.

- **`respawn-pane` workers inherit the tmux server's OS env, not a per-session table; a new isolation-gated env-strip needs an `isolation?: WorkerIsolation` field threaded through EVERY argv-builder options interface, defaulting to `'host'` (US-001, CAM-242, 2026-07-10)**: `cam run` starts the `tmux -L cam` server with `.env` loaded, so any var in that `.env` (e.g. `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`) lives in the server process's OS env and every `respawn-pane` worker inherits it -- overriding the interactive config-dir login even though `tmux set-environment -g -u` looks like the fix (it only mutates the session-level table, never the live process's OS env the child actually forks from). The durable fix is a SECOND env-strip list alongside the existing `WORKER_ENV_UNSET` (CAM-43's nesting-detection strip): `HOST_ONLY_ENV_UNSET` in `worker-argv.ts`, applied by `workerEnvPrefix(isolation)` only when `isolation === 'host'`. Making the `isolation` PARAMETER OF `workerEnvPrefix` itself required (not defaulted) is cheap and forces every one of its 4 call sites (inside `buildImplementerWorkerArgv`/`buildReviewerWorkerArgv`/`buildPlannerWorkerArgv`/`buildAuditorWorkerArgv`) to be explicit. But making the PUBLIC `isolation?: WorkerIsolation` field on each builder's own Options interface OPTIONAL-with-`?? 'host'` default (mirroring the existing `workerIsolation ?? 'host'` convention already used at `review.ts:347`/`loop.ts:706`/`plan-runner.ts:715`) keeps the blast radius to the ~4 production call sites that thread the caller's real `workerIsolation` through, instead of forcing every one of the dozens of pre-existing test call sites (`buildReviewerWorkerArgv({ uuid: SAMPLE_UUID })` with no isolation) to add an explicit field just to keep compiling. Container parity tests should assert DIVERGENCE, not byte-equality: build both the host and the (unwrapped, pre-`dockerExecWrap`) container inner string from the same fixture inputs, then assert `hostCmd.replace('-u CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN ', '') === containerInner` -- proving the token strip is the ONLY delta, without hardcoding the rest of the `env -u ...` prefix twice.

- **Deriving a hand-copied runtime constant from a JSON schema file: enable `resolveJsonModule`, import the schema by relative path, walk it with plain dot-notation property access (US-001, CAM-248, 2026-07-10)**: `resolveJsonModule` needs no companion tsconfig flag (confirmed against TS docs) and, once set, `import handoffSchema from '../../scripts/cam/handoff.schema.json'` gives TS a literal-typed object inferred straight from the file's static shape -- so `handoffSchema.properties.officialDocsValidated.items.properties.status.enum` and `Object.keys(handoffSchema.properties.officialDocsValidated.items.properties)` typecheck with zero casts and zero `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` guards, because named dot-notation property access on a statically-known JSON literal type is never the same as array/index access. `bun build --compile` inlines the JSON import at build time (verified via `bun run build:release`), so this has no runtime file dependency in the shipped binary. This is the general pattern for retiring any hand-copied "source of truth: some `.json` file" comment elsewhere in the codebase: replace the literal array/Set with a derived read off a real JSON import instead of trusting a comment to keep two hand-written copies in sync.

- **Widening a single required-check constant to a required-check SET touches every fixture that hand-builds a GET-verification response, not just the source module (US-002, CAM-244, 2026-07-10)**: `configureBranchProtection` (`src/release/branch-protection.ts`) generalized its single `CI_CHECK_NAME` const to a `REQUIRED_CHECK_NAMES` tuple (`['ci', 'ci-container']`) and its `hasCiCheck` predicate to `hasAllRequiredChecks` (an `.every()` over the tuple against either the `checks[]` or deprecated `contexts[]` GET shape). The PUT body change (`checks: REQUIRED_CHECK_NAMES.map((context) => ({ context }))`) is a one-site edit, but downstream consumer test suites (`test/commands/setup-merge-mode.test.ts`, `test/commands/config-merge-mode.test.ts`) hand-roll their OWN local `protectionResponseChecks()` fixture (copy-pasted, not imported from the branch-protection test file) that only stubbed `checks: [{ context: 'ci' }]`; once the predicate started requiring both contexts, those fixtures silently flipped `configured-and-verified` to `fallback-warned` in 4 unrelated tests. Grep every test file for the fixture-builder's literal shape (`grep -rln "context: 'ci' }"`) before declaring a required-check-set change complete, since `bun test test/release/branch-protection.test.ts` alone stays green while the full `bun test` run catches the fallout. Confirmed against the current GitHub REST docs (`PUT /repos/{owner}/{repo}/branches/{branch}/protection`): the `checks[]` (current) and deprecated `contexts[]` forms both appear in the GET response simultaneously, so accepting either form per-check-name (not per-response) is the correct verification shape for a multi-check required set.

- **The orchestrator-exit wrapper's teardown branch must never `rm` the loop state file (US-002, CAM-195, 2026-07-11, Defect 3, second half)**: `buildOrchestratorPaneCommand` (`src/commands/run.ts`) previously did `rm -f ${q(opts.stateFile)}` in its teardown ("else", clean-`/exit`) branch, deliberately timed before `readyMarker`/`pidMarker` removal and `kill-session`. That rm is the second wedge trigger alongside the US-001 fix: an orchestrator recycle under a running sidecar (any respawn-cap teardown, or a plain `/exit` with no pending handoff) deleted `cam-loop.local.md` out from under the sidecar mid-cycle, erasing the in-flight phase signal it was polling. Fix is a pure deletion, not a rewrite: remove only that one `rm -f` line; `readyMarker`/`pidMarker` removal is untouched and still fires in the same branch. `cam stop` (`performStop`, `stop.ts`) remains the single deliberate remover of the state file (per the CAM-67 six-marker invariant, patterns.md line ~56) -- this is intentional asymmetry: an operator-invoked `cam stop` is a real teardown-and-forget, while an orchestrator-pane recycle/exit under a live sidecar is not. `OrchestratorPaneCommandOptions.stateFile` stays on the interface (still read by other call sites building the options object) even though the wrapper body itself no longer references `opts.stateFile` at all -- an unused-in-body field is not dead code here, it is retained shape for the caller-supplied options bag. Test pattern for proving a teardown-branch file-preservation invariant without tmux: build the wrapper string via `buildOrchestratorPaneCommand`, run it directly with `bash -c` (no real tmux session), and use a fake `claude` script on `PATH` that exits 0 immediately without writing a handoff file -- the `[ -f handoffMarker ]` guard is then false on the very first loop iteration, so control falls straight into the teardown branch deterministically (no polling/timeouts needed); `kill-session` against a nonexistent tmux session fails harmlessly since the wrapper has no `set -e`. See `test/integration/orch-exit-preserves-state.test.ts`.

- **Re-arm's "drain preconditions" are a DIFFERENT, narrower set than `evaluateDrainPreconditions` -- do not conflate the two (US-003, CAM-195, 2026-07-11, Defect 1)**: the AC phrase "gated on the SAME drain preconditions as meta-loop dispatch" is defined by its own parenthetical (no blocked terminal, no pending merge-watch, no plan/ship phase in progress), NOT by literally calling `evaluateDrainPreconditions` (`drain-preconditions.ts`, container-active + plan_approval=auto) -- that evaluator gates the meta-loop's NEW-cycle auto-dispatch and is an autonomy escalation gate, unrelated to resuming an ALREADY-in-flight PRD. The new `evaluateRearmPreconditions` (`src/supervisor/rearm-preconditions.ts`) is a separate pure predicate wired UNCONDITIONALLY (regardless of meta_loop/worker_isolation config) via `makeProductionRearmImplementingFn` (`sidecar.ts`) and a `rearmImplementingFn` option on `RunSidecarLoopOptions` (`loop.ts`), checked in the `active !== true` idle branch BEFORE `runMergeWatchFn`/`runMetaLoopObserveFn` so a resumable PRD is never shadowed by a new backlog dispatch. "No blocked terminal" falls out for free by reusing `makeHasPendingStories()` as the `prdInFlight` input: it already returns `false` at the MAX_ROUNDS_DEBT terminal (CAM-109 guard), so no separate verdict read was needed. "No plan/ship phase in progress" likewise falls out for free from requiring an EXACT `phase==='implementing'` match (`LoopPhase` is a mutually-exclusive enum): `phase:idle` -- the parked discriminator -- and `phase:planning`/`phase:shipping` all fail the same one check. This outer loop has no separate "boot" preamble, so a story requiring "at sidecar boot AND at the idle-tick" needs only ONE call site: the very first tick of `runSidecarLoop`'s `while(true)` already covers boot.

- **`finishTerminal` (`runSupervisor`, `loop.ts`) now emits a loop-level `'sidecar-exit'` event on EVERY terminal, not just crash/self-exit paths (US-004, CAM-195, 2026-07-11, Defect 2)**: prior `'sidecar-exit'` emit sites (plan/ship-phase-crash, session-absent) were all in the OUTER `runSidecarLoop`, keyed to a real worker/story context. `finishTerminal` is INNER (`runSupervisor`), called on every `blocked`/`complete`/`awaiting-operator`/`max-iterations` return, including terminals with no worker ever dispatched (e.g. an unreadable PRD) -- so there is no per-story `uuid` in scope at its definition site (it is declared once, before the `outer: while` loop, not per-iteration). Fixed uuid `'supervisor'` (never a real worker session id) and `storyId` sourced from `lastIterProgress.currentStoryId` (undefined on the `complete`/`awaiting-operator` action kinds, since those aren't `'implement'` actions) are both intentional, not gaps. The `SupervisorStatus -> reason` mapping is an explicit `Record<SupervisorStatus, string>` lookup (`TERMINAL_REASON`) rather than passing `status` straight through, so a future status can diverge from its logged reason string without touching the call site. Gotcha for a full-lifecycle sequence test (`test/supervisor/events.test.ts`) that asserts an exact `events.map(e => e.kind)` array and `new Set(events.map(e => e.uuid))`: both now need the trailing `'sidecar-exit'` kind and the extra `'supervisor'` uuid appended, and any assertion filtering "every event's storyId equals the completed story" must also exclude `'sidecar-exit'` (undefined storyId on that terminal). `emit` is declared textually AFTER `finishTerminal` in the same function scope but is referenced from inside `finishTerminal`'s closure body, not evaluated at definition time -- forward-referencing a later `const` closure from an earlier one in the same scope is safe in TS/JS as long as invocation happens after both are initialized (mirrors `blockedResult` calling the earlier-defined `finishTerminal`).

- **The durable implement-blocked marker's `issueId` is `prd.issueNumber` stringified, not a backlog issue-id string like `state.issueId` elsewhere (US-005, CAM-195, 2026-07-11, Defect 2)**: `src/supervisor/implement-blocked-marker.ts` (`IMPLEMENT_BLOCKED_FILENAME`, `ImplementBlockedMarker { issueId, story, reason, writtenAt }`) is the third marker mirroring the ship-stalled/plan-escalation precedent, but its `issueId` field is deliberately `String(prd.issueNumber)`, NOT the `state.issueId` backlog-id string used by `updateShipStalledMarker` -- inside `runSupervisor`, only `PrdSnapshot.issueNumber` (the GitHub issue number the PRD cycle backs) is ever in scope. `finishTerminal` (`loop.ts`) is the single write seam for ALL terminals (mirroring the US-004 `'sidecar-exit'` event seam), guarded on `status === 'blocked' && lastIssueNumber !== undefined`: `lastIssueNumber` is a mutable outer variable set right after every successful (non-null) `readPrd()`, because `finishTerminal` itself is declared BEFORE the per-iteration `const prd = readPrd()` (same forward-reference-safe pattern as `emit`) and so cannot close over a per-iteration `prd` directly. `reason` falls back to `lastOutcome?.detail ?? TERMINAL_REASON.blocked`: every one of the 7 `blockedResult`/inline `finishTerminal('blocked')` call sites in the implement/review branches sets `lastOutcome.detail` immediately before the terminal, EXCEPT the very first `prd === null` guard and the `blocked-no-implementable` branch (both fire before any worker outcome exists), which fall back to the generic `'blocked'` string. The CONSUME side lives in `sidecar.ts`'s `makeProductionRearmImplementingFn`: re-arming implementing (US-003's Defect-1 fix) IS "the next implement dispatch for that issue" per this story's AC2, since `hasPendingStoriesFn` already scopes to the CURRENT `scripts/cam/prd.json` (cam-cli's single-hub model means there is only ever one active PRD/issue at a time) -- `clearImplementBlockedMarkerForCurrentIssue(markerPath, prdPath)` reads the current prd's `issueNumber`, compares it (stringified) against the marker's `issueId`, and removes ONLY on a match (or unconditionally when the current prd is unreadable, since there is nothing to compare against); a marker for a DIFFERENT issueId is left untouched, mirroring the "caller's responsibility to check issueId match first" contract already documented (but not actually enforced at any existing call site) on `removeShipStalledMarker`/`removePlanEscalatedMarker`.

- **Dedup a re-fired EVENT without suppressing the underlying RETRY ACTION: the CAM-98 `dispatched:true` storm fix (US-007, CAM-195, 2026-07-11, Defect 2 rider)**: the ~4.7s storm was NOT the meta-loop dispatcher misbehaving -- it was `exitPhaseAfterPlan` (`sidecar.ts`) correctly resetting `phase` back to `'idle'` every time `runPlanPhase` returns `{ kind: 'mutex-busy' }` (pane-count mutex busy; a legitimate, must-keep-working retry path), which made the SAME still-open backlog issue look freshly dispatchable to `dispatchOrDrain` on every idle tick, since no PRD was ever created to trip the existing `handleBlockedCycleBoundary`/`prdPresentFn` in-flight guard. The fix is a `DispatchClosureState.pendingIssueId: string | undefined` field compared by VALUE (`selected.id`/`resolved.id`), not a boolean flag: `deps.setPhaseFn(...)` is called UNCONDITIONALLY on every dispatch attempt (so the mutex-busy retry keeps firing correctly), but the `'meta-loop-dispatch' {dispatched:true}` event is only emitted when `ctx.pendingIssueId !== selected.id`, mirroring `blockedCycleEmitted`/`stoppedEmitted`'s existing shape (a dedup flag gates the OBSERVABLE side-effect, never the structural park/retry itself -- see e.g. `handleKillSwitchBoundary`, where the early-return already blocks every tick regardless of the boolean). No explicit reset hook is needed or was added: the guard self-clears the instant a DIFFERENT issue id is selected, because that only happens once the previous issue's cycle actually reaches a terminal and vacates the top of the backlog -- comparison-by-identity IS the reset. Generalize: when a retry loop's OWN state machine already decides whether to retry (here, `runPlanPhase`'s mutex probe), a dedup fix for its noisy observability side-channel must NEVER also gate the retry call itself, or you convert a noisy-but-working retry into a silent permanent wedge. This is also why AC1's three sibling skip paths (`cycle-in-flight` in `handleBlockedCycleBoundary`, `merge-watch-present`, `phase-not-idle` in `makeProductionMetaLoopDispatchFn`) got NO dedup at all: those three are structural EARLY RETURNS already guaranteed to fire every tick they're hit (the story only asked to stop them being silent, not to rate-limit them), so adding dedup there would have been unrequested scope, not a fix.

- **Widening a marker's required schema fields ripples through an `Omit<Marker, ...>` seam type + every intermediate spread, even when the story's own scope is "just the pure module" (US-001, CAM-214, 2026-07-11)**: adding required `consecutiveCount`/`keyHash` to `ImplementBlockedMarker` (`src/supervisor/implement-blocked-marker.ts`) broke typecheck two hops away: `loop.ts`'s `ImplementBlockedWriterParams = Omit<ImplementBlockedMarker, 'writtenAt'>` seam type (deliberately narrow so the pure `loop.ts` stays clock-free) needed the new fields added to its `Omit` list too, and `host.ts`'s production writer (`{ ...params, writtenAt: ... }`) then needed a placeholder value for each newly-required field (`consecutiveCount: 1, keyHash: ''`) to keep the object literal well-typed, since the real `advanceBlockedMarker` wiring into that writer is explicitly a LATER story (US-002) per the PRD's own `requires` chain. Pattern: when a PRD splits "extend a marker's pure schema/transition fn" from "wire it into the production writer" across sequential stories, the schema-only story still owns fixing every downstream compile break the wider required-field set causes -- but the fix must stay a type-level `Omit` widening + a dumb placeholder default, never a premature partial-implementation of the next story's actual logic (no reading prd.json's hash, no calling `advanceBlockedMarker`, from this story's writer). Comment the placeholder inline naming which future story replaces it, so a reviewer doesn't mistake it for real dedup behavior.

- **`buildSupervisorOptions(cwd)` is safe to call against a bare temp dir to test a single host.ts writer closure end-to-end, no tmux/mocking needed (US-002, CAM-214, 2026-07-11)**: wiring `readImplementBlockedMarker`/`advanceBlockedMarker`/`computePrdHashForBlockedMarker` into `host.ts`'s `writeImplementBlockedMarkerFn` needed a test proving the REAL production closure (not the `runSupervisor`-level fakes already covering the pure `loop.ts` seam in `test/supervisor/implement-blocked-marker-wiring.test.ts`). Following the `auto-ship-real-writers.test.ts`/`commit-exists-real-git.test.ts` precedent, `buildSupervisorOptions(cwd)` only builds closures at call time (confirmed again here): a `mkdtempSync` dir with just `.claude/` + `scripts/cam/prd.json` created is enough to call `opts.writeImplementBlockedMarkerFn` directly and assert on the marker file it writes, with zero tmux/spawn side effects triggered merely by the factory call. Also confirmed via Bun docs (bun.com/docs/api/hashing): `Bun.CryptoHasher('sha256').update()` accepts a `Buffer` directly (`readFileSync(path)` with no encoding arg), so hashing "prd.json's raw file bytes" needs no `Bun.file()`/`.arrayBuffer()` detour -- `node:fs`'s `readFileSync` Buffer feeds straight into `.update()`. To prove the hash is content-derived (not path- or clock-derived) without duplicating `advanceBlockedMarker`'s own unit tests, drive two SEPARATE temp cwds with byte-identical `prd.json` content and assert their resulting `keyHash`es are equal.

- **A circuit breaker for a re-dispatch auto-chain belongs INSIDE the existing gate condition, not as a standalone check (US-003, CAM-214, 2026-07-11)**: gating `flipActiveFn`'s re-dispatch on the durable implement-blocked marker's `escalated:true` (`loop.ts`'s `'US-005: Auto-chain in auto mode'` block) could have read the marker unconditionally at the top of the tick, but nesting the `readImplementBlockedMarkerFn` read and the `escalated` check INSIDE the existing `opts.flipActiveFn !== undefined && opts.hasPendingStories()` `if` makes the breaker automatically inert whenever `flipActiveFn` itself is absent (operator/ci-gated mode) -- zero extra code needed to satisfy "default behavior unchanged", and the marker is never even read on a tick where the auto-chain wouldn't have fired anyway. The halt is recorded via the EXISTING `logEvent`/`'sidecar-exit'` emitter (a free-form `Record<string, unknown>` detail carrying `{ reason: 'circuit-broken', consecutiveCount }`) rather than a new `WorkerEventKind`: `WorkerEventDetail`'s union already includes a `Record<string, unknown>` fallback member, so a new reason string needs no type-level change at all, mirroring the existing `'plan-phase-crash-outer'`/`'ship-phase-crash-outer'`/`'session-absent'` reason strings already emitted through the same `'sidecar-exit'` kind elsewhere in `loop.ts`. Recovery is automatic and needs no special-case code: since the marker is read FRESH every tick (never cached across cycles), the next tick where `clearImplementBlockedMarkerForCurrentIssue` (a DONE outcome) or `advanceBlockedMarker`'s hash-based reset (a PRD amendment, US-002) has cleared/un-escalated the marker, the very same `if` branch's `else` arm fires `flipActiveFn()` again with no additional plumbing. Test-file gotcha: when the pinned oracle file (here `test/supervisor/sidecar-loop.test.ts`) doesn't already have a `driveOneTick`-style per-cycle harness (that one lives in the sibling `test/supervisor/auto-chain.test.ts`), duplicate a small self-contained `driveOneAutoChainTick` helper locally rather than importing across test files -- the two files intentionally test the same `loop.ts` block from different angles (regression coverage vs. this story's pinned oracle) and should stay independently readable.

- **Pre-existing dual-copy drift found in `.claude/agents/subagent-orchestrator.md` vs `templates/agents/subagent-orchestrator.md` (US-004, CAM-214, 2026-07-11)**: the whole CAM-195 "implement blocked" boot-read section (numbered item 10 + the `.cam-implement-blocked.json` blocker-line paragraph) existed only in `templates/`, never synced into the `.claude/` runtime mirror -- confirmed via `git show HEAD:<path>` on both copies before touching either, so this predates this story. `embed-vendor:check` does NOT catch this class of drift: it only diffs `templates/` against the generated `src/vendor/_generated.ts`, never `templates/` against `.claude/` directly, so a story that edits only one of the two dual copies passes the vendor-drift gate silently. When a story's AC requires the `.claude/` mirror to carry specific new text and the surrounding section is itself missing there, restoring the missing surrounding paragraph (not just the new sentence) is the minimal coherent fix, not scope creep -- verify byte-parity afterward with a plain `diff` between the two files for that section before running `embed-vendor`.

- **`runPostMerge`'s failure variant now carries a step-vocabulary trail via a `failAt(step, reason)` helper (US-002, CAM-241/174, 2026-07-11)**: `PostMergeOutcome`'s `ok:false` branch gained `completedSteps: string[]` + `remainingSteps: string[]` derived from a single ordered `const ALL_STEPS = ['checkout', 'pull-rebase', 'tag', 'prune', 'close']`. `failAt(step, reason)` slices `ALL_STEPS` at the failing step's index (everything before = completed, the failing step itself + everything after = remaining) so every failure site (`checkout-main-failed`, `rebase-failed`, `version-file-read-failed`, `version-parse-failed`, `tag-create-failed`, `tag-push-failed`) returns a consistent shape instead of a bare `{ ok:false, reason }`. `prune` and `close` never appear as the failing step because both are best-effort/non-fatal (they can never produce an `ok:false` outcome). Any other production/test fixture constructing a `PostMergeOutcome & { ok:false }` literal by hand (`merge-watch.test.ts`, `sidecar-loop.test.ts`) must add the two new required fields (`completedSteps: []`/`remainingSteps: []` is fine when the test doesn't assert on them) or typecheck fails. Also: `git pull --rebase origin main` (never `--reapply-cherry-picks`, never `git reset --hard`) replaces the old plain `git pull origin main` -- a subsumed local commit (already squash-merged into the PR) drops cleanly via patch-id comparison during the rebase and the existing tag/prune/close continuation runs in the same call; a genuine un-squashed divergence fails the rebase loudly (`reason: 'rebase-failed'`) instead of silently discarding work.

- **`sendKeysVerified` extraction: composer-emptied verify + bounded retry, logEvent optional-no-side-effect seam (US-002, CAM-200, 2026-07-11)**: `sendKeysWhenIdle` (`src/tmux/dispatch.ts`) now delegates to a new exported `sendKeysVerified`, which adds a POST-send delivery check on top of the existing PRE-send `isOrchPaneIdle` gate: a busy TUI can drop the trailing Enter even after the pane looked idle at check time (capture-pane lags one refresh cycle), silently losing the wake-up. The verify step (`isComposerEmptied`) is a pane-STATE check only -- it looks for the pushed `text` string still sitting in the tail lines of a fresh `capture-pane`, never for parsed report content (capture-pane is rendered markdown and lossy for structured content per the CAM-75/77/78 decision). Retries reuse the existing pure `computeBackoffMs` (`src/supervisor/loop.ts:606`) with LOCAL (not the supervisor's 60s/300s) base/max constants scoped to this fast UI-retry context, up to `maxAttempts` (default 3) total send attempts; on exhaustion it emits `'push-undelivered'` via an injected `logEvent?: (kind, detail) => void` callback rather than the full `WorkerEventLogger` (event-object) shape -- this mirrors the "sub-state-machine logEvent seam" pattern (merge-watch, US-008/CAM-101): the low-level helper doesn't know ts/storyId/uuid, so it hands the caller a bare `(kind, detail)` pair to wrap into a full `WorkerEvent`, and omitting `logEvent` entirely is a zero-side-effect no-op (no default file writing), which is what let `sendKeysWhenIdle`'s existing callers (review.ts/issue.ts/spec.ts) get verify+retry for free with NO signature-shape change at their call sites. Cycle-safety note: `dispatch.ts` importing `computeBackoffMs`/`JITTER_FRACTION` from `../supervisor/loop.ts` is safe because `loop.ts` has no import of `tmux/dispatch.ts` (verified via grep before adding the import) -- re-verify this both ways before adding a similar cross-module import elsewhere in this codebase, since a cycle here would silently break bundling/typecheck in confusing ways.

- **`makeNotifyOrchestrator` wired to `sendKeysVerified`: capture-pane reader + logEvent adapter threaded through all 4 call-sites (US-003, CAM-200, 2026-07-11)**: `makeNotifyOrchestrator(sessionName, spawnFn, capturePaneFn?, logEvent?)` (`src/supervisor/host.ts`) gained two new optional params so the sidecar report pusher gets US-002's idle-gate + composer-emptied verify + bounded retry for free, replacing the old fire-and-forget `spawnFn('tmux', argv, { stdio: 'ignore' })`. Two small factories ship alongside it: `makeCapturePaneFn(spawnFn)` builds a bare `capture-pane -p -t <paneId>` reader over any `TmuxSpawnFn` (for callers, e.g. `sidecar.ts`, that only have a raw spawnFn in scope, not a ready-made capture closure); `adaptLogEventForPush(logEvent: WorkerEventLogger)` downcasts the full `(event) => void` logger to the bare `(kind, detail) => void` seam `sendKeysVerified` expects, fixing `uuid: 'sidecar'` and `storyId: undefined` (a push-undelivered narration is never tied to one story/session). All 4 call-sites (`host.ts` reuses its own real `capturePane` closure + `logEvent`; the 3 `sidecar.ts` sites build a fresh `makeCapturePaneFn(realSpawnFn)` each call) now pass both explicitly rather than relying on `sendKeysVerified`'s internal default-from-spawnFn fallback, per AC2's explicit threading requirement. **Real-tmux test fixture gotcha**: a raw `cat` pane never renders a claude-style `>` prompt, so `isOrchPaneIdle`'s tail-5-lines check never matches on its own and the idle-gate polls the full 5s `idleTimeoutMs` budget before falling back to send-anyway -- since `Bun.sleepSync` blocks synchronously, this pushes a naive real-tmux integration test past bun's default 5s per-test timeout even though the code is correct. Fix: (1) prime the pane by typing a bare `>` + Enter before calling `notify()` so `isOrchPaneIdle` matches on the very first capture; (2) shrink the pane height (e.g. `-y 5` instead of `-y 10`) so the primed prompt line stays inside the LAST-5-lines tail window instead of being pushed out by trailing blank filler rows below the cursor (capture-pane always returns exactly `pane_height` rows, blank-padded). Also note: a `cat` fixture's own echo means the pushed text is NEVER "composer-emptied" (it stays visible in scrollback forever, unlike a real TUI that consumes/clears it), so a real-tmux `notify()` call against a `cat` pane always exhausts all `maxAttempts` retries before returning -- harmless for assertions that only check final `capture-pane` content via `toContain`, but budget the wall-clock accordingly (small backoff sleeps, still well under the 5s per-test timeout with `maxAttempts:3`).

- **Single unconditional exit-teardown via try/finally beats enumerated per-kind teardown calls (US-001, CAM-269/167, 2026-07-11)**: `runPlanPhaseWithReplan` (`src/supervisor/plan-runner.ts`) previously called `teardown()` explicitly on only two of its many terminal branches (`audit-approved`, `plan-escalated`), so every OTHER `PlanPhaseResult` kind returned as-is fell through with no teardown -- and every new non-audit kind added since (`planner-timeout`, `auditor-timeout`, `planner-failed`, the pre-spawn kinds) silently reopened the same gap, because "add teardown() to this one new branch too" is not drift-proof. Fix: wrap the ENTIRE function body (the round-loop plus every terminal branch) in `try { ... } finally { teardown(); }` and delete the inline `teardown()` calls from the individual branches -- the round-boundary `teardown()` call inside the `while` loop (a MID-function call, not an exit) is untouched and unrelated. This is the same shape as the `finishTerminal`-always-tears-down invariant (patterns.md, CAM-188) applied to a second module: any pure driver function with N terminal return paths and one required side-effect on every exit should use ONE `finally` at the function's outer boundary, never N per-branch calls. Regression-locking a fix like this means updating every existing test that asserted "teardown NOT called" for a non-audit kind (`plan-runner-replan.test.ts`, `plan-runner-target-invalid.test.ts`) to now assert "teardown called exactly once" instead -- a stale "not called" assertion is the test-side mirror of the same missing-arm bug and must flip, not just gain new cases alongside it.

- **A durable marker's remove-side lifecycle can live in the SAME guard function that writes it, when both are boot-time-only (US-001, CAM-207, 2026-07-11)**: unlike `plan-escalation.ts`/`implement-blocked-marker.ts` (write in one module at one seam, remove in a distant caller at a different seam -- rearm/converge), the new `sidecar-stalled.ts` marker's only writer this story (`runContainerEnsureGuard`'s `FirewallError` arm, `src/commands/sidecar.ts`) and its remove-on-healthy-boot lifecycle are the SAME function: every early-return path of `runContainerEnsureGuard` (host-mode no-op, container-mode ensure success) calls `removeSidecarStalledMarker` before returning, and only the `FirewallError` catch arm writes it. This is safe specifically because the guard runs once at boot before any dispatch decision is made -- there is no risk of a remove racing a concurrent writer, unlike the plan/implement markers which live across multiple sidecar ticks. Don't default to the write-here/remove-there split just because the two precedents used it; check whether the read+write+remove all happen inside one synchronous boot-time function first, and collapse to that simpler shape when they do. Also: a shared marker file/shape with a `reason` discriminator for two independent producers (this story's `firewall-init-failed`, a future story's `sidecar-died`) is the right call over two marker files -- the orchestrator boot-read surfaces one file, one opening-blocker line, regardless of which producer wrote it.

- **A second producer for a shared marker file is a pure bounded-retry-then-escalate state machine, not a poll-and-signal watcher (US-002, CAM-207, 2026-07-11)**: `sidecar-liveness-watch.ts` mirrors `orch-recycle-watch.ts`'s injectable-seams/while(true)/`Bun.sleepSync` shape but is structurally different where it matters: `orch-recycle-watch` is stateless per tick (consume-once marker, no counters), while the liveness watcher needs mutable state (`attempts`/`exhausted`) carried BETWEEN ticks to implement bounded respawn + escalate-once + auto-recovery-reset. The cleanest split is a pure `handleOneTick(deps, state): number` (returns the next sleep-ms, mutates `state` in place) wrapped by a trivial `while (true) { sleepFn(handleOneTick(deps, state)) }` driver: this makes every branch (alive-resets, dead-under-bound-respawns, dead-at-bound-escalates-once, dead-past-bound-is-inert) directly unit-testable without the `sleepFn`-throws-to-stop-the-loop harness gymnastics `orch-recycle-watch.test.ts` needs for its integration-style tests (both styles are still useful: keep a thin integration describe block too, using the same sentinel-throw-in-sleepFn trick, to prove the outer wiring). The escalate-once + reset-on-recovery latch (`state.exhausted`) is what prevents a hot-loop on a deterministically-dying sidecar while still self-healing after a manual/external fix, without any special-case "was it manually restarted" detection: the very next `aliveFn() === true` tick is indistinguishable from a fresh boot and unconditionally resets both counters.

- **A config-gated background-process spawn (container-only) is unit-tested via a pure extracted predicate, not by threading fake config paths through the parent command's full option bag (US-002, CAM-207, 2026-07-11)**: `run.ts`'s existing config reads (`readMetaLoop`, `readPhaseModel`, `readBackend`) are all called with NO `configPath` override inside `runRun` itself: they always resolve against the real launch-time `process.cwd()`, never the `cwd` `RunOptions` field tests pass in (that field only targets the tmux/session-creation side effects). `shouldSpawnSidecarLivenessWatch(configPath?)` follows this exact precedent (mirrors `buildOrchestratorBootPrompt(configPath?)`, already tested the same way in `test/run.test.ts`'s `writeMetaLoopToml` helper): a thin exported pure wrapper over `readWorkerIsolation(configPath)`, unit-tested directly with a tmp-dir `scripts/cam/project.toml` fixture, while `runRun`'s own call site stays parameterless. This is why this repo being dogfooded in host mode (`scripts/cam/project.toml`'s `worker_isolation = "host"`) is NOT a test gap for AC6 ("validated by tests, not a live run"): the gate function has its own isolated container/host/absent/malformed unit tests, and `runRun`'s integration test only needs to assert the no-op holds true in THIS repo's real (host-mode) config, which every existing `runRun` test already implicitly exercises.

- **Adding one gate check to 3 sibling thin-proxies (plan/next/ship) trips `noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity` on the one whose function was already closest to the ceiling (US-003, CAM-207, 2026-07-11)**: the `sidecarLivenessGate(claudeDir, cmdName, sidecarAliveFn?)` refusal (shared in a new tiny `src/commands/sidecar-gate.ts`, defaulting `sidecarAliveFn` to `sidecarAlive` from `sidecar-pid.ts`) was inlined identically into `runPlan`/`runNext`/`runShip`'s existing liveness/mutex preamble (right after the `paneCountMutex` busy-check, before the phase/active write), but only `runPlan` broke Biome's complexity budget (17, then 16 after the shared-helper extraction, still over 15) -- `runNext`/`runShip` had headroom, `runPlan` didn't. Fix was a SECOND extraction specific to `runPlan`: pull the F-01 issue-resolution + state-file write (the `try/catch` + ternary that were already there pre-story) into `resolveAndWritePlanningSignal(cwd, claudeDir, options)`, returning `string | null` instead of threading `resolved`/try-catch inline. General lesson: when a cross-cutting gate lands on 3 near-identical call sites, expect exactly one of them to need a second, call-site-specific extraction beyond the shared helper -- don't assume uniform headroom just because the diff is textually identical across files.

- **`bun run lint` runs the Biome spine standalone and is the fast pre-`check:all` iteration loop for complexity/line-count fixes (US-003, CAM-207, 2026-07-11)**: iterating `bun run lint` (single-digit seconds) instead of the full `bun run check:all` (includes the ~40s `bun test` + coverage run) while fixing a `noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity`/`noExcessiveLinesPerFunction` finding is much faster feedback; only re-run the full spine once lint is clean, since file-size-budget ceilings shift again after any extraction that changes line counts (re-measure with `content.split('\n').length`, not `wc -l`, before writing the `_ref`).

- **Finding the actual process bound to a port inside the slim worker image, without lsof/fuser/ss (US-004, CAM-207, 2026-07-11)**: `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`'s `apt-get install` list does NOT include `psmisc` (fuser), `net-tools` (netstat), or `iproute2` (ss) — only the exact tools each prior story needed (`iptables`, `ipset`, `dnsmasq`, `dnsutils`, `curl`, `jq`). To reap a stale/foreign process actually holding port 53 (not just whatever pid `/var/run/dnsmasq-cam.pid` records — that pid can be stale, wrong, or absent while a different process still holds the port), `init-firewall.sh` parses `/proc/net/tcp` + `/proc/net/udp` directly (`local_address` field is `IP:PORT` in hex, port 53 = `0035`; the 10th whitespace-separated field is the socket inode), then globs `/proc/[0-9]*/fd/*` and `readlink`s each entry looking for a `socket:[<inode>]` target to recover the owning pid. This is pure bash + coreutils (`readlink`, parameter expansion) — no new apt package needed. Root-cause lesson: before adding a apt/OS package to reap/inspect processes in this image, check whether `/proc` parsing already covers it; the image is intentionally minimal.

- **`commitSubjectMatchesStory`'s completion convention now accepts any conventional-commit type, not just `feat:` (US-001, CAM-194, 2026-07-11)**: the story-id gate in `src/supervisor/result.ts` was hardcoded to `^feat:`, which falsely reported `no-commit` for a `passes:true` story whose commit legitimately used a different type (e.g. a Step 5.5 follow-up `fix: [Story ID] - correct <issue>`). Fixed by swapping only the type-prefix segment for the open `^[a-z]+(?:\([^)]*\))?!?:` idiom already used by the release-side matchers (`src/release/bump.ts`'s `BREAKING_BANG_RE`, `src/release/changelog.ts`'s `STRIP_PREFIX_RE`) — same regex idiom, but intentionally NOT extracted into a shared const across the supervisor/release boundary (per this story's notes: the release-side sibling matchers are a distinct concern and out of scope). The bracketed-or-bare id alternation and the immediately-following separator requirement (id must be directly followed by the hyphen or closing bracket) are what make the prefix-collision guard hold regardless of type; a subject that merely mentions the id after other words (e.g. `chore: mention US-001 in the README`) still fails to match because the id isn't immediately after the prefix.

- **Auditor/planner spawn payloads should carry the resolved identity record, never a bare id for the LLM to re-resolve (US-001, CAM-273, ADR-0027, 2026-07-11)**: `runPlanWorkerSequence` in `src/supervisor/plan-runner.ts` now defaults `auditorTaskPrompt` to `buildAuditorTaskPrompt(issue, branchName)`, which embeds the already-selected `IssueEntry`'s id/title/description/`spec.acceptanceCriteria` plus a code-derived `branchName` verbatim into the spawn string, replacing the old static `DEFAULT_AUDITOR_TASK_PROMPT`. The branch name is computed via a new `deriveBranchNameFromIssueId(issueId)` helper that parses the numeric suffix off `issue.id` (mirrors the private `numericSuffix` in `src/commands/plan.ts`, kept local rather than cross-imported for a one-line parse) and reuses `deriveBranchName` (ADR-0016) — never a planner-authored slug. Root cause this closes: CAM-156 escalated a false-critical BLOCK because the auditor took a bare issue number and resolved it against GitHub, coincidentally matching an unrelated PR #156. Because `runPlanPhase` re-resolves `issue = selectIssueFn()` at the top of every call, the re-plan rounds in `runPlanPhaseWithReplan` automatically get a fresh record-bearing prompt each round with zero additional wiring, as long as the production caller never sets `opts.auditorTaskPrompt` itself (the override still wins, for back-compat).

- **Auditor's own instructions must consume the embedded record, never re-derive identity (US-002, CAM-273, ADR-0027, 2026-07-11)**: `subagent-auditor.md` (both `templates/` source-of-truth and the `.claude/` runtime mirror, per the self-hosting pattern above) now instructs the auditor to trust the Issue ID/Title/Description/Branch/Acceptance-Criteria block the spawn prompt embeds (US-001's `buildAuditorTaskPrompt`) rather than re-resolving `issueNumber`/`branchName` via `gh`. Two new checklist items were added to section E (Repo invariants, now items 17-18, with the project-specific F section renumbered up by 2 in both files): (a) branch-collision must be judged only against real git refs (`git rev-parse --verify refs/heads/<branch>` + `git ls-remote origin <branch>`), never a same-numbered GitHub PR, and a nonexistent branch is never a collision; (b) prior-art/duplication signals must come from `git log` (backend-agnostic) and are always `severity: "suggestion"` (non-blocking), never escalated to `critical`/`important`. This closes the same CAM-156 identity-collision class of bug that US-001 fixed on the plan-runner side, applied to the auditor's own audit checklist.

- **`ensurePushed` rewritten compare-first via `git ls-remote` before any `git push` (US-001, CAM-156, 2026-07-11)**: `src/supervisor/host.ts`'s `ensurePushed` closure previously ran `git push origin <branch>` unconditionally, then compared local `HEAD` to the CACHED `origin/<branch>` tracking ref — when the worker had already pushed as its own final step, this could send a stale compare-and-swap old-oid and produce a false `ok:false` (a real cycle-halting BLOCKED per `loop.ts`, not advisory). Fixed by extracting a pure, injectable-`GitSpawnFn` helper `resolveEnsurePushed(spawnFn, cwd)`: read local `HEAD` first, then `git ls-remote origin <branch>` (read-only, no fetch, no local ref mutation) — if the remote sha already equals local `HEAD`, return `{ ok:true, pushed:false }` with **zero** `git push` calls; otherwise (remote behind/missing the branch, or `ls-remote` itself failed) fall through to the original push-then-compare path (`pushThenCompare`), preserving the exact old behavior as the fallback. Same `SpawnFn`-injection idiom as `src/release/post-merge.ts`'s `checkAndPruneRemoteBranch` (git args always `['-C', cwd, ...]`, no `cwd` option on the spawn call) — mirror that idiom for any future read-then-mutate git decision instead of inlining `spawnSync` with a `cwd` option. Return contract `{ ok, pushed, sha, detail }` is unchanged by construction, so `loop.ts`'s consumer and the `'pushed'` event stay untouched.

- **Pinning a CLI-only tool as an exact devDependency trips knip's unused-dependency check; `ignoreDependencies` is the fix (US-001, CAM-199, 2026-07-11)**: adding `jscpd` to `package.json` devDependencies (to de-tokenize the `@5` version pin off the `bunx jscpd` gate command in `scripts/check-all.ts`) makes knip flag it as an "Unused devDependency", because knip only detects usage via static imports/requires, not via a shell-string CLI invocation inside a `.ts` file. `knip` itself is exempt from this check (knip special-cases its own package name), but any other CLI-only devDependency invoked purely through `bunx <name>` in a script string needs an explicit `ignoreDependencies: ["<name>"]` entry in `knip.json`, or the `dead-code` gate goes red the moment the tool is added as a real devDependency. `bunx` resolves the locally installed exact-pinned version first (confirmed against Bun's official docs), so once pinned, no inline `@version` token is needed on the gate command string at all.

- **Signal-then-deterministic-fallback needs exactly one mutable per-episode timestamp, not a full state enum (US-002, CAM-172, 2026-07-12)**: `checkBackstop`'s "ask the agent first, fall back to a deterministic write after a grace period" state machine is driven entirely by one nullable field, `BackstopEpisodeState.signaledAt` (allocated ONCE per watcher lifetime by the caller, mutated in place across polls, never per-tick). Occupancy-resolved-or-handoff-present clears it to `null` (episode over); first over-threshold tick with `signaledAt === null` fires `signalPaneFn()` once and stamps `nowFn()`; subsequent ticks within `signalTimeoutMs` no-op; the tick that finally exceeds the timeout calls the deterministic `writeMinimalHandoffFn()` (here, `writeOrchHandoff` from `src/orchestrator/handoff.ts` — its first PRODUCTION caller; previously only test-invoked) and clears the episode. This shape (nullable timestamp + injectable `nowFn`) generalizes to any future "ask-first, deterministic-fallback-after-N-seconds" producer without needing a distinct enum type, and keeps every branch unit-testable with a fake advancing clock instead of `sleepFn`-throws-to-stop-the-loop gymnastics. The composed no-arg `signalPaneFn` (pane-id resolution + send) is deliberately best-effort: a not-found pane silently no-ops the signal, because the 30s deterministic fallback already covers that failure mode unconditionally — no separate error path needed.

- **A second marker-arming producer must mirror an existing guard's SHAPE, not just its outcome (US-001, CAM-172, 2026-07-12)**: `checkBackstop` (`src/commands/orch-recycle-watch.ts`) previously armed `ORCH_RECYCLE_MARKER` whenever context occupancy crossed the backstop fraction, with no check that the orchestrator handoff file (`.claude/.cam-orch-handoff.json`, `ORCH_HANDOFF_FILENAME` from `src/orchestrator/handoff.ts`) already existed — unlike the sibling `--cycle-close` producer in `index.ts`'s `dispatchJournal` (~1558-1569), which refuses to arm the marker (exit 3) until `handoffExistsFn()` is true. Fixed by adding the same seam name and same guard order to `BackstopDeps`/`OrchRecycleWatchOptions`: `handoffExistsFn: () => boolean`, defaulting to `existsSync(join(claudeDir, ORCH_HANDOFF_FILENAME))`, checked strictly after the occupancy-over-threshold check and strictly before `armMarkerFn()`. Both `checkBackstop` and `BackstopDeps` had to be exported (they were previously module-private) so the unit test can drive each branch directly instead of only through the `runOrchRecycleWatch` integration harness. Any future second producer of a shared marker/signal file should grep for the FIRST producer's guard shape and mirror the seam name (`handoffExistsFn`, not `handoffPresent`/`hasHandoff`/etc.) so both producers share one mental model and one invariant description, rather than inventing an equivalent-but-differently-named guard. Gotcha: an existing integration-style test (`test/orch-recycle-watch.test.ts`, not the co-located `test/commands/orch-recycle-watch.test.ts`) asserted the pre-guard behavior (marker arms on over-threshold occupancy alone) without injecting `handoffExistsFn`; the fix silently defaults that test's `handoffExistsFn` to the real `existsSync` (false in the test's `cwd`), so the test started failing once the guard landed — always grep for every existing caller of a function whose default-guard behavior you are tightening, not just the file the story names.

- **Fail-safe integer-key config reader extends the enum-reader idiom to numeric values (US-003, CAM-172, 2026-07-12)**: `readOrchContextWindow` (`src/config/models.ts`) mirrors `readMetaLoop`'s try/catch/default shape but validates `typeof value === 'number' \&\& Number.isInteger(value) \&\& value > 0` instead of an exact-string match, since the config value here is a token-count integer (`[loop] orch_context_window`), not an enum tag. Every failure mode (missing file, missing `[loop]` section, missing key, malformed TOML, non-number, non-integer float, zero, negative) collapses to one fail-safe default (`DEFAULT_ORCH_CONTEXT_WINDOW = 200_000`), never throws. This retired `orchestratorContextWindow()`'s prior 1M-by-model-prefix derivation (`MODEL_CONTEXT_WINDOWS`/`OPUS_CONTEXT_WINDOW` in `src/orchestrator/context-window.ts`), which conflated "what context window does this model expose via the API" with "what window does the actual Claude Code flat-subscription session run under" -- the 1M window is API/Bedrock/Vertex credit-billed only; the subscription this orchestrator runs under is a flat 200k, so deriving the backstop threshold from the model's theoretical 1M ceiling meant the 0.80 backstop (800k) was never reachable in practice before a hard 400 overflow. Any future numeric `[loop]`-section config key should follow this same shape: reuse the section-read scaffold, swap only the value-validation predicate.

- **Central `--help`/`-h` guard in `index.ts` fires BEFORE the command switch, driven by one `HELP_REGISTRY: Record<string, string>` keyed by command name (US-001, CAM-211, 2026-07-12)**: `isHelpRequested(command, tail)` (exported from `index.ts`) is checked once, right after the `--version` branch and before `switch (command)`; a match looks up `HELP_REGISTRY[command]`, prints it, and returns 0 without ever entering the case body. This is why `cam sidecar --help` (and the other internal commands spawned by `cam run` — `orch-recycle-watch`, `sidecar-liveness-watch`, `orch-budget`, none of which had any `--help` guard before this story) now returns instantly instead of booting the long-lived daemon: the registry entry is the only thing a new internal command needs to be help-safe, not a bespoke per-case guard (which is exactly the footgun class this story closed — every future internal command must get a `HELP_REGISTRY` entry, full stop). `isHelpRequested` treats `--help`/`-h` as matching **anywhere** in `tail` for every command except `claude`, which matches **only when leading** (`tail[0]`) — this exactly mirrors `parseClaudeArgs`'s own leading-only forwarding contract (`src/commands/claude.ts`), so a non-leading `--help` meant for the child `claude` process still flows through untouched. The pre-existing per-command `if (parsed.help)` / `tail.includes('--help')` checks inside each switch case are now unreachable-for-help dead code (the guard already returned 0 before the switch runs) — deliberately left in place per Surgical Changes rather than stripped, since the story's diff is scoped to the guard + registry. New internal-command help constants can be terse `renderHelp` calls with only a `body` section (no `entries`), which sidesteps the CAM-217 24-char name-padding gotcha entirely since that bug only affects the `entries` column renderer.

- **A 3rd verbatim real-spawnSync closure trips jscpd; extract a shared factory instead of a 3rd copy-paste (US-001, CAM-206, 2026-07-12)**: `defaultCloseIssueFn`/`defaultAbandonIssueFn` (`src/release/post-merge.ts`) each inlined an identical `(cmd, args, opts) => spawnSync(cmd, args, {...})` closure; that 2-copy duplication stayed under the jscpd threshold, but adding a 3rd sibling verbatim (`defaultDemoteIssueFn`, for the new `demoteIssueOnMain` stage:'specified'->'idea' writer) was the point to stop copy-pasting. Fix: extract `makeRealIssueSpawnFn(): CloseSpawnFn` once and have all three `default<Verb>IssueFn` wrappers call it, so the real-spawnSync closure lives in exactly one place regardless of how many on-main-writer siblings accumulate. Guard-reason design for a 3rd stage-transition verb: never collapse multiple "wrong stage" cases into one blanket `reason: 'wrong-stage'` token — `demoteIssueOnMain` returns a DISTINCT reason per source stage (`already-idea`, `is-planned`, `is-shipped`) so a caller (and a human reading a failed-guard log) can tell "nothing to do" apart from "would strand an in-flight PRD" apart from "already shipped", none of which should be silently conflated even though all three refuse the same mutation.

- **A 3rd sibling axis-mutation subcommand is a pure copy-paste of the CAM-210 shape across the same four sites, and the CAM-211 `--help` guard needs zero new code for it (US-002, CAM-206/CAM-210, 2026-07-12)**: wiring `cam issue demote <id>` (positional, mirroring `close`/`abandon` 1:1 per the CAM-210 bullet above) touched exactly the four documented sites in `index.ts` (`ParsedIssueArgs` union, `parseIssueArgs` positional branch ahead of the free-text fallthrough, `IssueDispatchDeps.demoteIssueOnMainFn` + its `dispatchIssue` branch, `ISSUE_HELP` usage/entries) plus the existing `defaultDemoteIssueFn`/`demoteIssueOnMain` primitives already landed in US-001 -- no new plumbing class was needed. Separately: because the CAM-211 central `--help`/`-h` guard (see bullet above) matches on the top-level `command` token (`'issue'`) and scans `tail` for `--help`/`-h` **anywhere**, `cam issue demote --help` was ALREADY correctly short-circuited to `ISSUE_HELP` before this story touched any code -- `main()` never even reaches `parseIssueArgs`/`dispatchIssue` for it. The story's AC4 was satisfied by adding a regression-lock test (`main(['bun','index.ts','issue','demote','--help'])` asserts exit 0 + no `CAM_ISSUE_RESULT` in stdout), not by writing a new guard. General rule: when a new positional subcommand is added under an ALREADY-`HELP_REGISTRY`-covered top-level command, its own `--help` handling is free -- only add a locking test, never a bespoke per-subcommand guard.

- **Two independently-maintained hardcoded model-id lists (`DEFAULTS` in `src/config/models.ts`, `MODEL_OPTIONS` in `src/ui/ConfigScreen.tsx`) drift silently unless a test cross-checks them, and a THIRD site (`scripts/cam/project.toml` itself) can independently diverge from both (US-001, CAM-286, 2026-07-12)**: fixed the concrete drift (project.toml's `[models] implementer = "claude-sonnet-5"` vs. `DEFAULTS.implementer`/`MODEL_OPTIONS` both still saying `claude-sonnet-4-6` and omitting `claude-sonnet-5` entirely) by (1) updating `DEFAULTS.implementer` to `claude-sonnet-5` + its docstring (which had drifted from "implementer/ship share one line" to needing two separate bullets since they no longer share a default), (2) adding a `claude-sonnet-5` entry to `MODEL_OPTIONS` and `export`-ing the const (it was module-private) so a test can import it without duplicating the literal list, (3) a `DEFAULTS vs MODEL_OPTIONS consistency` test asserting every non-`'backend'` `DEFAULTS` value is a member of `MODEL_OPTIONS`'s value set, and (4) a `readPhaseModel vs repo project.toml` test that reads the REAL `scripts/cam/project.toml` via `loadConfig` (not a tmp fixture) for every `Phase`, computes the expected value as `configured ?? DEFAULTS[phase]`, and asserts `readPhaseModel(phase, repoConfigPath)` matches AND is a member of `MODEL_OPTIONS ∪ {DEFAULTS[phase]}` — this is the guard that would have caught the original drift, since it fails the moment someone changes `project.toml`'s `[models]` section without keeping the picker/fallback in sync. Gotcha: fixing the two lists alone is not enough — grep for every OTHER hardcoded assertion of the old default value before running the full suite. `test/supervisor/worker-argv-model.test.ts` (a file this story's `notes` field never named) had an unrelated literal `expect(DEFAULTS.implementer).toBe('claude-sonnet-4-6')` that correctly went red the moment `DEFAULTS.implementer` changed; always `grep -rn` the exact old literal across `test/` before declaring a default-value change complete, not just the files the story's `notes` field names.
